### `Roth/HasseDeriv.lean`

#### `def MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`

### `def MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} → (d k : σ →₀ ℕ) → ℕ`
- **What**: The multi-index binomial coefficient `∏_{j ∈ supp d} (k j).choose (d j)`, i.e. `binom(k, d)`; the scalar by which the Hasse derivative `Δ_d` multiplies the monomial `X^k`.
- **How**: One-line definition `d.prod fun j ↦ (k j).choose` (`Finsupp.prod` over the support of `d`; `Nat.choose`).
- **Hypotheses**: none (no typeclasses; `R` from the section `variable` is not included).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `hasseDeriv`, `hasseDeriv_monomial`, `hasseDeriv_apply`, `hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff`, `coeff_hasseDeriv`, `hasseCoeff_eq_prod`, `hasseDeriv_zero_apply`, `hasseDeriv_C`, `hasseDeriv_rename`, `hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff` (private), `hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv`, `hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint`, `hasseCoeff_mul_succ` (private), `pderiv_hasseDeriv`, `hasseDeriv_monomial_mul`, `hasseDeriv_single_aeval_X`, `uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv`, `hasseCoeff_le_two_pow`; Roth/CoeffNorm.lean: `abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le` (l.139); Roth/Taylor.lean: `hasseCoeff_single_add_eq` (private, l.82-85), `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (private, l.113-117, via lemmas)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 49–50, proof length 1 line (definition body)
- **Notes**: Docstring says "a product over the support of `d`"; `hasseCoeff_eq_prod` gives the `Fintype` full-product form. Argument order `(d k)` is the reverse of `Nat.choose`'s `(n k)` convention (top index second).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff.md)_

#### `def MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`

### `def MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`
- **Type**: `{σ R : Type*} → [CommSemiring R] → (d : σ →₀ ℕ) → MvPolynomial σ R →ₗ[R] MvPolynomial σ R`
- **What**: The multivariate Hasse derivative `Δ_d`, the `R`-linear map with `Δ_d (a X^k) = binom(k, d) a X^(k - d)` (zero unless `d ≤ k`); over a `ℚ`-algebra it is `(∏ j (d j)!)⁻¹ ∏ j ∂_j^(d j)`.
- **How**: `Finsupp.lsum R (fun k ↦ (monomial (k - d)).comp (LinearMap.lsmul R R (hasseCoeff d k : R)))` precomposed with `AddMonoidAlgebra.coeffLinearEquiv R`, i.e. extend the monomial rule linearly; key API lemma `hasseDeriv_monomial` is proved by `simp [hasseDeriv]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); no other hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean (all lemmas from `hasseDeriv_monomial` to `factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single`, ~60 occurrences); Roth/CoeffNorm.lean (`abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le`, `normOne_hasseDeriv_le`, generalised-Wronskian degree/coefficient bounds l.247-265); Roth/GenWronskian.lean (`genWronskian` l.34, `kronHasse` l.146, l.241-245); Roth/Index.lean (l.63, l.70, taylor/index lemmas); Roth/Taylor.lean (l.103-105, coefficient of `taylor` as `eval α (hasseDeriv d P)`); Roth/Separation.lean (l.147-148); Roth/Theta.lean (l.378-383); Roth/MainLemma.lean (27 occurrences, l.362-745); Roth/Main.lean (l.385, l.392). Roth/Wronskian.lean uses the univariate `Polynomial.hasseDeriv`, not this one.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 57–60, proof length 3 lines (definition body)
- **Notes**: `noncomputable`. Argument order of the coefficient is `hasseCoeff d k` (lower index first), see the parent report `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff.md`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (d k : σ →₀ ℕ) (a : R), hasseDeriv d (monomial k a) = monomial (k - d) ((hasseCoeff d k : R) * a)`
- **What**: The Hasse derivative `Δ_d` sends `a X^k` to `binom(k, d) a X^(k - d)`, where `binom(k, d) = ∏ j (k j).choose (d j)`; the defining equation of `hasseDeriv` on monomials. Tagged `@[simp]`.
- **How**: `simp [hasseDeriv]` unfolds the `Finsupp.lsum` / `AddMonoidAlgebra.coeffLinearEquiv` composition and evaluates it on a single monomial (`Finsupp.lsum_single`-style simp).
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:94), `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_C` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:127), private `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial_mul` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:233), private `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (Roth/Taylor.lean:111, 116); also available as a simp lemma project-wide.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 66–68, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Name matches mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial` (`hasseDeriv k (monomial n r) = monomial (n - k) (↑(n.choose k) * r)`), so the multivariate statement is uniform with it.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_apply`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_apply`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), hasseDeriv d P = ∑ k ∈ P.support, monomial (k - d) ((hasseCoeff d k : R) * P.coeff k)`
- **What**: Expands the Hasse derivative `Δ_d P` as the sum, over the monomials `a_k X^k` of `P`, of `binom(k, d) a_k X^(k - d)`.
- **How**: `simp [hasseDeriv, sum_def]`: unfolds the `Finsupp.lsum ∘ₗ AddMonoidAlgebra.coeffLinearEquiv` definition and rewrites the resulting `Finsupp.sum` with `MvPolynomial.sum_def`. The statement is in fact definitional (closes by `rfl`, see Evidence).
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: `MvPolynomial.normOne_hasseDeriv_le` (Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:151); no other occurrence in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 73–75, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Name and shape mirror mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_apply` (`hasseDeriv k f = f.sum fun i r => monomial (i - k) (↑(i.choose k) * r)`, Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/HasseDeriv.lean:62). The project states it with an explicit `Finset` sum over `P.support` rather than the `Finsupp.sum` form; both are `rfl`-equal.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_apply.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_apply.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} (d k : σ →₀ ℕ), hasseCoeff d k = 0 ↔ ¬d ≤ k`
- **What**: The multi-index binomial coefficient `binom(k, d) = ∏_{j ∈ supp d} (k j).choose (d j)` vanishes exactly when `d ≰ k` (componentwise order).
- **How**: One `simp` call unfolding `hasseCoeff` and `Finsupp.prod`, then `Finset.prod_eq_zero_iff`, `Nat.choose_eq_zero_iff`, `Finsupp.le_iff`, `not_forall`.
- **Hypotheses**: none (the section `[CommSemiring R]` is not included in the statement).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `coeff_hasseDeriv` (l.99), `hasseDeriv_C` (l.127); Roth/Taylor.lean: `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (private, l.113)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 80–82, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Not `@[simp]`. Stated in the negative form `= 0 ↔ ¬ …`; the positive form `≠ 0 ↔ d ≤ k` (mirroring `Nat.choose_ne_zero_iff`) is a better simp normal form (see Phase 5.5).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (d e : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), coeff e (hasseDeriv d P) = ↑(hasseCoeff d (e + d)) * coeff (e + d) P`
- **What**: The coefficient of `X^e` in `Δ_d P` is `binom(e + d, d)` times the coefficient of `X^(e+d)` in `P`; in particular distinct surviving monomials do not merge under `Δ_d`.
- **How**: Induction on `P` via `MvPolynomial.induction_on'`. Monomial case: `hasseDeriv_monomial`, `coeff_monomial` twice, then a case split on `k = e + d` and on `d ≤ k`, closed by `simp` using `tsub_eq_iff_eq_add_of_le` (when `d ≤ k`) or `hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff` (when `d ≰ k`). Additive case: `simp [hp, hq, mul_add]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); no other hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `hasseDeriv_zero_apply` (l.114), `hasseDeriv_map` (l.137), `hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv` (l.178, three rewrites), `pderiv_hasseDeriv` (l.203, two rewrites), `degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le` (l.213), `hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt` (l.222), `uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv` (l.270); Roth/CoeffNorm.lean: `abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le` (l.137), which is in turn used in Roth/MainLemma.lean l.468, l.509.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 89–101, proof length 11 lines
- **Notes**: Not `@[simp]`. Name follows mathlib's multivariate convention (`MvPolynomial.coeff_pderiv`, `MvPowerSeries.coeff_pderiv`), whereas the univariate analogue is `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_coeff`. Source paper explicitly asks for this identity (clean.tex l.147-151).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_prod`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_prod`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (d k : σ →₀ ℕ), hasseCoeff d k = ∏ j, (k j).choose (d j)`
- **What**: Over a finite index type, the multi-index binomial `hasseCoeff d k` (defined as a product over `d.support`) equals the full product `∏_j binom(k_j, d_j)` over all variables.
- **How**: Term proof `d.prod_fintype _ fun _ ↦ Nat.choose_zero_right _`: mathlib's `Finsupp.prod_fintype` applied with the side condition `binom(n, 0) = 1` (`Nat.choose_zero_right`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (finite set of variables); the section `[CommSemiring R]` is not included.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `hasseCoeff_le_two_pow` (l.278). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 106–107, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Not a glue lemma (needs `Finsupp.prod_fintype` plus `Nat.choose_zero_right`, not `rfl`), so the full workflow was run. `[Fintype σ]` is stronger than needed: any finset `s ⊇ d.support` suffices (evidence file).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_prod.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_prod.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero_apply`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero_apply`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (hasseDeriv 0) P = P`
- **What**: The Hasse derivative of order zero fixes every polynomial: `Δ_0 P = P`.
- **How**: `ext e` then `simp [coeff_hasseDeriv, hasseCoeff]`: the coefficient formula gives `[X^e] Δ_0 P = hasseCoeff 0 (e + 0) * [X^(e+0)] P`, and `hasseCoeff 0 k` is the empty `Finsupp.prod` over the support of `0`, i.e. `1`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); no other hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `hasseDeriv_zero` (l.121, `LinearMap.ext hasseDeriv_zero_apply`); Roth/Index.lean: `index_eq_zero_iff` (l.104 and l.105, as a `simpa` argument).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 112–114 (docstring 109–111), proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Not `@[simp]`, but the bundled `hasseDeriv_zero` is `@[simp]`, so `simp` already proves `hasseDeriv 0 P = P` via `LinearMap.id_apply` (evidence file); the `simpa [hasseDeriv_zero_apply]` arguments in Roth/Index.lean are therefore probably redundant (not checked in Roth/ since the file must not be edited). The mathlib univariate analogue is named `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_zero'`; current mathlib naming prefers the `_apply` suffix for pointwise forms of bundled-map equalities, so `hasseDeriv_zero_apply` is the better name.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero_apply.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero_apply.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R], hasseDeriv (0 : σ →₀ ℕ) = (LinearMap.id : MvPolynomial σ R →ₗ[R] MvPolynomial σ R)`
- **What**: The Hasse derivative of order zero is the identity linear map: `Δ_0 = id`.
- **How**: `LinearMap.ext hasseDeriv_zero_apply` (bundles the pointwise lemma `Δ_0 P = P`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero_apply`
- **Used by**: no explicit call site in `Roth/` (`grep -rn hasseDeriv_zero Roth/` finds only the declaration and the docstring of `hasseDeriv_zero_apply`); it is `@[simp]`, so it may fire implicitly in `simp` calls on `hasseDeriv 0 _` (e.g. the `simpa` calls in Roth/Index.lean:104-105, `index_eq_zero_iff`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 120–121 (docstring 116–118, `@[simp]` 119), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Name, statement shape and proof are identical to mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_zero` (Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/HasseDeriv.lean:86-87, `LinearMap.ext <| hasseDeriv_zero'`) and parallel to `LaurentSeries.hasseDeriv_zero`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_C`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_C`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {d : σ →₀ ℕ}, d ≠ 0 → ∀ (a : R), (hasseDeriv d) (C a) = 0`
- **What**: A multivariate Hasse derivative of nonzero order annihilates constants: `Δ_d (C a) = 0` for `d ≠ 0`.
- **How**: `rw [C_apply, hasseDeriv_monomial, (hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff d 0).2 _]` then `simp`: `C a = monomial 0 a`, so `Δ_d (C a) = monomial (0 - d) (hasseCoeff d 0 * a)`, and `hasseCoeff d 0 = 0` because `d ≰ 0` (from `d ≠ 0`, discharged by `simpa using hd`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hd : d ≠ 0` (necessary: `Δ_0 = id`); `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff` (and implicitly `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:127 (`coeff_taylor_monomial_aux`, zero case of the `Finsupp.induction`: `simp [← C_apply, hasseDeriv_C hd, hd.symm]`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 126–128 (docstring 123–125), proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Mathlib's univariate analogue is `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_C (k : ℕ) (r : R) (hk : 0 < k)` (explicit order, hypothesis last); the project form takes `d` implicit and the hypothesis first, which is fine since `d` is inferable from `hd`. `d ≠ 0` (not `0 < d`) is the idiomatic spelling for `σ →₀ ℕ`. Mathlib also has the companion `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_apply_one`; the project has no `hasseDeriv_one`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_C.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_C.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {S : Type*} [CommSemiring S] (f : R →+* S) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (hasseDeriv d) ((map f) P) = (map f) ((hasseDeriv d) P)`
- **What**: The multivariate Hasse derivative commutes with changing coefficients along a ring homomorphism: `Δ_d (f_* P) = f_* (Δ_d P)`; used to pass between `ℤ`, `ℚ` and `ℝ` coefficients.
- **How**: `ext e` then `simp [coeff_hasseDeriv, coeff_map]`: both coefficients equal `f (hasseCoeff d (e + d) * P.coeff (e + d))`, using that `f` fixes natural-number casts (`map_natCast`, `map_mul` via simp).
- **Hypotheses**: `f : R →+* S` between commutative semirings (forced by `MvPolynomial.map`); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv` (and implicitly `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`)
- **Used by**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:409 (`eval_hasseDeriv_sub_eq_zero`), Roth/MainLemma.lean:489, 492, 495 (`exists_hasseDeriv_index_eval_bound_aux`), Roth/Theta.lean:524 (`mapped_det_ne_zero_aux`), Roth/Theta.lean:580 (`index_le_lem6`), Roth/Separation.lean:533 (`exists_rat_factors_det_hasseDeriv_aux`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 134–137 (docstring 130–133), proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Not tagged `@[simp]`, whereas mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_map` is `@[simp]`; every call site passes it to `simp`/`simpa`/`rw` explicitly. Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.pderiv_map` takes `φ`, `f`, `i` implicit; the project (like `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_map`) takes them explicit.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_rename`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_rename`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {τ : Type*} {f : σ → τ}, Function.Injective f → ∀ (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (hasseDeriv (Finsupp.mapDomain f d)) ((rename f) P) = (rename f) ((hasseDeriv d) P)`
- **What**: For an injective relabelling `f : σ → τ` of variables, the Hasse derivative of order `f_* d` of the renamed polynomial is the renaming of `Δ_d P`: `Δ_{f_* d} ∘ rename f = rename f ∘ Δ_d`. In particular an operator in a subset of the variables acts on a polynomial that only involves those variables as it did before the embedding.
- **How**: `induction P using MvPolynomial.induction_on'`; the monomial case is `simp` with `rename_monomial`, `hasseCoeff`, `Finsupp.prod_mapDomain_index_inj hf` (the binomial product is unchanged under injective reindexing), `Finsupp.mapDomain_apply_of_injective hf` and `← Finsupp.mapDomain_tsub hf` (`f_*(k - d) = f_* k - f_* d`); the additive case is `simp [hp, hq]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hf : Function.Injective f` (necessary; see evidence item 3); `[CommSemiring R]`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial` (via `simp`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Separation.lean:165 (`hasseDeriv_rename_mul_aeval`, with `f = some : τ → Option τ`), which in turn is used at Roth/Separation.lean:365.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 143–152 (docstring 139–142), proof length 7 lines
- **Notes**: Docstring cites "§4, Lean implementation note after Lemma 4-2"; the paper's corresponding remark is that the operators `Δ_μ` use only the variables `x_1, …, x_{p-1}` (clean.tex l.408, 463). Statement shape is identical to mathlib's `MvPolynomial.pderiv_rename` (same `hf` argument, same orientation).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_rename.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_rename.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} (f d e : σ →₀ ℕ), hasseCoeff d (f + d) * hasseCoeff e (f + d + e) = hasseCoeff d (d + e) * hasseCoeff (d + e) (f + (d + e))`
- **What**: Multi-index "subset-of-a-subset" binomial identity: for multi-indices f, d, e, binom(f+d, d) · binom(f+d+e, e) = binom(d+e, d) · binom(f+d+e, d+e), with binom(k, d) = ∏_j binom(k_j, d_j). It is the coefficient identity behind the Hasse-derivative composition rule Δ_d Δ_e = binom(d+e, d) Δ_{d+e}.
- **How**: Unfolds `hasseCoeff` to `Finsupp.prod`, rewrites all four support-indexed products as products over the common finset `d.support ∪ e.support` via `Finsupp.prod_of_support_subset` (side condition `Nat.choose_zero_right`, support inclusions `Finset.subset_union_left/right`, `Finsupp.support_add`), merges them with `Finset.prod_mul_distrib`, and closes each factor by `Finset.prod_congr` with the scalar identity `Nat.choose_mul` (n := f i + d i + e i, k := d i + e i, s := e i) plus `Nat.choose_symm_add` and `mul_comm`/`add_assoc` reshuffling.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the types (`σ` arbitrary; section `[CommSemiring R]` is not included since R does not occur).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv` (l.179, `rw`), `hasseCoeff_mul_succ` (l.195, private, `simpa using hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff e (Finsupp.single j 1) d`). No other use in `Roth/` (grep over `Roth/` and `Roth.lean`).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 153–168, proof length 13 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The statement shape is tuned to the `rw` chain in `hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv` (f + d, f + d + e, then `add_assoc`), not to the literature/mathlib `Nat.choose_mul` shape `choose n k * choose k s = choose n s * choose (n - s) (k - s)`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (d e : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), hasseDeriv d (hasseDeriv e P) = (↑(hasseCoeff d (d + e)) : R) • hasseDeriv (d + e) P`
- **What**: Composition rule for multivariate Hasse derivatives: `Δ_d Δ_e P = binom(d+e, d) · Δ_{d+e} P`, with the multi-index binomial `binom(d+e,d) = ∏_j binom(d_j+e_j, d_j)`.
- **How**: `ext f`, then rewrite the three coefficients with `coeff_hasseDeriv` (and `coeff_smul`, `smul_eq_mul`), regroup with `mul_assoc`/`Nat.cast_mul`, and close with the private multi-index binomial identity `hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff` (a product of `Nat.choose_mul`) plus `add_assoc`.
- **Hypotheses**: none (section variable `[CommSemiring R]`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff` (private)
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:113 (`index_sub_weight_le_index_hasseDeriv`, `rw [hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv, smul_eq_C_mul, ...] at hi`); Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:187 (`hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint`, which in turn is used at Roth/Separation.lean:156 in `hasseDeriv_rename_mul_aeval`). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 175–179, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Mathlib's univariate counterpart is `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_comp` (operator form, `ℕ`-smul); `LaurentSeries.hasseDeriv_comp` is pointwise with `ℕ`-smul. The project form is pointwise with an `R`-cast scalar.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {d e : σ →₀ ℕ}, Disjoint d.support e.support → ∀ (P : MvPolynomial σ R), hasseDeriv d (hasseDeriv e P) = hasseDeriv (d + e) P`
- **What**: If the multi-indices `d` and `e` involve disjoint sets of variables, then `Δ_d Δ_e = Δ_{d+e}` (the binomial factor of the composition law is `1`).
- **How**: `rw [hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv]`, then show `hasseCoeff d (d + e) = 1` by `Finset.prod_eq_one` over `d.support`, each factor being `(d i + 0).choose (d i) = 1` via `Finsupp.notMem_support_iff` and `Finset.disjoint_left`; finish with `Nat.cast_one`, `one_smul`.
- **Hypotheses**: `h : Disjoint d.support e.support`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/Separation.lean:156 (`hasseDeriv_rename_mul_aeval`, `rw [← hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint hdisj, ...]`). No other use in `Roth/` (also referenced in the docstring of `hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv`, Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:172).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 185–189, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Mathlib has no multivariate Hasse derivative; univariate disjointness is trivial (one index is 0), so there is no univariate analogue.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_hasseDeriv_of_disjoint.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_succ`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_succ`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} (j : σ) (d e : σ →₀ ℕ), hasseCoeff d (e + Finsupp.single j 1 + d) * (e j + 1) = (d j + 1) * hasseCoeff (d + Finsupp.single j 1) (e + Finsupp.single j 1 + d)`
- **What**: Multi-index absorption identity for the binomial coefficient binom(k, d) = ∏_i binom(k_i, d_i): with n = e + e_j + d (e_j the j-th unit multi-index), binom(n, d) · (e_j + 1) = (d_j + 1) · binom(n, d + e_j). Only the j-th factor changes; it is the scalar identity C(N, a)·(N - a) = (a+1)·C(N, a+1). It is the coefficient identity behind ∂_j Δ_d = (d_j + 1) Δ_{d + e_j}.
- **How**: One-line `simpa [hasseCoeff, add_comm, add_left_comm, add_assoc, mul_comm] using hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff e (Finsupp.single j 1) d`, i.e. instantiates the private multi-index `Nat.choose_mul` identity with (f, d, e) := (e, single j 1, d) and lets `simp` evaluate `hasseCoeff (single j 1) _` to `e j + 1` and `hasseCoeff (single j 1) (single j 1 + d)` to `d j + 1` (via `Finsupp.prod_single_index`, `Nat.choose_one_right`) and normalise the additions.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the types (σ arbitrary; R does not occur).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff` (private)
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `pderiv_hasseDeriv` (l.200, `rw` at l.205) only (grep over `Roth/` and `Roth.lean`). Transitively: `factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:286, uses `pderiv_hasseDeriv` at l.291) and `derivative_kronHasse_aux` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:153, uses `pderiv_hasseDeriv` at l.159).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 191–195, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The statement shape (`e + single j 1 + d`, factor on the right of LHS / left of RHS) is tailored to the `rw` chain in `pderiv_hasseDeriv` after `← add_assoc e d, add_right_comm e d, mul_right_comm, ← Nat.cast_add_one, ← Nat.cast_mul`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_succ.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_succ.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (j : σ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (pderiv j) (hasseDeriv d P) = (↑(d j + 1) : R) • hasseDeriv (d + Finsupp.single j 1) P`
- **What**: A partial derivative of a multivariate Hasse derivative: `∂_j Δ_d P = (d_j + 1) · Δ_{d + e_j} P`.
- **How**: `ext e`, rewrite with `coeff_pderiv`, `coeff_smul` and `coeff_hasseDeriv` (twice), reassociate the multi-indices (`add_assoc`, `add_right_comm`) and casts (`Nat.cast_add_one`, `Nat.cast_mul`), and close with the private absorption identity `hasseCoeff_mul_succ` (itself a specialisation of the private `hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff`, a product of `Nat.choose_mul`).
- **Hypotheses**: none (section variable `[CommSemiring R]`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_succ` (private; via it `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_mul_hasseCoeff`)
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean:159 (`derivative_kronHasse_aux`, in a `simp [..., pderiv_hasseDeriv, ...]` set); Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:291 (`factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single`, induction step `rw [..., pderiv_hasseDeriv, ...]`). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 200–205, proof length 4 lines
- **Notes**: Listed under `## Main results` of the module docstring. Mathlib's univariate API has no direct analogue (`derivative ∘ hasseDeriv k = (k+1) • hasseDeriv (k+1)` is not stated); there it follows from `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_one` + `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_comp`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (j : σ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), degreeOf j (hasseDeriv d P) ≤ degreeOf j P`
- **What**: A Hasse derivative `Δ_d` does not increase the degree of a polynomial in any variable `x_j`.
- **How**: `degreeOf_le_iff` reduces to monomials `m` in the support of `Δ_d P`; `coeff_hasseDeriv` rewrites the coefficient as `hasseCoeff d (m + d) * coeff (m + d) P`, so `m + d ∈ P.support` (`right_ne_zero_of_mul`); then `monomial_le_degreeOf j` gives `(m + d) j ≤ degreeOf j P`, and `m j ≤ (m + d) j` closes by `simp`.
- **Hypotheses**: none (section variable `[CommSemiring R]`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`
- **Used by**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:465, :467 (`abs_eval_iterated_hasseDeriv_lt_aux`), :491 (`exists_hasseDeriv_index_eval_bound_aux`), :504 (`abs_coeff_diag_hasseDeriv_modByMonic_le_aux`); Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:253 (`degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le`). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 210–214, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Mathlib's univariate counterpart is the sharper `Polynomial.natDegree_hasseDeriv_le : natDegree (hasseDeriv n p) ≤ natDegree p - n`; the project form drops the `- d j`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {j : σ} {d : σ →₀ ℕ} {P : MvPolynomial σ R}, degreeOf j P < d j → hasseDeriv d P = 0`
- **What**: If the order `d j` of the Hasse derivative `Δ_d` in some variable `x_j` exceeds the degree of `P` in `x_j`, then `Δ_d P = 0`.
- **How**: `ext e`; `coeff_hasseDeriv` turns the coefficient into `hasseCoeff d (e + d) * coeff (e + d) P`. The second factor is zero by `notMem_support_of_degreeOf_lt j`, since `degreeOf j P < d j ≤ (e + d) j` (`simp`). `simp` closes the goal.
- **Hypotheses**: `h : degreeOf j P < d j` (section variable `[CommSemiring R]`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:173 (`degreeOf_taylor_le`), Roth/Taylor.lean:207 (`eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`), Roth/MainLemma.lean:425 (`le_index_of_forall_eval_eq_zero`). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 219–223, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: This is the multivariate analogue of mathlib's `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_lt_natDegree`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial_mul`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial_mul`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {d a : σ →₀ ℕ}, (∀ j ∈ d.support, a j = 0) → ∀ (c : R) (g : MvPolynomial σ R), hasseDeriv d (monomial a c * g) = monomial a c * hasseDeriv d g`
- **What**: If the exponent vector a vanishes on every variable that the multi-index d differentiates, then the monomial c X^a passes through the Hasse derivative: Δ_d (c X^a · g) = c X^a · Δ_d g. This is the monomial case of the "constants w.r.t. the differentiated variables pull out" rule.
- **How**: Induction on `g` via `MvPolynomial.induction_on'`. Monomial case `g = c' X^b`: rewrite with `monomial_mul_monomial` and the project's `hasseDeriv_monomial` twice; the binomial factor `hasseCoeff d (a + b) = hasseCoeff d b` by `Finset.prod_congr` since `a j = 0` on `d.support`; the exponent identity `a + b - d = a + (b - d)` is proved pointwise (`Finsupp.ext`, case split on `j ∈ d.support`, `simp_all`); then `simp [monomial_mul_monomial, hs, mul_left_comm]`. Additive case: `simp [mul_add, hp, hq]` (uses linearity of `hasseDeriv`).
- **Hypotheses**: `ha : ∀ j ∈ d.support, a j = 0` (needed: otherwise the binomial factor and exponent change); `R` a commutative semiring (file-wide variable).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`
- **Used by**: Roth/HasseDeriv.lean: `hasseDeriv_mul_of_degreeOf_eq_zero` (l.243, used at l.246) only (grep over `Roth/` and `Roth.lean`). Transitively: Roth/Separation.lean `hasseDeriv_rename_mul_aeval` (l.146, uses `hasseDeriv_mul_of_degreeOf_eq_zero` at l.157 and l.161).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 225–236, proof length 10 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The hypothesis form `a j = 0` on `d.support` is the monomial-level version of the public lemma's `degreeOf j f = 0`; the public lemma reduces to it via `f.as_sum` and `monomial_le_degreeOf`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial_mul.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial_mul.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_mul_of_degreeOf_eq_zero`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_mul_of_degreeOf_eq_zero`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {d : σ →₀ ℕ} {f g : MvPolynomial σ R}, (∀ j ∈ d.support, degreeOf j f = 0) → hasseDeriv d (f * g) = f * hasseDeriv d g`
- **What**: If `f` does not involve any variable that `Δ_d` differentiates (degree 0 in every `x_j` with `d j ≠ 0`), then `f` passes through the Hasse derivative: `Δ_d (f g) = f · Δ_d g`.
- **How**: Expand `f` as a sum of monomials (`MvPolynomial.as_sum`), distribute (`Finset.sum_mul`, `map_sum`), and apply the private monomial case `hasseDeriv_monomial_mul` termwise; its hypothesis `a j = 0` comes from `monomial_le_degreeOf j` and `hf`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hf : ∀ j ∈ d.support, degreeOf j f = 0` (equivalently `Disjoint f.vars d.support`); section variable `[CommSemiring R]`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial_mul` (private)
- **Used by**: Roth/Separation.lean:157 and Roth/Separation.lean:161 (both in `hasseDeriv_rename_mul_aeval`, l.146). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 243–247, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Special case of the multivariate Leibniz rule `Δ_d (f g) = Σ_{a+b=d} Δ_a f · Δ_b g`, not present in the project or mathlib.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_mul_of_degreeOf_eq_zero.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_mul_of_degreeOf_eq_zero.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_single_aeval_X`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_single_aeval_X`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (j : σ) (n : ℕ) (f : Polynomial R), hasseDeriv (Finsupp.single j n) (Polynomial.aeval (X j : MvPolynomial σ R) f) = Polynomial.aeval (X j : MvPolynomial σ R) (Polynomial.hasseDeriv n f)`
- **What**: The multivariate Hasse derivative `Δ_{n e_j}` applied to a polynomial `f(x_j)` in the single variable `x_j` is the univariate Hasse derivative `D^(n) f`, evaluated at `x_j`.
- **How**: Induction on `f` via `Polynomial.induction_on'`; the additive case is `simp`; the monomial case is a `simp` computation with `X_pow_eq_monomial`, `C_mul_monomial`, `hasseCoeff`, `Finsupp.single_tsub`, `mul_assoc` (unfolding `hasseDeriv_monomial` and `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial` via simp lemmas, with `map_natCast` disabled).
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond section variables `[CommSemiring R]`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff` (unfolded in simp; `hasseDeriv_monomial`/`hasseDeriv_apply` as simp lemmas)
- **Used by**: Roth/Separation.lean:160 (in `hasseDeriv_rename_mul_aeval`, l.146, `rw` chain). Mentioned in Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:36 (module docstring). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 253–260, proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: Companion of `uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv` (l.266), which states the same compatibility for `σ` with `[Unique σ]`; this lemma follows from that one plus `hasseDeriv_rename` (evidence item 2).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_single_aeval_X.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_single_aeval_X.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] [Unique σ] (n : ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), uniqueAlgEquiv R σ (hasseDeriv (Finsupp.single default n) P) = Polynomial.hasseDeriv n (uniqueAlgEquiv R σ P)`
- **What**: When `σ` has a single element, mathlib's algebra isomorphism `MvPolynomial σ R ≃ₐ[R] R[X]` carries the multivariate Hasse derivative `Δ_{n e}` to the univariate Hasse derivative `D^(n)`.
- **How**: `ext m`; rewrite coefficients on both sides with mathlib's `coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv` and `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_coeff` and the project's `coeff_hasseDeriv`; close with `simp [hasseCoeff, Finsupp.single_add]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Unique σ]` (needed for `uniqueAlgEquiv`), section variable `[CommSemiring R]`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff` (unfolded in simp)
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:239 (in `index_unique`, l.234, `rw [Polynomial.taylor_coeff, ← uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv, ← Finsupp.unique_single d]`). Mentioned in Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:36 (module docstring). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 266–271, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Companion of `hasseDeriv_single_aeval_X` (l.253); each can be derived from the other (evidence item 3).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv_hasseDeriv.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {d k : σ →₀ ℕ} {r : σ → ℕ}, (∀ j, k j ≤ r j) → hasseCoeff d k ≤ 2 ^ ∑ j, r j`
- **What**: On the box `k ≤ r` (componentwise), the multi-index binomial coefficient `binom(k, d) = ∏_j binom(k_j, d_j)` is at most `2^(r_1 + ⋯ + r_m)`.
- **How**: Rewrites with `hasseCoeff_eq_prod` and `Finset.prod_pow_eq_pow_sum` to compare `∏_j binom(k_j,d_j)` with `∏_j 2^(r_j)` factorwise via `Finset.prod_le_prod`, each factor bounded by `Nat.choose_le_two_pow` followed by `Nat.pow_le_pow_right two_pos (hk j)`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (finitely many variables); `hk : ∀ j, k j ≤ r j` (the exponent lies in the degree box `r`). The section `[CommSemiring R]` is not included.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_prod`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean: `abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le` (l.140), `normOne_hasseDeriv_le` (l.157). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 276–280, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Not a glue lemma (real inequality argument), so the full workflow was run. Both hypotheses are avoidable: `hasseCoeff d k ≤ 2 ^ k.degree` holds for any `σ` (evidence file), and the box version follows by monotonicity.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (j : σ) (n : ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (n ! : R) • hasseDeriv (Finsupp.single j n) P = (⇑(pderiv j))^[n] P`
- **What**: `n! · Δ_{n e_j} P = ∂_j^n P`: the `n`-th iterate of the partial derivative in the variable `x_j` equals `n!` times the Hasse derivative of order `n e_j`. Over a `ℚ`-algebra this identifies `Δ_{n e_j}` with `(1/n!) ∂_j^n`.
- **How**: Induction on `n`; the zero case is `simp` (via `hasseDeriv_zero_apply`); the successor case rewrites with `Function.iterate_succ_apply'`, the induction hypothesis, `Derivation.map_smul`, the project lemma `pderiv_hasseDeriv` (`∂_j Δ_d = (d_j + 1) Δ_{d + e_j}`), `smul_smul`, `Finsupp.single_eq_same`, `Finsupp.single_add`, `Nat.factorial_succ`, `Nat.cast_mul`, `mul_comm`.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond section variables `[CommSemiring R]`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_zero_apply` (via `simp` in the zero case)
- **Used by**: unused in project (no code callers; `grep -rn factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single Roth/` finds only the declaration and the module docstring `Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:35`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 286–292, proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: Listed under `## Main results` of the module docstring. It is the exact multivariate counterpart of mathlib's `Polynomial.factorial_smul_hasseDeriv` (Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/HasseDeriv.lean:133), whose proof has the same induction shape.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.factorial_smul_hasseDeriv_single.md)_

### `Roth/Box.lean`

#### `def Roth.box`

### `def Roth.box`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} → [Fintype σ] → [DecidableEq σ] → (r : σ → ℕ) → Finset (σ →₀ ℕ)`
- **What**: The finite set of multi-indices `d : σ →₀ ℕ` with `d j ≤ r j` for every `j` (the box `∏ j [0, r j]` of exponent vectors).
- **How**: Image of `Fintype.piFinset fun j ↦ Finset.range (r j + 1)` under the embedding `Finsupp.equivFunOnFinite.symm.toEmbedding` (`Finset.map`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (finite variable set), `[DecidableEq σ]` (needed by `Fintype.piFinset`).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/Box.lean: `mem_box` (l.51), `card_box` (l.57), `card_le_card_box` (l.66-68, via `mem_box`/`card_box`), docstring of `weight_le_card` (l.100); Roth/Taylor.lean: l.199, 200, 206, 219 (Taylor expansion over the box); Roth/CoeffNorm.lean: l.215, 216 (unfolds `box` to use `Finset.prod_univ_sum`), 234, 236; Roth/Main.lean: l.392, 404, 405; Roth/MainLemma.lean: l.570, 608, 616; Roth/Lattice.lean: l.102, 104, 109, 117, 124, 132-133, 138, 144, 148, 151, 159, 162-163, 168, 170, 177, 203 (Lemma 8 lattice count).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 45–48, proof length 1 line (definition body)
- **Notes**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:282 and :295 (private `boxFamily_eq_image_finsupp_aux`) already use `Finset.Iic (Finsupp.ofSupportFinite r _)` as an inline box, so the project itself contains the mathlib form.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.box.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.box.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.mem_box`

### `theorem Roth.mem_box`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ] {r : σ → ℕ} {d : σ →₀ ℕ} : d ∈ Roth.box r ↔ ∀ j, d j ≤ r j`
- **What**: A multi-index `d` lies in the box `box r` iff it is bounded coordinatewise by `r`.
- **How**: `simp [box, Nat.lt_succ_iff]` — unfolds `box` to the `Finset.map` of `Fintype.piFinset (fun j ↦ range (r j + 1))`; simp uses `Finset.mem_map`, `Fintype.mem_piFinset`, `Finset.mem_range` and `Nat.lt_succ_iff`.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond `[Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ]` (section variables required by `box`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box`
- **Used by**: Roth/Box.lean:68 (`card_le_card_box`); Roth/Taylor.lean:206; Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:236; Roth/Main.lean:404; Roth/MainLemma.lean:616; Roth/Lattice.lean:109, 117, 124, 138.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 50–52, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Not a `:= rfl`/`Iff.rfl` glue lemma (needs simp through `map`/`piFinset`/`range`), and the parent `Roth.box` is `NO-mathlib-has-it` because of the more general mathlib def `Finset.Iic`, so per the re-aim rule this was assessed against `Finset.Iic` rather than inherited.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.mem_box.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.mem_box.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.card_box`

### `theorem Roth.card_box`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ] (r : σ → ℕ) : (Roth.box r).card = ∏ j, (r j + 1)`
- **What**: The box of multi-indices `{d : σ →₀ ℕ : ∀ j, d j ≤ r j}` has `∏ j (r j + 1)` elements.
- **How**: `simp [box]` — unfolds `box` to `Finset.map` of `Fintype.piFinset (fun j ↦ range (r j + 1))`; simp closes with `Finset.card_map`, `Fintype.card_piFinset`, `Finset.card_range`.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the section variables `[Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ]` (required by `box`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box`
- **Used by**: Roth/Box.lean:68 (`card_le_card_box`, which is in turn used at Roth/MainLemma.lean:506 and Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:264); Roth/Main.lean:405 (`rw [Finset.sum_const, card_box, nsmul_eq_mul]`); Roth/Lattice.lean:163 (`simp [card_box]`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 54–58, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Not a `:= rfl`/`Iff.rfl` glue lemma. The parent `Roth.box` is NO-mathlib-has-it because mathlib has the more general `Finset.Iic`, so per the re-aim rule this lemma was assessed against `#(Finset.Iic _)` rather than inherited. Listed under "Main results" in the Box.lean module docstring.

#### `theorem Roth.card_le_card_box`

### `theorem Roth.card_le_card_box`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {r : σ → ℕ} {s : Finset (σ →₀ ℕ)} (hs : ∀ d ∈ s, ∀ j, d j ≤ r j) : s.card ≤ ∏ j, (r j + 1)`
- **What**: A finset of multi-indices that are coordinatewise bounded by `r` has at most `∏ j (r j + 1)` elements (it sits inside the box `box r`).
- **How**: `classical`, then `(Finset.card_le_card fun d hd ↦ mem_box.2 (hs d hd)).trans_eq (card_box r)`: the inclusion `s ⊆ box r` via `Roth.mem_box`, `Finset.card_le_card`, then rewrite the box cardinality with `Roth.card_box`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hs` — every element of `s` is bounded by `r` coordinatewise. Section variable `[Fintype σ]` (the `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted and recovered by `classical`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.mem_box`, `Roth.card_box` (and through them `Roth.box`)
- **Used by**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:506 (`abs_coeff_diag_hasseDeriv_modByMonic_le_aux`, bound on `#(hasseDeriv j P).support`); Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:264 (`abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`, Lemma 3, bound on `#P.support`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 62–68, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Not a `:= rfl`/`Iff.rfl` glue lemma. Parent `Roth.box` is NO-mathlib-has-it (mathlib's `Finset.Iic`), so per the re-aim rule this was assessed against `s ⊆ Finset.Iic r → #s ≤ #(Iic r)`, not inherited. Both callers apply it to the support of an `MvPolynomial` bounded via `MvPolynomial.monomial_le_degreeOf`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_le_card_box.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_le_card_box.md)_

#### `def Roth.weight`

### `def Roth.weight`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (r : σ → ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : ℝ`
- **What**: The real weight `∑ j, d j / r j` of a multi-index `d` with respect to the parameters `r`; the index of a polynomial (`Roth.index`) is the least weight of a nonvanishing Taylor coefficient.
- **How**: Direct definition `∑ j, (d j : ℝ) / r j` (sum over `Finset.univ`, casts to `ℝ`).
- **Hypotheses**: section variable `[Fintype σ]` (the `[DecidableEq σ]` section variable is not included in the elaborated def).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/Box.lean (API: `weight_nonneg` 75, `weight_zero` 80, `weight_add` 85, `weight_eq_zero_iff` 91, `weight_single` 96, `weight_le_card` 103, `weight_mul_left` 110, `weight_le_degree_div` 118, `weight_mapDomain` 127); Roth/Index.lean:59 (definition of `Roth.index`), 70, 71, 77, 80, 86, 111, 131, 133, 202, 203, 245, 249; Roth/MainLemma.lean:421, 456, 555, 570, 609; Roth/Theta.lean:380, 496, 500, 502; Roth/Main.lean:349, 364, 366; Roth/Lattice.lean:144, 148, 151, 160, 168, 170, 178, 203.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 70–71, body 1 line
- **Notes**: Listed under "Main definitions" of the Box.lean module docstring. Roth/Index.lean:131 already proves a bridge to `Finsupp.weight` (with natural weights `∏ r / r j`, divided by `∏ r`), so the project is aware of the mathlib def.

#### `theorem Roth.weight_nonneg`

### `theorem Roth.weight_nonneg`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (r : σ → ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : 0 ≤ Roth.weight r d`
- **What**: The weight `∑ j, d j / r j` of any multi-index is nonnegative.
- **How**: Term proof `Fintype.sum_nonneg fun _ ↦ by positivity` (each summand `(d j : ℝ) / r j` is a quotient of natural-number casts).
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the section variable `[Fintype σ]` (needed by `Roth.weight`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`

#### `theorem Roth.weight_add`

### `theorem Roth.weight_add`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (r : σ → ℕ) (d e : σ →₀ ℕ) : Roth.weight r (d + e) = Roth.weight r d + Roth.weight r e`
- **What**: The weight `∑ j, d j / r j` is additive in the multi-index.
- **How**: `simp [weight, add_div, Finset.sum_add_distrib]` (unfold, `Finsupp.coe_add`, `Nat.cast_add`, split each quotient with `add_div`, split the sum with `Finset.sum_add_distrib`).
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the section variable `[Fintype σ]` (needed by `Roth.weight`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:114 (`index_sub_weight_le_index_hasseDeriv`, `simpa [weight_add, hiw]`); Roth/Theta.lean:391 (`rw [weight_add, weight_mapDomain ..., weight_single] at h1`); Roth/Lattice.lean:155 (`rw [weight_add, weight_mapDomain (Fin.castSucc_injective n), weight_single, mul_div_assoc]`).

#### `theorem Roth.weight_eq_zero_iff`

### `theorem Roth.weight_eq_zero_iff`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {r : σ → ℕ} (hr : ∀ j, 0 < r j) {d : σ →₀ ℕ} : Roth.weight r d = 0 ↔ d = 0`
- **What**: For strictly positive parameters `r`, the weight `∑ j, d j / r j` of a multi-index vanishes exactly when the multi-index is zero.
- **How**: One `simp` call unfolding `weight` and using `Finset.sum_eq_zero_iff_of_nonneg` (each summand is nonnegative by `div_nonneg`), `(hr _).ne'` to cancel the division, and `Finsupp.ext_iff`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hr : ∀ j, 0 < r j` (essential: with `r j = 0`, `d j / 0 = 0` and any `d` supported at `j` has weight 0; evidence (5)); section variable `[Fintype σ]`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:102 (`index_eq_zero_iff`: `rw [h, weight_eq_zero_iff hr] at hdw`); mentioned in the Roth/Box.lean:31 module docstring.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 88–93, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Source docstring cites clean.tex §4 l.565–566 ("Since each `r_j>0`, such a weight is zero exactly when every `i_j=0`").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_eq_zero_iff.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_eq_zero_iff.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.weight_single`

### `theorem Roth.weight_single`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (r : σ → ℕ) (j : σ) (n : ℕ) : Roth.weight r (Finsupp.single j n) = n / r j`
- **What**: The single multi-index `n • e_j` has weight `n / r_j`.
- **How**: `classical; simp [weight, Finsupp.single_apply, ite_div]` (unfold to the `univ` sum, rewrite each coordinate with `Finsupp.single_apply`, push the `if` through the division with `ite_div`, collapse with `Finset.sum_ite_eq`).
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the section variable `[Fintype σ]` (needed by `Roth.weight`).

#### `theorem Roth.weight_le_card`

### `theorem Roth.weight_le_card`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {r : σ → ℕ} {d : σ →₀ ℕ} (hd : ∀ j, d j ≤ r j) : Roth.weight r d ≤ Fintype.card σ`
- **What**: A multi-index in the box `d ≤ r` (coordinatewise) has weight `∑ j, d j / r j` at most the number of variables `card σ`.
- **How**: Term proof: `Finset.sum_le_sum` with each summand bounded by `1` via `div_le_one_of_le₀` (cast of `hd j`, `positivity` for `0 ≤ r j`), then `.trans_eq (by simp)` to rewrite `∑ j, 1 = card σ`. Zero parameters `r j = 0` are harmless: `hd` forces `d j = 0`, and `0 / 0 = 0 ≤ 1`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hd : ∀ j, d j ≤ r j` (membership in `box r`, stated unbundled); section variable `[Fintype σ]`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:227 (`index_le_card`, `exact hdw ▸ weight_le_card fun j ↦ ...`). Also listed in the Box.lean module docstring (Main results, line 32).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 100–106, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Docstring cites clean.tex §5 l.667 ("Taylor's theorem gives a nonzero derivative with `0 ≤ i_j ≤ r_j`, of weight at most `m`").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_le_card.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_le_card.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.weight_mul_left`

### `theorem Roth.weight_mul_left`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {l : ℕ} (r : σ → ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : Roth.weight (fun j ↦ l * r j) d = Roth.weight r d / l`
- **What**: Scaling every parameter `r j` by the same natural number `l` divides the weight `∑ j, d j / r j` by `l`.
- **How**: One `simp [weight, Finset.sum_div, div_div, mul_comm]`: unfold to the sum, pull `/ l` inside with `Finset.sum_div`, merge with `div_div`, commute the product. Holds for `l = 0` too (both sides `0` under `x / 0 = 0`).
- **Hypotheses**: none (section variable `[Fintype σ]`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:206 (`index_mul_left_weights`, `rw [weight_mul_left, smul_eq_mul, div_eq_inv_mul]` inside the image computation).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 107–111, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Docstring cites clean.tex §4 l.639 (`ind(P; α; l r) = (1/l) ind(P; α; r)`); the paper's Lean note (l.630–634) asks for "scaling of the index weights" as a separate lemma.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_mul_left.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_mul_left.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.weight_le_degree_div`

### `theorem Roth.weight_le_degree_div`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {r : σ → ℕ} {m : ℕ} (hm : 0 < m) (hr : ∀ j, m ≤ r j) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : Roth.weight r d ≤ ↑d.degree / ↑m`
- **What**: If every parameter `r j` is at least a positive natural `m`, then the weight `∑ j, d j / r j` of a multi-index `d` is at most its total degree divided by `m`.
- **How**: `rw [weight, Finsupp.degree_eq_sum, Nat.cast_sum, Finset.sum_div]` turns the goal into `∑ j, d j / r j ≤ ∑ j, d j / m`, closed by `gcongr with j` (termwise `div_le_div_of_nonneg_left`, positivity side goals discharged from `hm`) and `exact hr j`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hm : 0 < m` (needed so `d j / m` dominates `d j / r j`; with `m = 0` the RHS is `0`), `hr : ∀ j, m ≤ r j`; section variable `[Fintype σ]` (the `[DecidableEq σ]` section variable was omitted earlier in the file).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`
- **Used by**: unused in project (`grep -rn weight_le_degree_div Roth/ Roth.lean` returns only the declaration at Roth/Box.lean:118).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 113–122 (docstring 113–117, statement 118–119, proof 120–122), proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Docstring cites §5, proof of Lemma 6 ("`∑_{j<p} i_j / r_j ≤ w / r_{p-1}`"). The formalised Lemma 6 (Roth/Theta.lean, `sum_max_sub_le_index_det` and its callers) instead uses the private helper `weight_le_delta_aux` (Roth/Theta.lean, ~l.493), which re-derives a closely related bound `weight (r ∘ some) d ≤ δ` from a real-ratio hypothesis `r none ≤ δ * r (some j)` and `d.degree ≤ r none`; that form is not an instance of `weight_le_degree_div` (the cleanup audit `.mathlib-quality/cleanup/Theta-index_le_lem6.md` already noted the lemma "requires a common NAT denominator lower bound; cannot directly express scaled ratio with arbitrary real δ").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_le_degree_div.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_le_degree_div.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.weight_mapDomain`

### `theorem Roth.weight_mapDomain`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {f : σ → τ} (hf : Function.Injective f) (r : τ → ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : Roth.weight r (d.mapDomain f) = Roth.weight (r ∘ f) d`
- **What**: Pushing a multi-index forward along an injective change of variables `f : σ → τ` and evaluating the weight with parameters `r` equals evaluating the weight of the original multi-index with the pulled-back parameters `r ∘ f`.
- **How**: `simpa [Finsupp.sum_fintype, weight]` applied to `Finsupp.sum_mapDomain_index_inj` with `h := fun j n ↦ (n : ℝ) / r j` (rewrites both `univ` sums as `Finsupp.sum` and transports along the injective `mapDomain`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hf : Function.Injective f` (superfluous, see below); section variable `[Fintype σ]`, plus `[Fintype τ]` (both needed for `Roth.weight` to typecheck).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight`
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:219 (`exact weight_mapDomain hf r d`, inside `index_rename`); Roth/Theta.lean:391 (`rw [weight_add, weight_mapDomain (Option.some_injective _), weight_single] at h1`); Roth/Lattice.lean:155 (`rw [weight_add, weight_mapDomain (Fin.castSucc_injective n), weight_single, mul_div_assoc]`). Also named in the Box.lean module docstring (line 33).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 123–130, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: docstring cites §4, Lemma 4-2. The injectivity hypothesis is not needed: the weight is additive, so it commutes with `mapDomain` for any `f` (evidence (3), (4), (6)).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_mapDomain.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.weight_mapDomain.md)_

### `Roth/Wronskian.lean`

#### `def Roth.wronskianMatrix`

### `def Roth.wronskianMatrix`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} → [Field K] → {l : ℕ} → (φ : Fin l → K[X]) → Matrix (Fin l) (Fin l) K[X]`
- **What**: The `l × l` Wronskian matrix of a family of univariate polynomials built from Hasse derivatives: entry `(μ, ν)` is `hasseDeriv μ (φ ν) = (1/μ!) d^μ φ_ν / dt^μ`.
- **How**: One-line definition `Matrix.of fun μ ν ↦ hasseDeriv μ (φ ν)` using mathlib's `Polynomial.hasseDeriv` and `Matrix.of`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); no other hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (`hasseDeriv` here is mathlib's `Polynomial.hasseDeriv`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean: `Roth.wronskian` (l.60, definition), `wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent` (l.71), `coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective` (l.139), `wronskian_update_sub_smul` (l.161, l.163); Roth/GenWronskian.lean: `wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux` (private, l.244); Roth/Separation.lean: `det_hasseDeriv_separated` (l.358)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 55–57, proof length 1 line (definition body)
- **Notes**: Uses Hasse derivatives, not ordinary derivatives, so the determinant differs from the classical Wronskian by the constant `∏ μ!` (equal for `l ≤ 2`). For `l = 2` its determinant is exactly mathlib's `Polynomial.wronskian a b` (verified in evidence).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskianMatrix.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskianMatrix.md)_

#### `def Roth.wronskian`

### `def Roth.wronskian`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} → [Field K] → {l : ℕ} → (φ : Fin l → K[X]) → K[X]`
- **What**: The Wronskian of `l` univariate polynomials, `W(φ_0, …, φ_{l-1}) = det (D^{(μ)} φ_ν)_{0 ≤ μ, ν < l}` with `D^{(μ)} = hasseDeriv μ` the `μ`-th Hasse derivative (`(1/μ!) d^μ/dt^μ`).
- **How**: One-line definition `(wronskianMatrix φ).det`, i.e. mathlib's `Matrix.det` of the project's `Roth.wronskianMatrix`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); no other hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskianMatrix`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean: `wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent` (l.66), `coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective` (l.123 statement, l.139 unfolds it), `wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective` (l.149), `wronskian_update_sub_smul` (l.159), `wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent` (l.206), `wronskian_eq_zero_iff` (l.220); Roth/GenWronskian.lean: `wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux` (private, l.241 statement, l.244 unfolds it); Roth/Separation.lean: `det_hasseDeriv_separated` (l.356 statement, l.357 unfolds it), `exists_rat_factors_det_hasseDeriv_aux` (private, l.528)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 59–60, proof length 1 line (definition body)
- **Notes**: Hasse-derivative normalisation: differs from the classical Wronskian by the constant `∏_{μ<l} μ!` (equal for `l ≤ 2`). For `l = 2` it is exactly mathlib's `Polynomial.wronskian a b` over any commutative ring (verified in evidence). The name clashes conceptually with mathlib's 2-ary `Polynomial.wronskian` if moved to the `Polynomial` namespace.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent`

### `theorem Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} {φ : Fin l → K[X]} (h : ¬ LinearIndependent K φ) : wronskian φ = 0`
- **What**: A `K`-linearly dependent family of `l` polynomials has vanishing (Hasse) Wronskian `det (D^{(μ)} φ_ν)`.
- **How**: From `Fintype.not_linearIndependent_iff` get a nontrivial relation `∑ g ν • φ ν = 0`; the constant vector `C ∘ g` is a nonzero kernel vector of the Wronskian matrix (apply the linear map `hasseDeriv μ` to the relation, `smul_eq_C_mul`), so `Matrix.exists_mulVec_eq_zero_iff` gives `det = 0`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); `h : ¬ LinearIndependent K φ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian`, `Roth.wronskianMatrix`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:222 (`wronskian_eq_zero_iff`, reverse direction). No other project use.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 62–71, proof length 6 lines
- **Notes**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean:41 `genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent` repeats the identical argument for the multivariate generalized Wronskian (same 5-line proof shape); both are instances of one abstract fact (det of `(L_μ (φ ν))` vanishes for linear maps `L_μ` and dependent `φ`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.det_choose_eq`

### `theorem Roth.det_choose_eq`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} [CharZero K] (d : Fin l → ℕ), (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ ((d ν).choose μ : K)).det = (∏ μ : Fin l, ((μ : ℕ).factorial : K)⁻¹) * ∏ i : Fin l, ∏ j ∈ Finset.Ioi i, ((d j : K) - d i)`
- **What**: The binomial determinant `det (C(d_ν, μ))_{μ,ν}` equals the Vandermonde product `∏_{i<j} (d_j − d_i)` divided by `∏_{μ<l} μ!`.
- **How**: Rewrites each entry as `(μ!)⁻¹ · descPochhammer K μ` evaluated at `d ν` (`Nat.cast_choose_eq_descPochhammer_div`), so the matrix is a column-scaled transpose. It then pulls the scalars out with `Matrix.det_mul_column` and `Matrix.det_transpose`, and finishes with `Matrix.det_eval_matrixOfPolynomials_eq_det_vandermonde` (using `descPochhammer_natDegree` and `monic_descPochhammer`) and `Matrix.det_vandermonde`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable), `[CharZero K]`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:151 `wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective` (in a `simpa` call). It is also named in the module docstring at l.28.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 79–93, proof length 11 lines
- **Notes**: Mathlib proves the same identity, with the factorials multiplied in, over ℤ and with transposed indexing, as the private lemma `Matrix.of_eval_descPochhammer_eq_mul_of_choose` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Vandermonde.lean:301). It uses it only to prove `superFactorial_dvd_vandermonde_det`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.det_choose_eq.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.det_choose_eq.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'`

### `theorem Roth.coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {ι : Type*} (s : Finset ι) (f : ι → K[X]) (n : ι → ℕ), (∀ i ∈ s, (f i).natDegree ≤ n i) → (∏ i ∈ s, f i).coeff (∑ i ∈ s, n i) = ∏ i ∈ s, (f i).coeff (n i)`
- **What**: If each factor `f i` has degree at most `n i`, then the coefficient of `X^(∑ n i)` in `∏ f i` is the product of the coefficients of `X^(n i)` in the `f i` (the "top" coefficient of a product with per-factor degree bounds).
- **How**: `Finset.induction_on`; empty case `simp`; insert case rewrites with `Finset.prod_insert`, `Finset.sum_insert`, then `Polynomial.coeff_mul_add_eq_of_natDegree_le` applied with the bound `h.1` for the new factor and `(Polynomial.natDegree_prod_le _ _).trans (Finset.sum_le_sum h.2)` for the remaining product, then the induction hypothesis.
- **Hypotheses**: `h : ∀ i ∈ s, (f i).natDegree ≤ n i`; section variable `[Field K]` (only `CommSemiring` is needed, verified in evidence file).
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib lemmas; `K[X]` from the section variable `{K : Type*} [Field K]`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:131 in `Roth.coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective` (inside the `key` step) only (`grep -rn "coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'" Roth/`). Transitively: `Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective` (Roth/Wronskian.lean:148, uses it at l.152).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 93–103, proof length 8 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The prime in the name distinguishes it from mathlib's `Polynomial.coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le` (opened via `open Polynomial`). No docstring.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.sum_range_le_sum_of_injective`

### `theorem Roth.sum_range_le_sum_of_injective`
- **Type**: `∀ {l : ℕ} (d : Fin l → ℕ), Function.Injective d → ∑ ν : Fin l, (ν : ℕ) ≤ ∑ ν, d ν`
- **What**: `l` pairwise distinct natural numbers sum to at least `0 + 1 + ⋯ + (l − 1) = l(l−1)/2`. This is the source's `D = ∑ d_ν − l(l−1)/2 ≥ 0` (clean.tex l.183).
- **How**: Casts to ℤ (`exact_mod_cast`). It then applies mathlib's `Finset.sum_range_le_sum` with `c := 0` to the finset `(univ.image d).map Nat.castEmbedding` of integers, and simplifies with `Finset.card_image_of_injective`, `Finset.sum_image hd.injOn`, `Fin.sum_univ_eq_sum_range` and `Nat.cast_sum`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hd : Function.Injective d`. This is needed: for `l = 2` and `d = 0` the claim reads `1 ≤ 0`. The counterexample was kernel-checked in the earlier cleanup report `.mathlib-quality/cleanup/Wronskian-sum_range_le_sum_of_injective.md` and also appears in `.mathlib-quality/decomposition.md` R4.4.
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: unused in project. `grep -rn sum_range_le_sum_of_injective Roth Roth.lean` finds only the declaration at Roth/Wronskian.lean:107. Outside `.lean` files the name appears only in planning and cleanup notes (`tickets.md`, `decomposition.md`, `cleanup/*.md`, `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md`, `overview/decls.tsv`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 105–114 (docstring 105–106, statement 107–108, proof 109–114), proof length 6 lines
- **Notes**: It was planned (tickets.md step 1) as the reason `D := ∑ natDegree − ∑ ν` is well defined. The final `coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective` states the coefficient index with truncated ℕ subtraction and holds for any family, so this lemma became dead code.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_range_le_sum_of_injective.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_range_le_sum_of_injective.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective`

### `theorem Roth.coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} {φ : Fin l → K[X]}, (wronskian φ).coeff (∑ ν, (φ ν).natDegree - ∑ ν : Fin l, (ν : ℕ)) = (∏ ν, (φ ν).leadingCoeff) * (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ ((φ ν).natDegree.choose μ : K)).det`
- **What**: For any family `φ_0, …, φ_{l-1}` of polynomials with degrees `d_ν`, the coefficient of `t^D`, `D = ∑ d_ν − ∑ ν` (truncated subtraction), in the Hasse Wronskian `det (D^{(μ)} φ_ν)` is `(∏ lc(φ_ν)) · det (C(d_ν, μ))_{μ,ν}`.
- **How**: Expands both determinants with `Matrix.det_apply'` and `finsetSum_coeff` and compares permutation by permutation. For a permutation `σ` with `σ i ≤ d_i` for all `i`, it rewrites `D = ∑ (d_i − σ i)` (`Equiv.sum_comp`, `Finset.sum_tsub_distrib`), applies the private per-index top-coefficient lemma `coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'` (via `natDegree_hasseDeriv_le`) and `hasseDeriv_coeff`. Otherwise some factor vanishes (`hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_lt_natDegree`, `Nat.choose_eq_zero_of_lt`). Finishes with `simp [coeff_intCast_mul, Finset.prod_mul_distrib, ...]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); none on `φ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian`, `Roth.wronskianMatrix`, `Roth.coeff_prod_of_natDegree_le'` (private)
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:152 `wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective` (in `simpa ... using`); also named in the module docstring at l.41.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 122–143, proof length 19 lines
- **Notes**: The name suffix `_of_natDegree_injective` refers to a hypothesis the statement does not have (the docstring says so explicitly). The identity holds for every family; injectivity is only needed downstream to make the RHS nonzero.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective`

### `theorem Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} [CharZero K] {φ : Fin l → K[X]}, (∀ ν, φ ν ≠ 0) → Function.Injective (fun ν ↦ (φ ν).natDegree) → wronskian φ ≠ 0`
- **What**: In characteristic zero, nonzero polynomials `φ_0, …, φ_{l-1}` of pairwise distinct degrees have nonzero (Hasse) Wronskian `det (D^{(μ)} φ_ν)`.
- **How**: Assumes `wronskian φ = 0` and `simpa`s the coefficient identity `coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective` (coefficient of `t^{∑d − ∑ν}` is `(∏ lc φ_ν) · det (C(d_ν, μ))`), rewriting the binomial determinant with `det_choose_eq` (`(∏ μ!)⁻¹ ∏_{i<j}(d_j − d_i)`) and closing with `Finset.prod_eq_zero_iff`, `sub_eq_zero`, `Nat.factorial_ne_zero`, `hd.eq_iff`, `hφ`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable), `[CharZero K]`, `hφ : ∀ ν, φ ν ≠ 0`, `hd : Function.Injective fun ν ↦ (φ ν).natDegree`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian`, `Roth.coeff_wronskian_of_natDegree_injective`, `Roth.det_choose_eq`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:210 (`wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, base case of the strong induction); module docstring l.30.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 148–153, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Short proof because the work is in the two helper lemmas; it is the "distinct degrees ⇒ nonzero" step of §2, first lemma.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.wronskian_update_sub_smul`

### `theorem Roth.wronskian_update_sub_smul`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} {φ : Fin l → K[X]} {ν ν' : Fin l} (h : ν ≠ ν') (c : K) : wronskian (Function.update φ ν' (φ ν' - C c * φ ν)) = wronskian φ`
- **What**: Replacing `φ ν'` by `φ ν' - c φ ν` (constant `c`, `ν ≠ ν'`) does not change the Wronskian `det (D^{(μ)} φ_ρ)`.
- **How**: The new Wronskian matrix is `(wronskianMatrix φ).updateCol ν' (col ν' + (-C c) • col ν)` (entrywise by `Matrix.updateCol_apply`, `Function.update_apply`, linearity of `hasseDeriv`, `smul_eq_C_mul`); then `Matrix.det_updateCol_add_smul_self`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); `h : ν ≠ ν'`; `c : K`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian`, `Roth.wronskianMatrix`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:212 (`wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, induction step on total degree). No other project use (also mentioned in the module docstring l.38).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 158–164, proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: Companion of `linearIndependent_update_sub_smul` (l.170). The source (§2, clean.tex l.179–180) states the general form `W(g) = W(φ) det A` for a constant change of basis `A`; the project only formalises the transvection case it needs.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_update_sub_smul.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_update_sub_smul.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.linearIndependent_update_sub_smul`

### `theorem Roth.linearIndependent_update_sub_smul`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} {φ : Fin l → K[X]} {ν ν' : Fin l} (h : ν ≠ ν') (c : K) (hφ : LinearIndependent K φ) : LinearIndependent K (Function.update φ ν' (φ ν' - C c * φ ν))`
- **What**: Replacing `φ ν'` by `φ ν' - c φ ν` (constant `c`, `ν ≠ ν'`) in a linearly independent family of polynomials keeps it linearly independent.
- **How**: Rewrites linear independence as `card = finrank (span)` (`linearIndependent_iff_card_eq_finrank_span`, `Set.finrank`), turns `C c *` into `c •` (`smul_eq_C_mul`), and uses mathlib's `Submodule.span_range_update_sub_smul` to see the span is unchanged.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); `h : ν ≠ ν'`; `c : K`; `hφ : LinearIndependent K φ`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib lemmas; `wronskian` not involved)
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:190 (`sum_natDegree_update_lt`, nonvanishing of the updated member), Roth/Wronskian.lean:214 (`wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, induction step). No other project use.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 170–175, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Nothing polynomial-specific: the statement is about a family of vectors in any module. The `finrank` proof needs a division ring (finite-dimensional span), but the statement itself holds over any ring (evidence (1)).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.linearIndependent_update_sub_smul.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.linearIndependent_update_sub_smul.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.sum_natDegree_update_lt`

### `theorem Roth.sum_natDegree_update_lt`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} {φ : Fin l → K[X]} (hφ : LinearIndependent K φ) {ν ν' : Fin l} (h : ν ≠ ν') (hdeg : (φ ν).natDegree = (φ ν').natDegree) : ∑ ρ, (Function.update φ ν' (φ ν' - C ((φ ν').leadingCoeff / (φ ν).leadingCoeff) * φ ν) ρ).natDegree < ∑ ρ, (φ ρ).natDegree`
- **What**: In a linearly independent family of polynomials, if two distinct members have equal degree, replacing `φ ν'` by `φ ν' - (lc φ ν' / lc φ ν) φ ν` (cancelling its leading term) strictly decreases the total degree `∑ deg φ ρ`.
- **How**: Gets `φ i ≠ 0` from `LinearIndependent.ne_zero`; nonvanishing of the updated member from the project lemma `linearIndependent_update_sub_smul`; the pointwise strict drop from mathlib's `degree_sub_lt_left` (after `degree_C_mul`, `leadingCoeff_mul`, `leadingCoeff_C`, `div_mul_cancel₀`) and `natDegree_lt_natDegree`; then `Finset.sum_lt_sum` with `Function.update_of_ne` for the other indices.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); `hφ : LinearIndependent K φ`; `h : ν ≠ ν'`; `hdeg` equal natDegrees.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.linearIndependent_update_sub_smul`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:213 (`wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, decreasing measure in the strong induction). No other project use.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 182–204, proof length 19 lines
- **Notes**: `hφ` and `h` are used only to obtain `φ ν ≠ 0` and `φ ν' - C a * φ ν ≠ 0`; both can be taken directly as hypotheses (verified in evidence file).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_natDegree_update_lt.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_natDegree_update_lt.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`

### `theorem Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} [CharZero K] {φ : Fin l → K[X]}, LinearIndependent K φ → wronskian φ ≠ 0`
- **What**: In characteristic zero, a linearly independent family `φ_0, …, φ_{l−1}` of univariate polynomials has nonzero (Hasse) Wronskian `det (D^{(μ)} φ_ν)`.
- **How**: Strong induction (`Nat.strong_induction_on`) on the total degree `∑ ν, natDegree (φ ν)`. If the degrees are pairwise distinct, conclude by `wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective` (with `LinearIndependent.ne_zero`). Otherwise pick `ν ≠ ν'` of equal degree (`Function.not_injective_iff`), replace `φ ν'` by `φ ν' − (lc φ ν' / lc φ ν) φ ν`. This leaves the Wronskian unchanged (`wronskian_update_sub_smul`), keeps the family independent (`linearIndependent_update_sub_smul`) and strictly lowers the total degree (`sum_natDegree_update_lt`), so the induction hypothesis applies.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable), `[CharZero K]`, `h : LinearIndependent K φ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian`, `Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_natDegree_injective`, `Roth.wronskian_update_sub_smul`, `Roth.linearIndependent_update_sub_smul`, `Roth.sum_natDegree_update_lt`
- **Used by**: Roth/Wronskian.lean:221 (`wronskian_eq_zero_iff`, forward direction); Roth/GenWronskian.lean:262 (`exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`, applied to the Kronecker-substituted family); Roth/Separation.lean:534 (the separated representation of the main induction).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 205–214, proof length 8 lines
- **Notes**: This is the hard direction of §2, first lemma of the source. The iff form `wronskian_eq_zero_iff` sits next to it.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_iff`

### `theorem Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_iff`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {l : ℕ} [CharZero K] {φ : Fin l → K[X]}, wronskian φ = 0 ↔ ¬ LinearIndependent K φ`
- **What**: In characteristic zero, the (Hasse) Wronskian `det (D^{(μ)} φ_ν)` of a family of `l` polynomials is zero iff the family is linearly dependent over `K`.
- **How**: Term-mode `Iff.intro`: forward direction is `imp_not_comm.mp wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, backward direction is `wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable), `[CharZero K]`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian`, `Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, `Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent`
- **Used by**: unused in project (`grep -rn wronskian_eq_zero_iff Roth/ Roth.lean`: only the module docstring l.32 and the declaration l.219; call sites use the two directional lemmas directly)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 219–223, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Listed under `## Main results` of the module docstring; it is the source statement (clean.tex §2, first lemma, l.166).
