### `Roth/Taylor.lean`

#### `def MvPolynomial.taylor`

### `def MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Type**: `{σ R : Type*} → [CommSemiring R] → (α : σ → R) → MvPolynomial σ R →ₐ[R] MvPolynomial σ R`
- **What**: The translation (multivariate Taylor shift) `P ↦ P(X + α)`, the `R`-algebra endomorphism sending each variable `X j` to `X j + α j`.
- **How**: `aeval fun j ↦ X j + C (α j)` (one line, mathlib's `MvPolynomial.aeval`).

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_apply`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_apply`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), taylor α P = aeval (fun j ↦ X j + C (α j)) P`
- **What**: The translate `P(X + α)` is the evaluation of `P` at the point `X + α` (substitution `X j ↦ X j + α j`).
- **How**: `rfl` (the definition of `taylor` is `aeval fun j ↦ X j + C (α j)`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: unused in project (`grep -rn taylor_apply Roth/` finds only the declaration and Roth/Index.lean:243, which is mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.taylor_apply`; not `@[simp]`, so no implicit uses).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 45–47, proof length 1 line (`rfl`)
- **Notes**: Name and role mirror mathlib's `Polynomial.taylor_apply : taylor r f = f.comp (X + C r)` (Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Taylor.lean:43), which mathlib uses heavily as the unfolding lemma.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_apply.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_apply.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_monomial`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_monomial`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) (k : σ →₀ ℕ) (a : R), taylor α (monomial k a) = C a * k.prod fun j n ↦ (X j + C (α j)) ^ n`
- **What**: The translate `P(X + α)` of the monomial `a X^k` is `a ∏_j (X_j + α_j)^{k_j}`.
- **How**: term `aeval_monomial _ _ _` (mathlib's `MvPolynomial.aeval_monomial : aeval g (monomial d r) = algebraMap _ _ r * d.prod fun i k => g i ^ k`, Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Eval.lean:681), after `taylor α` unfolds definitionally to `aeval fun j ↦ X j + C (α j)` and `algebraMap R (MvPolynomial σ R)` is definitionally `C`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:212 (`eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`, in a `rw` chain turning each `taylor (-α) (monomial d c)` into a product over `Fintype σ`). Not `@[simp]`; no other uses (the private `coeff_taylor_monomial_*_aux` lemmas only share the name prefix).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 57–60, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Name and shape mirror mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.taylor_monomial : taylor r (monomial i k) = C k * (X + C r) ^ i` (Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Taylor.lean:64).

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.coeff_aeval_X_mul`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_aeval_X_mul`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (i : σ) (f : Polynomial R) (B : MvPolynomial σ R), (∀ e ∈ B.support, e i = 0) → ∀ (d : σ →₀ ℕ), (Polynomial.aeval (X i) f * B).coeff d = f.coeff (d i) * B.coeff (d.erase i)`
- **What**: If the multivariate polynomial B does not involve the variable X_i (every monomial in its support has i-exponent 0), then the coefficient of the product f(X_i) · B at the multi-index d factors as (coefficient of t^{d_i} in the one-variable polynomial f) times (coefficient of B at d with the i-th entry deleted). It is the "separation of variables" coefficient formula.
- **How**: Expands `Polynomial.aeval (X i) f` as a finite sum `∑_m C(f_m) X_i^m` (`Polynomial.aeval_def`, `Polynomial.eval₂_eq_sum`, `Polynomial.sum_def`, `Finset.sum_mul`, `coeff_sum`), rewrites each term as a monomial (`X_pow_eq_monomial`, `C_mul_monomial`) and uses `coeff_monomial_mul'` to get `if single i m ≤ d then f_m * B.coeff (d - single i m) else 0`. `Finset.sum_eq_single (d i)` isolates the term m = d i, evaluated with `tsub_eq_of_eq_add (Finsupp.erase_add_single i d).symm`; for m ≠ d i with `single i m ≤ d` one has m < d i so `(d - single i m) i ≠ 0`, hence `B.coeff (d - single i m) = 0` by `hB` (`notMem_support_iff`, `Finsupp.single_le_iff`, `Nat.sub_eq_zero_iff_le`); the case d i ∉ f.support is closed by `Polynomial.notMem_support_iff`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]`; `hB : ∀ e ∈ B.support, e i = 0` (B free of X_i). `classical` for decidability of `σ`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib lemmas; it sits in the project namespace `MvPolynomial` but references no project declaration)
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean: `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (private, l.99; `rw [..., coeff_aeval_X_mul i _ _ hsupp, ...]` at l.115) only — `grep -rn coeff_aeval_X_mul Roth/` gives the definition and that use. Transitively: `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux` (l.121) → `coeff_taylor` (l.136) and everything downstream of Taylor's theorem.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 63–80, proof length 14 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The `Polynomial.aeval (X i) f` form is chosen to match the call site, where `(X i + C (α i))^n` is rewritten to `Polynomial.aeval (X i) ((Polynomial.X + Polynomial.C (α i))^n)` so that `Polynomial.coeff_X_add_C_pow` applies.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_aeval_X_mul.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_aeval_X_mul.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_single_add_eq`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_single_add_eq`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} {i : σ} {n : ℕ} {k' : σ →₀ ℕ}, k' i = 0 → ∀ (d : σ →₀ ℕ), hasseCoeff d (Finsupp.single i n + k') = n.choose (d i) * hasseCoeff (d.erase i) k'`
- **What**: The multinomial binomial factor hasseCoeff d k = ∏_{j ∈ supp d} C(k_j, d_j) splits off the i-th variable: if k = n·e_i + k' with k' not involving i, then hasseCoeff d k = C(n, d_i) · hasseCoeff (d with i-th entry removed) k'.
- **How**: Unfolds `hasseCoeff` and rewrites the product with `← Finsupp.mul_prod_erase' d i _ (by simp)` (valid since `(k j).choose 0 = 1`), giving `(single i n + k') i).choose (d i) * (d.erase i).prod ...`; `congr 1`: the first factor closes by `simp [hi]`; the second by `Finsupp.prod_congr`, using `Finsupp.support_erase`, `Finset.ne_of_mem_erase` and `Finsupp.single_eq_of_ne` to see `(single i n + k') j = k' j` for `j ≠ i`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hi : k' i = 0` (k' does not involve variable i); `classical`. No ring needed (statement is in ℕ); the ambient `[CommSemiring R]` section variable is not used by this lemma's statement.
- **Uses from project**: `hasseCoeff` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:50)
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean: `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (private, l.99; `rw [..., hasseCoeff_single_add_eq hi, ...]` at l.117) only — `grep -rn hasseCoeff_single_add_eq Roth/` gives the definition and that use. Transitively: `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux` → `coeff_taylor` and everything downstream of Taylor's theorem.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 82–89, proof length 6 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). Companion of `prod_pow_single_add_tsub` (l.91), which does the same splitting for the `α ^ (k - d)` evaluation factor; both are used together at l.117.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_single_add_eq.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_single_add_eq.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.prod_pow_single_add_tsub`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.prod_pow_single_add_tsub`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) {i : σ} {n : ℕ} {k' : σ →₀ ℕ}, k' i = 0 → ∀ (d : σ →₀ ℕ), (Finsupp.single i n + k' - d).prod (fun j e ↦ α j ^ e) = α i ^ (n - d i) * (k' - d.erase i).prod (fun j e ↦ α j ^ e)`
- **What**: The evaluated monomial α^(k - d) = ∏_j α_j^(k_j - d_j) splits off the i-th variable: if k = n·e_i + k' with k' not involving i, then α^(k - d) = α_i^(n - d_i) · α^(k' - d') where d' is d with its i-th entry erased.
- **How**: Proves the exponent identity `single i n + k' - d = single i (n - d i) + (k' - d.erase i)` pointwise (`ext j`, case split `eq_or_ne j i`, `simp [*, Finsupp.erase_ne]`), then rewrites with it and closes by `simp [h, Finsupp.prod_add_index', pow_add, -Finsupp.single_tsub]` (the product over a sum of finsupps splits multiplicatively; `Finsupp.prod_single_index` handles the single part; `Finsupp.single_tsub` must be excluded so simp does not split `single i (n - d i)`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hi : k' i = 0` (k' does not involve variable i); `classical`. Only the commutative-monoid structure of R is used (powers and products); `[CommSemiring R]` comes from the section variable.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib `Finsupp` API: `Finsupp.single`, `Finsupp.erase`, `Finsupp.erase_ne`, `Finsupp.prod_add_index'`, `pow_add`, `Finsupp.single_tsub`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean: `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (private, l.101; `rw [..., prod_pow_single_add_tsub α hi]` at l.117) only — `grep -rn prod_pow_single_add_tsub Roth/` gives the definition (l.92) and that single use. Transitively: `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux` -> `coeff_taylor` and everything downstream of Taylor's theorem.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 92–99, proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). Companion of `hasseCoeff_single_add_eq` (l.82), which does the same i-th-variable splitting for the binomial factor `hasseCoeff`; both are used together in the same `rw` chain at l.117 after `eval_monomial`. Name lives in namespace `MvPolynomial` though the statement is purely about `Finsupp.prod` in a commutative monoid (irrelevant while private).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.prod_pow_single_add_tsub.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.prod_pow_single_add_tsub.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) (a : R) {i : σ} (n : ℕ) {k : σ →₀ ℕ}, k i = 0 → (∀ d, (taylor α (monomial k a)).coeff d = eval α (hasseDeriv d (monomial k a))) → ∀ (d : σ →₀ ℕ), (taylor α (monomial (Finsupp.single i n + k) a)).coeff d = eval α (hasseDeriv d (monomial (Finsupp.single i n + k) a))`
- **What**: Inductive step of multivariate Taylor's theorem on monomials: if the coefficient formula coeff_d(P(X+α)) = (Δ_d P)(α) holds for the monomial a·X^k, and k does not involve variable i, then it holds for a·X_i^n·X^k.
- **How**: Writes `(X i + C (α i))^n` as `Polynomial.aeval (X i) ((Polynomial.X + Polynomial.C (α i))^n)` (`hXC`, by `simp`); shows every exponent in the support of `taylor α (monomial k a)` has i-th entry 0 (`hsupp`, via `ih`, `hasseDeriv_monomial` and `hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff`); then one `rw` chain: mathlib `monomial_single_add`, `map_mul`, `map_pow`, `taylor_X`, `hXC`, `coeff_aeval_X_mul i _ _ hsupp`, `ih`, mathlib `Polynomial.coeff_X_add_C_pow` (one-variable binomial expansion), back with `← monomial_single_add`, `hasseDeriv_monomial` (twice), `eval_monomial` (twice), `hasseCoeff_single_add_eq hi`, `prod_pow_single_add_tsub α hi`; closes with `simp [mul_assoc, mul_comm, mul_left_comm]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hi : k i = 0` (k does not involve variable i, as supplied by `Finsupp.induction`'s `single_add` case); `ih` is the induction hypothesis for `monomial k a`, for all d. `[CommSemiring R]` from the section variable (needed by `taylor`, `hasseDeriv`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_X`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_monomial`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean), `MvPolynomial.coeff_aeval_X_mul` (private), `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_single_add_eq` (private), `MvPolynomial.prod_pow_single_add_tsub` (private); implicitly `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff` via `hasseDeriv_monomial`.
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean: `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux` (private, l.124; `exact coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux α a n (Finsupp.notMem_support_iff.1 hi) ih d` at l.129) only. `grep -rn coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux Roth/` gives the definition (l.101) and that single use. Transitively: `coeff_taylor` and everything downstream of Taylor's theorem (`degreeOf_taylor_le`, `eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`, `eval_eq_sum_box`, ...).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 101–118, proof length 13 lines
- **Notes**: Declared with `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). Name ends in `_aux`. It was split out of `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux` so the induction there stays short; it ties together the three private helpers above it.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux.md)_

#### `lemma MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_aux`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) (d k : σ →₀ ℕ) (a : R), (taylor α (monomial k a)).coeff d = eval α (hasseDeriv d (monomial k a))`
- **What**: Multivariate Taylor's theorem (coefficient form) for a single monomial: the coefficient at d of (a·X^k)(X + α) is the value at α of the Hasse derivative Δ_d(a·X^k).
- **How**: `classical`; induction on `k` via mathlib's `Finsupp.induction`, generalizing `d`. Zero case: split `d = 0` (closed by `simp`) or `d ≠ 0` (closed by `simp [← C_apply, hasseDeriv_C hd, hd.symm]`, using the project lemma `hasseDeriv_C` and `taylor_C` as a simp lemma). `single_add i n k hi _ ih` case: `exact coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux α a n (Finsupp.notMem_support_iff.1 hi) ih d`.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond `[CommSemiring R]` from the section variable (needed by `taylor`, `hasseDeriv`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_C` (simp), `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_C` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:126), `MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux` (private, Roth/Taylor.lean:101); transitively `coeff_aeval_X_mul`, `hasseCoeff_single_add_eq`, `prod_pow_single_add_tsub`, `hasseDeriv_monomial`.
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:138 `coeff_taylor` (monomial case of `MvPolynomial.induction_on'`) only; `grep -rn coeff_taylor_monomial_aux Roth/` gives just the definition (l.120) and that use. Transitively everything downstream of `coeff_taylor` (`degreeOf_taylor_le` l.173, `eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv` l.202, Roth/Index.lean:64,153,158, ...).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 120–129, proof length 8 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). Name ends in `_aux`. Mirrors the source's "it suffices to prove the theorem for monomials" step (§4, Theorem on Taylor's expansion).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor_monomial_aux.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor`
- **Type**: `{σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R) : (taylor α P).coeff d = eval α (hasseDeriv d P)`
- **What**: Taylor's theorem for multivariate polynomials, coefficient form: the coefficient of `X^d` in the translate `P(X + α)` is the value at `α` of the `d`-th Hasse derivative `Δ_d P`.
- **How**: `MvPolynomial.induction_on'`; the `add` case is `simp` with linearity; the `monomial` case is the private `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux`, which runs `Finsupp.induction` on the exponent and, in the `single_add` step (`coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux`), splits `(X i + C (α i))^n` as `Polynomial.aeval (X i)` of a univariate binomial, using the private `coeff_aeval_X_mul`, mathlib's `Polynomial.coeff_X_add_C_pow`, `hasseDeriv_monomial`, the private `hasseCoeff_single_add_eq` and `prod_pow_single_add_tsub`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, private `coeff_taylor_monomial_aux` (which uses private `coeff_taylor_monomial_single_add_aux`, `coeff_aeval_X_mul`, `hasseCoeff_single_add_eq`, `prod_pow_single_add_tsub`, and public `taylor_X`, `hasseDeriv_monomial`, `hasseDeriv_C`, `hasseCoeff`, `hasseCoeff_eq_zero_iff`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:173 (`degreeOf_taylor_le`), Roth/Taylor.lean:202 (`eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`), Roth/Index.lean:64 (private `mem_support_taylor_iff`), Roth/Index.lean:153 and :158 (`index_eq_weightedOrder_div`)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 131–138 (docstring 131–133), proof length 4 lines (plus ~60 lines of private helpers at 63–128 dedicated to it)
- **Notes**: listed under `## Main results` of Roth/Taylor.lean; the private helpers exist only for this theorem.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.eval_taylor`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.eval_taylor`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α x : σ → R) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (eval x) ((taylor α) P) = (eval fun j ↦ x j + α j) P`
- **What**: Evaluating the translate `P(X + α)` at the point `x` equals evaluating `P` at the shifted point `x + α`.
- **How**: one `simp` call: unfold `taylor` to `aeval (fun j ↦ X j + C (α j))`, rewrite `aeval_def` and right-to-left with mathlib's `MvPolynomial.eval_assoc` (`eval (eval g ∘ f) p = eval g (eval₂ C f p)`), then `Function.comp_def` and `eval_X`/`eval_C`/`eval_add` close it.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (forced by `MvPolynomial`); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: unused in project (`grep -rn eval_taylor Roth/` finds only the declaration; `git log -S eval_taylor` shows it present since the initial autoformalisation commit 3cab03b, never consumed).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 141–143 (docstring 140), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Name follows mathlib's `eval x (f y)` → `eval_f` convention for `MvPolynomial` (univariate mathlib uses `taylor_eval` because of `(taylor r f).eval s` dot notation). Not tagged `@[simp]`; neither is `Polynomial.taylor_eval`.

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_taylor`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_taylor`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α β : σ → R) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (taylor α) ((taylor β) P) = (taylor fun j ↦ α j + β j) P`
- **What**: Translating first by `β` and then by `α` is translating by `α + β`: `P(X + β)` evaluated at `X + α` is `P(X + α + β)`.
- **How**: one `simp` call: unfold `taylor` to `aeval (fun j ↦ X j + C (α j))`, push the outer `aeval` inside with mathlib's `MvPolynomial.comp_aeval_apply` (`φ (aeval f p) = aeval (fun i ↦ φ (f i)) p`), then `aeval_X`, `map_add`, `algHom_C`, `C_add` and `add_assoc` identify the substitutions.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable, forced by `MvPolynomial`); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:184 (`taylor_neg_taylor`, via `simp [taylor_taylor]`), which in turn feeds `taylor_injective` and `taylor_eq_zero_iff` (Roth/Taylor.lean) and through them Roth/Index.lean. No other direct uses (`grep -rn taylor_taylor Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 146–148 (docstring 145), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Name identical to mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.taylor_taylor`. RHS uses the lambda `fun j ↦ α j + β j` rather than Pi addition `α + β` (definitionally equal; evidence item 4 proves the Pi form by the term `taylor_taylor α β P`). Not `@[simp]`; neither is the univariate one.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_taylor.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_taylor.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_zero`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_zero`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (taylor fun _ ↦ (0 : R)) P = P`
- **What**: Translation by the zero vector is the identity: `P(X + 0) = P`.
- **How**: `simp [taylor, aeval_X_left_apply]` — unfolds `taylor` to `aeval (fun j ↦ X j + C 0)`, simp normalises `C 0 = 0`, `X j + 0 = X j`, then mathlib's `MvPolynomial.aeval_X_left_apply : aeval X p = p` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Eval.lean:604) closes it.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (section variable); none else.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: no use by name in `Roth/` (`grep -rn taylor_zero Roth/` finds only the declaration). It is `@[simp]` and fires implicitly in `MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor` (Roth/Taylor.lean:184, `simp [taylor_taylor]`): evidence item 4 shows that `simp [taylor_taylor, -taylor_zero]` leaves `taylor (fun j ↦ 0) P = P` unsolved.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 150–153, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Mirrors mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.taylor_zero : taylor 0 f = f` (Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Taylor.lean:55) and `taylor_zero'`, but states the translation vector as `fun _ ↦ 0` rather than the Pi zero `0`; as a simp lemma it then does not fire on `taylor 0 P` (evidence item 1).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_zero.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_zero.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_map`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_map`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {S : Type*} [CommSemiring S] (f : R →+* S) (α : σ → R) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (taylor fun j ↦ f (α j)) (map f P) = map f ((taylor α) P)`
- **What**: Translation commutes with change of coefficient ring: applying `f` to the coefficients of `P(X + α)` gives `(f P)(X + f(α))`.
- **How**: one `simp` call: unfold `taylor` to `aeval`, `aeval_def` to `eval₂`, then mathlib's `MvPolynomial.map_eval₂` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Eval.lean) and `Function.comp_def` identify the two substitutions.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]`, `[CommSemiring S]` (forced by `MvPolynomial`); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: unused in project (`grep -rn taylor_map Roth/` finds only the declaration at Roth/Taylor.lean:157).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 155–159 (docstring 155–156), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Orientation is opposite to mathlib's univariate `Polynomial.map_taylor : (p.taylor r).map f = (p.map f).taylor (f r)` (`@[simp]`, Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/Taylor.lean:120). The proof is import-fragile: with `Mathlib.Algebra.MvPolynomial.Monad` imported, simp normalises `aeval` to `bind₁` and the current proof fails (evidence item 1); `simp [taylor, map_bind₁]` works (item 1b).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_map.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_map.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_rename`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_rename`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] {τ : Type*} (f : σ → τ) (α : τ → R) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), (taylor α) ((rename f) P) = (rename f) ((taylor (α ∘ f)) P)`
- **What**: Translating a renamed polynomial by `α` is the same as renaming the polynomial translated by the pulled-back point `α ∘ f`: `(f_* P)(X + α) = f_*(P(X + α∘f))`.
- **How**: one `simp` call: unfold `taylor` to `aeval`, rewrite `rename` as `aeval (X ∘ f)` via mathlib's `MvPolynomial.rename_eq_aeval` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Rename.lean), then `MvPolynomial.comp_aeval_apply` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Eval.lean) composes the two substitutions.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` (forced by `MvPolynomial`); `f : σ → τ` arbitrary (no injectivity); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:216 (`index_rename`, `rw [index, index, taylor_rename, support_rename_of_injective hf, ...]`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 161–165 (docstring 161–163), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Proof is import-fragile: with `Mathlib.Algebra.MvPolynomial.Monad` imported, simp normalises `aeval` to `bind₁` and the current proof fails (evidence item 1); `simp [taylor, bind₁_rename, rename_bind₁, Function.comp_def]` works (item 1b). Same phenomenon as recorded for `MvPolynomial.taylor_map`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_rename.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_rename.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.degreeOf_taylor_le`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.degreeOf_taylor_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (α : σ → R) (j : σ) (P : MvPolynomial σ R), degreeOf j (taylor α P) ≤ degreeOf j P`
- **What**: Translating `P ↦ P(X + α)` does not increase the degree in any single variable `X j`.
- **How**: `degreeOf_le_iff` reduces to: every `e` in the support of `taylor α P` has `e j ≤ degreeOf j P`; by contradiction, if `degreeOf j P < e j` then the project's `coeff_taylor` gives coefficient `eval α (hasseDeriv e P)`, which vanishes by the project's `hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt` (closed by `simp [mem_support_iff, ...]`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommSemiring R]` only (section variable; forced by `MvPolynomial`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt` (and implicitly `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean:229 (`index_le_card`: `((degreeOf_taylor_le α j P).trans (hd j))`). No other use (`grep -rn degreeOf_taylor_le Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 168–173 (docstring 168–169), proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Over a commutative semiring the inequality is in fact an equality (evidence item 3c); over a ring this is a two-line consequence of `taylor_neg_taylor` (item 2).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.degreeOf_taylor_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.degreeOf_taylor_le.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ A : Type*} [CommRing A] (α : σ → A) (P : MvPolynomial σ A), (taylor fun j ↦ -α j) ((taylor α) P) = P`
- **What**: Translating by `α` and then by `-α` gives back the polynomial: `P(X + α)(X - α) = P(X)`. So translation by `-α` is a left inverse of translation by `α`.
- **How**: one `simp [taylor_taylor]`: the project lemma `taylor_taylor` merges the two translations into translation by `fun j ↦ -α j + α j`, simp turns that into `fun _ ↦ 0` with `neg_add_cancel`, and the simp lemma `taylor_zero` finishes.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommRing A]` (section variable; negation is needed); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_zero` (simp lemma, used implicitly)
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:188 (`taylor_injective`, via `Function.LeftInverse.injective`), Roth/Taylor.lean:208 (`eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`, first `calc` step). Indirect: `taylor_eq_zero_iff` (through `taylor_injective`, Roth/Taylor.lean), `eval_eq_sum_box` (Roth/Taylor.lean) and so Roth/Main.lean:390. No direct uses outside Roth/Taylor.lean (`grep -rn taylor_neg_taylor Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 182–184 (docstring 181), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Only the left-inverse direction is stated; the right inverse (`taylor α (taylor (-α) P) = P`, surjectivity) is absent from the project. Negation is written as the lambda `fun j ↦ -α j` rather than Pi negation `-α` (definitionally equal, evidence item 3).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_injective`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_injective`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ A : Type*} [CommRing A] (α : σ → A), Function.Injective ⇑(taylor α : MvPolynomial σ A →ₐ[A] MvPolynomial σ A)`
- **What**: Over a commutative ring, the translation `P ↦ P(X + α)` is injective: `P(X + α) = Q(X + α)` implies `P = Q`.
- **How**: one term, `Function.LeftInverse.injective (taylor_neg_taylor α)`: translation by `-α` is a left inverse (project lemma `taylor_neg_taylor`), and a map with a left inverse is injective.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommRing A]` (section variable; needed, see Phase 4); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor`
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:193 (`taylor_eq_zero_iff`, via `(taylor_injective α).eq_iff' (map_zero _)`). `taylor_eq_zero_iff` is a `@[simp]` lemma not referenced by name elsewhere (`grep -rn taylor_eq_zero_iff Roth/`). No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 186–188 (docstring 186, statement 187, proof 188), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Surjectivity is not stated in the project; the companion `taylor α P = taylor α Q ↔ P = Q` (mathlib univariate `Polynomial.taylor_inj`, simp) is also absent.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_injective.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_injective.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_eq_zero_iff`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.taylor_eq_zero_iff`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ A : Type*} [CommRing A] (α : σ → A) (P : MvPolynomial σ A), taylor α P = 0 ↔ P = 0`
- **What**: A multivariate polynomial vanishes iff its translate `P(X + α)` vanishes. Tagged `@[simp]`.
- **How**: one term, `(taylor_injective α).eq_iff' (map_zero _)`: injectivity of translation (project lemma `taylor_injective`) applied against `taylor α 0 = 0`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommRing A]` (section variable; not needed, see Phase 4); no propositional hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_injective`
- **Used by**: never referenced by name (`grep -rn taylor_eq_zero_iff Roth/` gives only its declaration). As a `@[simp]` lemma it is the natural closer for the `simp`/`simpa` calls on translated polynomials in Roth/Index.lean:80 (`exists_weight_eq_index`, support of `taylor α P` nonempty from `hP : P ≠ 0`) and Roth/Index.lean:169 (`hfin`, `weightedOrder` of `↑(taylor α R)` is not `⊤` for `R ≠ 0`); these implicit uses were not individually traced with `simp?` (to save Lean runs), so they are recorded as probable.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 190–193 (docstring 190, attribute 191, statement 192, proof 193), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Mathlib's univariate counterpart is `Polynomial.taylor_eq_zero` (name without `_iff`), and there the dependency runs the other way: `Polynomial.taylor_injective := (injective_iff_map_eq_zero' _).2 (taylor_eq_zero r)`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_eq_zero_iff.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.taylor_eq_zero_iff.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`
- **Type**: `{σ A : Type*} [CommRing A] [Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ] (α : σ → A) (r : σ → ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ A) (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) : P = ∑ d ∈ Roth.box r, C (eval α (hasseDeriv d P)) * ∏ j, (X j - C (α j)) ^ d j`
- **What**: Taylor's expansion of a multivariate polynomial about `α`: if `deg_{X_j} P ≤ r_j` for every `j`, then `P = Σ_{d ≤ r} (Δ_d P)(α) ∏_j (X_j − α_j)^{d_j}`.
- **How**: first shows `taylor α P = ∑ d ∈ box r, monomial d (eval α (hasseDeriv d P))` coefficientwise via `coeff_taylor`, `coeff_sum`, `coeff_monomial`, `Finset.sum_ite_eq'`, and (outside the box) `Roth.mem_box` plus `hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt`; then writes `P = taylor (-α) (taylor α P)` (`taylor_neg_taylor`), pushes `taylor (-α)` through the sum (`map_sum`), and rewrites each term with `taylor_monomial` and `Finsupp.prod_fintype`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommRing A]` (subtraction in `X j - C (α j)`, and `taylor_neg_taylor`), `[Fintype σ]` (the product `∏ j` and `Roth.box`), `[DecidableEq σ]` (for `Roth.box`), `hP` (degree bound per variable, so that the box contains all nonzero Taylor coefficients).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.taylor`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_neg_taylor`, `MvPolynomial.taylor_monomial`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_eq_zero_of_degreeOf_lt`, `Roth.box`, `Roth.mem_box`
- **Used by**: Roth/Taylor.lean:220 (`eval_eq_sum_box`, which is in turn used at Roth/Main.lean:390). No other project uses (`grep -rn eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 195–212 (docstring 195–196), proof length 14 lines
- **Notes**: listed under `## Main results` of Roth/Taylor.lean; formalises the Theorem "Taylor's expansion for polynomials in several variables" of the source paper §4 (clean.tex l.502).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv.md)_

#### `theorem MvPolynomial.eval_eq_sum_box`

### `theorem MvPolynomial.eval_eq_sum_box`
- **Type**: `{σ A : Type*} [CommRing A] [Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ] (α x : σ → A) (r : σ → ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ A) (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) : eval x P = ∑ d ∈ Roth.box r, eval α (hasseDeriv d P) * ∏ j, (x j - α j) ^ d j`
- **What**: Taylor's theorem in evaluated form: if `deg_{X_j} P ≤ r_j` for all `j`, then for every point `x`, `P(x) = Σ_{d ≤ r} (Δ_d P)(α) ∏_j (x_j − α_j)^{d_j}`.
- **How**: one line, `simpa using congrArg (eval x) (eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv α r P hP)`: evaluates the polynomial identity `MvPolynomial.eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv` at `x`; `simp` pushes `eval` through `map_sum`, `map_mul`, `map_prod`, `map_pow`, `map_sub`, `eval_C`, `eval_X`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[CommRing A]` (subtraction `x j - α j`), `[Fintype σ]` (`∏ j`, `Roth.box`), `[DecidableEq σ]` (`Roth.box`), `hP` (degree box contains all nonzero Taylor coefficients).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.eq_sum_box_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `Roth.box`
- **Used by**: Roth/Main.lean:390 (`abs_eval_lt_of_le_index`, `rw [eval_eq_sum_box (fun _ ↦ α) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r Q hd]`). Also named in the module docstring Roth/Taylor.lean:26. No other uses (`grep -rn eval_eq_sum_box Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 215–220 (docstring 215–216), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: listed under `## Main results` of Roth/Taylor.lean; formalises the expansion used in the source paper §7 (clean.tex l.1658).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.eval_eq_sum_box.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/MvPolynomial.eval_eq_sum_box.md)_

### `Roth/CoeffNorm.lean`

#### `def Roth.normOne`

### `def Roth.normOne`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : ℝ`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ norm `‖P‖₁ = ∑_e |a_e|` of the integer coefficients of a multivariate polynomial, as a real number.
- **How**: Direct definition `∑ d ∈ P.support, |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)|`.
- **Hypotheses**: none (no typeclass assumptions on `σ`).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean only (no other file mentions `normOne`; Theta.lean and Separation.lean import the module but use only the downstream `abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`/`abs_coeff_diag_le`/`abs_eval_le`, whose statements do not mention `normOne`). Within CoeffNorm.lean: `normOne_nonneg` 50, `normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset` 54, `normOne_monomial` 59, `normOne_zero` 66, `abs_coeff_le_normOne` 71, `normOne_add_le` 77, `normOne_neg` 86, `normOne_sum_le` 90, `normOne_mul_le` 96, `normOne_prod_le` 105, `normOne_le_card_mul` 113, `normOne_det_le` 119, `normOne_hasseDeriv_le` 148, and the proof of `abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le` (lines 265-270).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 46–47, body 1 line
- **Notes**: Listed under "Main definitions" of the module docstring. Serves purely as an intermediate quantity in the proof of Lemma 3 (the final statement bounds `|coeff|`, not `normOne`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_nonneg`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_nonneg`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : 0 ≤ Roth.normOne P`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm `‖P‖₁ = ∑ |a_e|` of an integer polynomial is nonnegative.
- **How**: Term proof `Finset.sum_nonneg fun _ _ ↦ abs_nonneg _` (each summand of the support sum is an absolute value).

- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} {P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ} {s : Finset (σ →₀ ℕ)} (hs : P.support ⊆ s) : Roth.normOne P = ∑ d ∈ s, |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)|`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm `‖P‖₁ = ∑_{d ∈ supp P} |a_d|` may be computed as a sum over any finite set of exponents `s` containing the support.
- **How**: Term proof `Finset.sum_subset hs fun d _ hd ↦ by simp [notMem_support_iff.1 hd]` (the extra summands vanish because their coefficients are zero).
- **Hypotheses**: `hs : P.support ⊆ s`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:61 (`normOne_monomial`, with `support_monomial_subset`), Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:79, 80, 81 (`normOne_add_le`, three rewrites to put `P + Q`, `P`, `Q` over `P.support ∪ Q.support`). No uses outside CoeffNorm.lean (`grep -rn normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 52–56, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: none

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_monomial`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_monomial`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (e : σ →₀ ℕ) (c : ℤ) : Roth.normOne (MvPolynomial.monomial e c) = |(c : ℝ)|`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm of the monomial `c X^e` is `|c|`.
- **How**: `classical`; rewrite with `normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset support_monomial_subset` to sum over `{e}`, then `simp` (`Finset.sum_singleton`, `coeff_monomial`).
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:102 (`normOne_mul_le`, as a simp lemma for the product of two monomials), Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:108 (`normOne_prod_le`, `normOne_monomial 0 1` gives `normOne 1 ≤ 1`), Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:155 (`normOne_hasseDeriv_le`, rewriting each Hasse-derivative monomial term). No uses outside CoeffNorm.lean (`grep -rn normOne_monomial Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_zero`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_zero`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} : Roth.normOne (0 : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) = 0`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm of the zero polynomial is `0`.
- **How**: `simp [normOne]` — unfolds the def to a sum over `(0 : MvPolynomial σ ℤ).support`, which `MvPolynomial.support_zero` rewrites to `∅`, then `Finset.sum_empty`.
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:92 (`normOne_sum_le`, as `normOne_zero.le` for `Finset.le_sum_of_subadditive`); also tagged `@[simp]`, so available to any `simp` call in CoeffNorm.lean on `normOne 0`. No uses outside CoeffNorm.lean (`grep -rn normOne_zero Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 64–67, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: `@[simp]`-tagged.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_zero.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_zero.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_le_normOne`

### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_le_normOne`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ Roth.normOne P`
- **What**: Each coefficient of an integer polynomial is bounded in absolute value by the ℓ¹ coefficient norm (`|a_d| ≤ ∑_e |a_e|`).
- **How**: One term: `Finsupp.single_eval_le_sum P.coeff (g := fun z ↦ |(z : ℝ)|) (by simp) (fun _ ↦ abs_nonneg _) d` — a single summand of a sum of nonnegative terms is at most the sum (`Finsupp.single_eval_le_sum`, Mathlib/Data/Finsupp/Order.lean:165), with `g 0 = 0` by `simp` and nonnegativity by `abs_nonneg`; the RHS `Finsupp.sum` is definitionally `Roth.normOne P`.
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:266 (first step of the `calc` in `Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`, Lemma 3). No other uses project-wide (`grep -rn abs_coeff_le_normOne Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 69–73, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Explicitly requested by the source's Lean note (clean.tex:494, "Prove abc(f) ≤ ‖f‖₁").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_le_normOne.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_le_normOne.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_add_le`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_add_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P Q : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : Roth.normOne (P + Q) ≤ Roth.normOne P + Roth.normOne Q`
- **What**: Triangle inequality for the ℓ¹ coefficient norm: `∑ |a_e + b_e| ≤ ∑ |a_e| + ∑ |b_e|`.
- **How**: Rewrites all three norms as sums over the common finite set `P.support ∪ Q.support` with `Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset` (using mathlib's `MvPolynomial.support_add`, `Finset.subset_union_left/right`), merges with `Finset.sum_add_distrib`, and concludes termwise via `Finset.sum_le_sum` and `abs_add_le` (after `simp` turns `coeff_add` and the `Int.cast_add`).
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_eq_sum_of_support_subset`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:92 (`Roth.normOne_sum_le`, as the subadditivity argument of `Finset.le_sum_of_subadditive`). No other uses project-wide (`grep -rn normOne_add_le Roth/`); indirectly feeds `normOne_mul_le`, `normOne_det_le`, `normOne_hasseDeriv_le` and Lemma 3 through `normOne_sum_le`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 75–83, proof length 7 lines
- **Notes**: Explicitly requested by the source (clean.tex:450 "The triangle inequality gives …", clean.tex:494 Lean note "Prove … the triangle inequality").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_add_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_add_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_neg`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_neg`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : Roth.normOne (-P) = Roth.normOne P`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm is invariant under negation, `‖-P‖₁ = ‖P‖₁`.
- **How**: `simp [normOne]` — unfolds the def to a sum over the support, then `MvPolynomial.support_neg`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_neg`, `Int.cast_neg` and `abs_neg` close it.
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:127 (`normOne_det_le`, in `simpa [h, normOne_neg]` to absorb the sign `-1` of an odd permutation). No uses outside CoeffNorm.lean (`grep -rn normOne_neg Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 85–87, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: not `@[simp]`-tagged in the project (it is a natural simp lemma; mathlib form should carry `@[simp]`).

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_sum_le`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_sum_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} {ι : Type*} (s : Finset ι) (f : ι → MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : Roth.normOne (∑ i ∈ s, f i) ≤ ∑ i ∈ s, Roth.normOne (f i)`
- **What**: Triangle inequality for the ℓ¹ coefficient norm over a finite sum: `‖∑ᵢ fᵢ‖₁ ≤ ∑ᵢ ‖fᵢ‖₁`.
- **How**: One term: `Finset.le_sum_of_subadditive normOne normOne_zero.le normOne_add_le s f` (mathlib's generic subadditive-to-finite-sum lemma fed with the project's `normOne_zero` and `normOne_add_le`).
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_zero`, `Roth.normOne_add_le`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:99 and :101 (`Roth.normOne_mul_le`, twice), Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:123 (`Roth.normOne_det_le`), Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:152 (`Roth.normOne_hasseDeriv_le`). No uses outside CoeffNorm.lean (`grep -rn normOne_sum_le Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 89–92, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Used by the source's determinant estimate (clean.tex:477–482, `‖det(…)‖₁ ≤ ∑_σ ‖∏_ν …‖₁`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_sum_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_sum_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_mul_le`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_mul_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P Q : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : Roth.normOne (P * Q) ≤ Roth.normOne P * Roth.normOne Q`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm (length) of integer multivariate polynomials is submultiplicative: `‖PQ‖₁ ≤ ‖P‖₁ ‖Q‖₁`.
- **How**: Expand `P * Q` with `MvPolynomial.mul_def` as a double `Finsupp.sum` of monomials `monomial (a + b) (P_a Q_b)`; apply `Roth.normOne_sum_le` to the outer sum, rewrite the RHS by `Finset.sum_mul_sum`, then termwise apply `Roth.normOne_sum_le` again to the inner sum and close each term by `Roth.normOne_monomial` and `abs_mul` (via `Finset.sum_le_sum`, `Finset.sum_congr`).
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_sum_le`, `Roth.normOne_monomial`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:108 (`Roth.normOne_prod_le`, passed to `Finset.le_prod_of_submultiplicative_of_nonneg`); mentioned in the module docstring (line 27). No uses outside CoeffNorm.lean (`grep -rn normOne_mul_le Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 94–102, proof length 6 lines
- **Notes**: Listed under "Main results" of the module docstring. Source: clean.tex:495 (Lean note: "submultiplicativity for ‖·‖₁, then use these for the determinant").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_mul_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_mul_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_prod_le`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_prod_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} {ι : Type*} (s : Finset ι) (f : ι → MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : Roth.normOne (∏ i ∈ s, f i) ≤ ∏ i ∈ s, Roth.normOne (f i)`
- **What**: The ℓ¹ coefficient norm of a finite product of integer multivariate polynomials is at most the product of their ℓ¹ norms.
- **How**: Term-mode application of `Finset.le_prod_of_submultiplicative_of_nonneg` with `f := normOne`, nonnegativity `Roth.normOne_nonneg`, `normOne 1 ≤ 1` from `Roth.normOne_monomial 0 1` (via `simpa`), and submultiplicativity `Roth.normOne_mul_le`.
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_nonneg`, `Roth.normOne_monomial`, `Roth.normOne_mul_le`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:127 (`Roth.normOne_det_le`, bounding each Leibniz term `∏ i, M (τ i) i`). No other uses (`grep -rn normOne_prod_le Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 103–108, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Not listed in the module docstring's main results (only `normOne_mul_le`, `normOne_det_le`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_prod_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_prod_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_le_card_mul`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_le_card_mul`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {B : ℝ} (hB : ∀ d, |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ B) : Roth.normOne P ≤ P.support.card * B`
- **What**: If every coefficient of `P` has absolute value at most `B`, then the ℓ¹ coefficient norm is at most (number of monomials) · `B`.
- **How**: One term: `simpa [normOne] using P.support.sum_le_card_nsmul _ _ (fun d _ ↦ hB d)` (mathlib's `Finset.sum_le_card_nsmul`, after unfolding `normOne`; `simp` turns `nsmul` into `*`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hB` — uniform bound on all coefficients (only used on the support).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:270 (proof of `Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`, Lemma 3). No other uses (`grep -rn normOne_le_card_mul Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 110–115, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Formalises the source step "Pad the monomial expansion of `R` … `R = ∑_{i=1}^N α_i`, `|u_i| ≤ B`" (clean.tex:454–456), giving `‖R‖₁ ≤ N B`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_le_card_mul.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_le_card_mul.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_det_le`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_det_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} {l : ℕ} (M : Matrix (Fin l) (Fin l) (MvPolynomial σ ℤ)) {C : ℝ} (hM : ∀ μ ν, Roth.normOne (M μ ν) ≤ C) : Roth.normOne M.det ≤ l.factorial * C ^ l`
- **What**: If every entry of an `l × l` matrix of integer multivariate polynomials has ℓ¹ coefficient norm at most `C`, then its determinant has ℓ¹ norm at most `l! C^l`.
- **How**: Expands `Matrix.det_apply'` (Leibniz formula with `ε σ = ((sign σ : ℤ) : R)`), bounds the sum by `Roth.normOne_sum_le`, each term by case-splitting `Int.units_eq_one_or (sign τ)` and using `Roth.normOne_neg`, `Roth.normOne_prod_le` and `Finset.prod_le_prod₀` with `hM`; finally `Fintype.card_perm` counts the `l!` permutations.
- **Hypotheses**: `hM` — uniform entrywise ℓ¹ bound `C` (no sign condition needed on `C`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_sum_le`, `Roth.normOne_neg`, `Roth.normOne_prod_le`, `Roth.normOne_nonneg`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:268 (`Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`, Lemma 3); mentioned in the module docstring (CoeffNorm.lean:27). No other uses (`grep -rn normOne_det_le Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 117–128, proof length 9 lines
- **Notes**: Listed under `## Main results` of the module docstring. Stated for `Fin l` only; the proof works for any `Fintype n`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_det_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.normOne_det_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le`

### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {r : σ → ℕ} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) {B : ℝ} (hB : ∀ e, |((P.coeff e : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ B) (e : σ →₀ ℕ) : |(((hasseDeriv d P).coeff e : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ 2 ^ (∑ j, r j) * B`
- **What**: If `deg_{X_j} P ≤ r_j` for all `j` and all coefficients of the integer polynomial `P` are bounded by `B` in absolute value, then every coefficient of every Hasse derivative `Δ_d P` is bounded by `2^{r_1+⋯+r_m} B`.
- **How**: Rewrite with `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv` (`[X^e] Δ_d P = hasseCoeff d (e+d) · [X^(e+d)] P`), push casts and `abs_mul`/`Nat.abs_cast`; case on `e + d ∈ P.support`: if yes, bound the binomial factor by `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow` (box hypothesis supplied by `monomial_le_degreeOf` + `hP`) and combine with `hB` via `mul_le_mul`; if not, the coefficient is zero and the RHS is nonneg (`mul_nonneg`, `positivity`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (to form `∑ j, r j`), `hP` (degree box), `hB` (uniform coefficient bound).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow`
- **Used by**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:468 (twice, nested, in private `abs_eval_iterated_hasseDeriv_lt_aux`, feeding `Roth.abs_eval_le`); Roth/MainLemma.lean:509 (in private `abs_coeff_diag_hasseDeriv_modByMonic_le_aux`, feeding `Roth.abs_coeff_diag_le`). Not used elsewhere (`grep -rn abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 130–144, proof length 8 lines
- **Notes**: Source clean.tex:1445 ("abc(Δ_j W) ≤ 2^{r_1+⋯+r_m} B_1") and §3 proof of Lemma 3. Sibling `Roth.normOne_hasseDeriv_le` (line 149) is the ℓ¹ analogue.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.normOne_hasseDeriv_le`

### `theorem Roth.normOne_hasseDeriv_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {r : σ → ℕ} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) : normOne (hasseDeriv d P) ≤ 2 ^ (∑ j, r j) * normOne P`
- **What**: If `deg_{X_j} P ≤ r_j` for all `j`, every Hasse derivative `Δ_d P` of the integer polynomial `P` has ℓ¹ coefficient norm at most `2^{r_1+⋯+r_m} ‖P‖₁`.
- **How**: Expand `Δ_d P` with `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_apply` as `∑_{k ∈ supp P} monomial (k-d) (hasseCoeff d k · a_k)`, apply `Roth.normOne_sum_le`, unfold `normOne P` and distribute with `Finset.mul_sum`, compare termwise via `Roth.normOne_monomial`, `abs_mul`, `Nat.abs_cast`, and bound the binomial factor by `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow` (box hypothesis from `monomial_le_degreeOf` + `hP`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (to form `∑ j, r j`), `hP` (partial degree box).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.normOne_sum_le`, `Roth.normOne_monomial`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_apply`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff`, `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff_le_two_pow`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:269 (proof of `Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`, Lemma 3). No other use (`grep -rn normOne_hasseDeriv_le Roth/`; line 29 is the module docstring).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 145–157, proof length 8 lines
- **Notes**: Source clean.tex:450–461 (§3 proof of Lemma 3, "An individual Hasse derivative of such a term is zero or a monomial whose coefficient has absolute value at most B2^S"). ℓ¹ sibling of `Roth.abs_coeff_hasseDeriv_le` (line 134).

#### `theorem Roth.diag_eq_sum`

### `theorem Roth.diag_eq_sum`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : diag P = ∑ k ∈ P.support, Polynomial.monomial k.degree (P.coeff k)`
- **What**: The diagonal substitution `P(x, …, x)` sends each monomial `a_k X^k` to `a_k x^{|k|}`, so `diag P = ∑_k a_k x^{|k|}`.
- **How**: `rw [diag, aeval_def, eval₂_eq]`, then `simp_rw` with `Finset.prod_pow_eq_pow_sum`, `← Finsupp.degree_apply`, `Polynomial.algebraMap_eq`, `Polynomial.C_mul_X_pow_eq_monomial`.
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.diag`
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:176 (`abs_coeff_diag_le`), Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:185 (`natDegree_diag_le`); no uses outside CoeffNorm.lean.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 163–168, proof 3 lines
- **Notes**: Stated over `ℤ` only; nothing about `ℤ` is used (evidence: same proof over any `CommSemiring R`).

#### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_diag_le`

### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_diag_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {B : ℝ} (hB : ∀ d, |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ B) (n : ℕ) : |(((diag P).coeff n : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ P.support.card * B`
- **What**: Every coefficient of the diagonal specialisation `P(x, …, x)` is bounded in absolute value by the number of terms of `P` times a uniform bound `B` for the coefficients of `P`.
- **How**: Rewrites with `diag_eq_sum` and `Polynomial.finsetSum_coeff`, pushes the cast through with `Int.cast_sum`, then `Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs` followed by `Finset.sum_le_card_nsmul`; each summand is handled by `Polynomial.coeff_monomial` and `split_ifs <;> grind [abs_nonneg]` (the zero branch uses `0 ≤ |a| ≤ B`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hB` (uniform coefficient bound). No finiteness of `σ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.diag`, `Roth.diag_eq_sum`
- **Used by**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:509 (`abs_coeff_diag_hasseDeriv_modByMonic_le_aux`, private, applied to `hasseDeriv j P` with `B := 2^{∑ r_k} B`); also listed in the CoeffNorm.lean module docstring (line 32).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 170–179 (docstring 170–173, statement 174–175), proof length 4 lines
- **Notes**: Source: §6, proof of Lemma 9 (clean.tex l.1449). The bound with `#support` is crude; the per-degree count `#{k ∈ supp P : |k| = n}` is the natural sharp form (evidence (2)).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_diag_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_diag_le.md)_

### `theorem Roth.natDegree_diag_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {r : σ → ℕ} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) : (diag P).natDegree ≤ ∑ j, r j`
- **What**: If `P` has degree at most `r_j` in each variable `x_j`, the diagonal specialisation `P(x, …, x)` has degree at most `∑ r_j`.

#### `theorem Roth.eval_diag`

### `theorem Roth.eval_diag`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) (x : ℝ) : Polynomial.eval x (Polynomial.map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) (diag P)) = eval (fun _ ↦ x) (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) P)`
- **What**: Evaluating the diagonal polynomial `P(x, …, x)` (pushed to `ℝ[x]`) at a real `x` equals evaluating `P` (pushed to real coefficients) at the diagonal point `(x, …, x)`.
- **How**: `induction P using MvPolynomial.induction_on <;> simp_all [diag]` (constants, sums, multiplication by `X_i`; all closed by simp).
- **Hypotheses**: none.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.diag`

#### `theorem Roth.diag_sub`

### `theorem Roth.diag_sub`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (P Q : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) : diag (P - Q) = diag P - diag Q`

#### `theorem Roth.geom_sum_le_one_add_pow`

### `theorem Roth.geom_sum_le_one_add_pow`
- **Type**: `∀ {t : ℝ}, 0 ≤ t → ∀ (r : ℕ), ∑ s ∈ Finset.range (r + 1), t ^ s ≤ (1 + t) ^ r`
- **What**: For real `t ≥ 0` and `r ∈ ℕ`, the truncated geometric sum `1 + t + ⋯ + t^r` is at most `(1 + t)^r`. This is the step in clean.tex §6, proof of Lemma 9 (l.1546, l.1567) that turns a monomial count into `(1 + |α|)^r`.
- **How**: Rewrites `(t + 1)^r` with the binomial theorem `add_pow`, compares term by term with `Finset.sum_le_sum`, and bounds each term using `le_mul_of_one_le_right`, `pow_nonneg` and `Nat.choose_pos` (with `Finset.mem_range_succ_iff`) cast to ℝ, i.e. `1 ≤ C(r, m)`.
- **Hypotheses**: `ht : 0 ≤ t`. This is needed: `t = -1/2`, `r = 2` gives `3/4 ≤ 1/4` (counterexample compiled in the evidence file).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: `Roth.sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux` (private, Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:218), which feeds `Roth.abs_eval_le`. `grep -rn geom_sum_le_one_add_pow Roth/` finds only l.205 (declaration) and l.218.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 202–211 (docstring 202–204, statement 205–206, proof 207–211), proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: Stated over ℝ only, though nothing in the proof is specific to ℝ.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.geom_sum_le_one_add_pow.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.geom_sum_le_one_add_pow.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux`

### `theorem Roth.sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (r : σ → ℕ) {B : ℝ}, 0 ≤ B → ∀ (x : σ → ℝ), ∑ k ∈ box r, B * ∏ j, |x j| ^ k j ≤ B * ∏ j, (1 + |x j|) ^ r j` (stated under `open scoped Classical in`, so the `DecidableEq σ` needed by `box` is the classical instance).
- **What**: Summing `B · ∏_j |x_j|^{k_j}` over all multi-indices `k` with `0 ≤ k_j ≤ r_j` gives at most `B · ∏_j (1 + |x_j|)^{r_j}`: the box sum factors as `B · ∏_j ∑_{s ≤ r_j} |x_j|^s`, and each truncated geometric sum is at most `(1 + |x_j|)^{r_j}`.
- **How**: single `simpa [← Finset.mul_sum, box, Finset.prod_univ_sum] using mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (Finset.prod_le_prod₀ (s := Finset.univ) (fun _ _ ↦ by positivity) fun j _ ↦ geom_sum_le_one_add_pow (abs_nonneg (x j)) (r j)) hB`. The `simpa` unfolds `box` (a `Finset.map` of `Fintype.piFinset fun j ↦ range (r j + 1)` along `Finsupp.equivFunOnFinite.symm`), pulls `B` out with `Finset.mul_sum`, and rewrites `∏_j ∑_{s ∈ range (r j + 1)}` into the sum over `piFinset` with mathlib's `Finset.prod_univ_sum` (Mathlib/Algebra/BigOperators/Ring/Finset.lean:157); the bound comes from `Finset.prod_le_prod₀` applied to the project lemma `geom_sum_le_one_add_pow`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (needed for `box` and the finite products); `0 ≤ B` (needed to multiply the inequality by `B`; without it the inequality flips). No redundant hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box` (Roth/Box.lean:47), `Roth.geom_sum_le_one_add_pow` (Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:204).
- **Used by**: Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:239 `Roth.abs_eval_le` (last step of its calc) only; `grep -rn sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux Roth/` returns only the definition (l.214) and that use. Transitively everything downstream of `abs_eval_le`.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 213–218 (including the `open scoped Classical in` line), proof length 4 lines
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`. Mirrors the source's step "`∏_j ∑_{s ≤ r_j} |α|^s ≤ ∏_j (1 + |α|)^{r_j}`" in §6, proof of Lemma 9 (quoted in the docstring of `abs_eval_le`). The `open scoped Classical in` is there because `box` needs `DecidableEq σ`, which the file's `variable {σ : Type*}` does not provide; `abs_eval_le` uses the `classical` tactic, which supplies the same classical instance, so the call typechecks.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.abs_eval_le`

### `theorem Roth.abs_eval_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {r : σ → ℕ} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) {B : ℝ} (hB : ∀ d, |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ B) (x : σ → ℝ) : |eval x (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) P)| ≤ B * ∏ j, (1 + |x j|) ^ r j`
- **What**: If every coefficient of the integer polynomial `P` is at most `B` in absolute value and `P` has degree at most `r_j` in `x_j`, then `|P(x)| ≤ B ∏_j (1 + |x_j|)^{r_j}` for every real point `x`.
- **How**: `eval_map` and `eval₂_eq'` expand `P(x)` as `∑_{k ∈ supp P} a_k ∏_j x_j^{k_j}`; `Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs` plus `abs_mul`/`Finset.abs_prod`/`abs_pow` and `hB` bound it by `∑_{k ∈ supp P} B ∏_j |x_j|^{k_j}`; `Finset.sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg` with `monomial_le_degreeOf` and `mem_box` enlarges the index set to `box r`; the private helper `sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux` (via `Finset.prod_univ_sum` and `geom_sum_le_one_add_pow`) finishes.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]`; `hP` (partial degree bounds); `hB` (uniform coefficient bound). Nonnegativity of `B` is derived from `hB 0`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box`, `Roth.mem_box`, `Roth.sum_box_prod_pow_le_aux` (which uses `Roth.geom_sum_le_one_add_pow`)
- **Used by**: Roth/MainLemma.lean:466 (`abs_eval_iterated_hasseDeriv_lt_aux`, private, applied to `hasseDeriv i (hasseDeriv k P)` at the diagonal point `fun _ ↦ α`); also listed in the CoeffNorm.lean module docstring (line 32).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 219–240 (docstring 219–222, statement 223–225), proof length 15 lines
- **Notes**: Source: §6, proof of Lemma 9 (clean.tex l.1546–1568). The paper's Lean note explicitly prefers this route ("absorbs the monomial sum without an additional box-cardinality factor").

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_eval_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_eval_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le`

### `theorem Roth.degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} {l : ℕ} (E : Fin l → Fin l → (σ →₀ ℕ)) (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {r : σ → ℕ} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) (j : σ) : degreeOf j (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ hasseDeriv (E μ ν) P).det ≤ l * r j`
- **What**: If `P` has degree at most `r_j` in each variable `x_j`, then the `l × l` determinant `det(Δ_{E μ ν} P)` of Hasse derivatives of `P` has degree at most `l r_j` in `x_j`.
- **How**: `Matrix.det_apply'` expands the determinant by Leibniz; `degreeOf_sum_le` plus `Finset.sup_le` reduce to one permutation; `degreeOf_C_mul_le` drops the sign; `degreeOf_prod_le` bounds the product by the sum of entry degrees; each entry is bounded by `degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le` then `hP j`; `Finset.sum_le_sum` and `simpa` give `l * r j`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hP` (partial degree bounds on `P`).
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`, `MvPolynomial.degreeOf_hasseDeriv_le`
- **Used by**: Roth/Separation.lean:555 and :558 (`exists_det_eq_mul`, degree bounds `l r_j` for `U` and `l r_p` for `V`). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 245–253 (docstring 241–244), proof length 6 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.435 (end of the proof of Lemma 2): the determinant of an `l × l` matrix with entries of degree at most `r_j` in `x_j` has degree at most `l r_j` in `x_j`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`

### `theorem Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {l : ℕ} (E : Fin l → Fin l → (σ →₀ ℕ)) (P : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) {r : σ → ℕ} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) {B : ℝ} (hB0 : 0 ≤ B) (hB : ∀ d, |((P.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ B) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : |(((Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ hasseDeriv (E μ ν) P).det.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ (∏ j, ((r j : ℝ) + 1)) ^ l * l.factorial * B ^ l * 2 ^ ((∑ j, r j) * l)`
- **What**: Roth's Lemma 3: if `P ∈ ℤ[x_σ]` has partial degrees at most `r_j` and coefficients of absolute value at most `B`, every coefficient of the `l × l` determinant `det(Δ_{E μ ν} P)` of Hasse derivatives has absolute value at most `(∏ (r_j+1))^l · l! · B^l · 2^{(∑ r_j) l}`.
- **How**: `card_le_card_box` bounds `#supp P ≤ ∏ (r_j+1)`; then a calc: `abs_coeff_le_normOne` (coefficient at most ℓ¹ norm), `normOne_det_le` (Leibniz `l! C^l`) with entry bound `C = 2^{∑ r_j} ∏(r_j+1) B` from `normOne_hasseDeriv_le` and `normOne_le_card_mul`; `ring` rearranges.
- **Hypotheses**: `hP` (partial degree bounds), `hB0` (`0 ≤ B`, superfluous: follows from `hB 0`), `hB` (sup coefficient bound), `[Fintype σ]` (finite variable set, needed for `∏`/`∑`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.normOne`, `Roth.abs_coeff_le_normOne`, `Roth.normOne_det_le`, `Roth.normOne_hasseDeriv_le`, `Roth.normOne_le_card_mul`, `Roth.card_le_card_box`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:475 (`hFcoef`, Step 1 of the Lemma 6 coefficient bound, passing `hB0` as `(abs_nonneg _).trans (hR.2.2 0)`). No other use.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 258–275 (docstring 255–257), proof length 14 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.438 (Lemma `roth:lem3`); the paper's proof explicitly bounds the stronger quantity `‖F‖₁`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le.md)_

### `Roth/GenWronskian.lean`

#### `def Roth.genWronskian`

### `def Roth.genWronskian`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} → [Field K] → {σ : Type*} → {l : ℕ} → (D : Fin l → σ →₀ ℕ) → (φ : Fin l → MvPolynomial σ K) → MvPolynomial σ K`
- **What**: The generalized Wronskian of `l` multivariate polynomials `φ_0, …, φ_{l-1}` with respect to `l` Hasse operators `Δ_{D 0}, …, Δ_{D (l-1)}`: the determinant `det (Δ_{D μ} φ_ν)_{μ, ν}`.
- **How**: One-line definition `(Matrix.of fun μ ν ↦ hasseDeriv (D μ) (φ ν)).det`, i.e. mathlib's `Matrix.det` applied to the matrix of the project's `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable). The section's `[Fintype σ]` is not included (hover confirms). No order constraint `|D μ| ≤ μ` is imposed; that constraint lives in `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean: `genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent` (l.42), `wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux` (private, l.242 statement, l.244 unfolds it), `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (l.256); Roth/Separation.lean: `det_hasseDeriv_separated` (l.355 statement, l.357 unfolds it), `exists_rat_factors_det_hasseDeriv_aux` (private, l.524 uses `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`, l.527 states `hFmap`); also mentioned in the Separation.lean module docstring (l.34)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 30–34, proof length 1 line (definition body)
- **Notes**: Hasse normalisation: it differs from the ordinary-derivative generalized Wronskian by the constant `∏_μ (D μ)!`, following the source. The literature allows arbitrary constant-coefficient operators `Δ_μ` of order `≤ μ`. Here each `Δ_μ` is a single monomial Hasse operator, but by multilinearity of `det` every general generalized Wronskian is a linear combination of these, and the Kronecker proof (`wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux`) uses exactly that expansion.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.genWronskian.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.genWronskian.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent`

### `theorem Roth.genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} {l : ℕ} (D : Fin l → σ →₀ ℕ) {φ : Fin l → MvPolynomial σ K} (h : ¬ LinearIndependent K φ) : genWronskian D φ = 0`
- **What**: If multivariate polynomials `φ_0, …, φ_{l-1}` over a field are `K`-linearly dependent, then every generalized Wronskian `det (Δ_{D μ} φ_ν)` vanishes, for any choice of Hasse multi-indices `D` (no order constraint).
- **How**: `Fintype.not_linearIndependent_iff` gives a nontrivial relation `∑ g ν • φ ν = 0`; the constant vector `C ∘ g` is a nonzero kernel vector of the matrix (apply the linear map `hasseDeriv (D μ)` to the relation, `map_smul`, `smul_eq_C_mul`), so `Matrix.exists_mulVec_eq_zero_iff` gives `det = 0`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]` (section variable); `[Fintype σ]` is omitted (`omit [Fintype σ] in`); `h : ¬ LinearIndependent K φ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.genWronskian`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv`
- **Used by**: unused in project (`grep -rn genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent Roth/` returns only the declaration).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 37–48, proof length 7 lines
- **Notes**: Same 6-line proof as `Roth.wronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent` (Roth/Wronskian.lean:65); both are instances of one abstract lemma (compiled in evidence). It is the formalisation of the source's second lemma of §2 (clean.tex l.213) and the converse companion of `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.genWronskian_eq_zero_of_not_linearIndependent.md)_

#### `def Roth.kronecker`

### `def Roth.kronecker`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} → [Field K] → {σ : Type*} → [Fintype σ] → (k : ℕ) → MvPolynomial σ K →ₐ[K] Polynomial K`
- **What**: The Kronecker substitution `x_j ↦ t^{k^{e j}}`, where `e = Fintype.equivFin σ` is the (noncanonical) chosen enumeration of the variables, bundled as a `K`-algebra map `K[x_j : j ∈ σ] → K[t]`.
- **How**: One-line definition `aeval fun j ↦ Polynomial.X ^ k ^ (Fintype.equivFin σ j : ℕ)`, i.e. mathlib's `MvPolynomial.aeval` at a specific point.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` (section variables; `Fintype` is needed only for `Fintype.equivFin`).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean only: `kronecker_monomial` (l.60–62, unfolds it), `coeff_kronecker` (l.88–93), `kronecker_eq_zero_iff` (l.106–111), `linearIndependent_kronecker` (l.117–123), private `kronHasse` (l.146), private `derivative_kronHasse_aux` (l.159, unfolds it), private `hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem` (l.190–205), `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` (l.214–219), private `wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux` (l.238–245), `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (l.260–268, via the lemmas). No uses in other files.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 51–54, proof length 1 line (definition body)

#### `def Roth.kroneckerExp`

### `def Roth.kroneckerExp`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} → [Fintype σ] → (k : ℕ) → (σ →₀ ℕ) → ℕ`
- **What**: The exponent of the image of the monomial `X^d` under the Kronecker substitution `x_j ↦ t^{k^{e j}}`: `kroneckerExp k d = ∑ j, d j * k ^ (e j)`, with `e = Fintype.equivFin σ`.
- **How**: One-line definition as a `Finset.univ` sum; no proof.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Fintype σ]` (for the `Finset.univ` sum and for `Fintype.equivFin`). The section's `[Field K]` is not a parameter (unused by this def).

#### `theorem Roth.kronecker_monomial`

### `theorem Roth.kronecker_monomial`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (k : ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) (a : K) : kronecker k (monomial d a) = Polynomial.C a * Polynomial.X ^ kroneckerExp k d`
- **What**: The Kronecker substitution `x_j ↦ t^{k^{e j}}` sends the monomial `a x^d` to `a t^{∑_j d_j k^{e j}}`.
- **How**: One `simp` call unfolding `kronecker`, `kroneckerExp`, then `MvPolynomial.aeval_monomial`, `Finsupp.prod_fintype`, `← pow_mul`, `mul_comm`, `← Finset.prod_pow_eq_pow_sum`.
- **Hypotheses**: section variables `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]`; no hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker`, `Roth.kroneckerExp`
- **Used by**: `Roth.coeff_kronecker` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:93, `simp_rw [kronecker_monomial, Polynomial.coeff_C_mul_X_pow]`). No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 59–62, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Neither `Field` nor `Fintype` is used essentially. `kroneckerExp k d` equals `Finsupp.weight (fun j ↦ k ^ equivFin σ j) d` (see the `Roth.kroneckerExp` report).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.kronecker_monomial.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.kronecker_monomial.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.kroneckerExp_injOn`

### `theorem Roth.kroneckerExp_injOn`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {k : ℕ} (hk : 1 < k) : Set.InjOn (Roth.kroneckerExp (σ := σ) k) {d | ∀ j, d j < k}`
- **What**: The Kronecker exponent `d ↦ ∑ j, d j * k ^ (equivFin σ j)` is injective on exponent vectors all of whose entries are `< k` (uniqueness of base-`k` expansions).
- **How**: Rewrites `kroneckerExp k d` as `Nat.ofDigits k (List.ofFn (d ∘ e.symm))` (local `hsum`, via `Nat.ofDigits_eq_sum_mapIdx`, `List.mapIdx_eq_ofFn`, `Fin.sum_ofFn`, `Equiv.sum_comp`), then concludes with `Nat.ofDigits_inj_of_len_eq hk`, `List.ofFn_injective`, `Function.Surjective.injective_comp_right` and `DFunLike.coe_injective`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hk : 1 < k` (needed by `Nat.ofDigits_inj_of_len_eq`; mathematically superfluous, see below); `[Fintype σ]` (for `equivFin`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kroneckerExp`
- **Used by**: `Roth.coeff_kronecker` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:97). Nothing else in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 69-82, proof length 12 lines
- **Notes**: The `1 < k` hypothesis is not needed: for `k ≤ 1` the box `{d | ∀ j, d j < k}` is a subsingleton (`k = 1`: only `d = 0`; `k = 0`: empty unless `σ` is empty). The evidence proof via `Behrend.map_injOn` has no such hypothesis.
