#### `theorem Roth.coeff_kronecker`

### `theorem Roth.coeff_kronecker`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {k : ℕ} (hk : 1 < k) {P : MvPolynomial σ K} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P < k) {d : σ →₀ ℕ} (hd : ∀ j, d j < k) : (kronecker k P).coeff (kroneckerExp k d) = P.coeff d`
- **What**: If every partial degree of `P` is `< k` and every entry of `d` is `< k`, then the coefficient of `t^{∑ d_j k^{e j}}` in the Kronecker substitution of `P` equals the coefficient of `X^d` in `P`.
- **How**: Expands `P` as a sum of monomials (`MvPolynomial.as_sum`), pushes through `map_sum` / `Polynomial.finsetSum_coeff`, rewrites each term with `Roth.kronecker_monomial` and `Polynomial.coeff_C_mul_X_pow`, then `Finset.sum_eq_single d`. Off-diagonal terms vanish by `Roth.kroneckerExp_injOn` plus `MvPolynomial.monomial_le_degreeOf`; the case `d ∉ support` uses `MvPolynomial.notMem_support_iff`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hk : 1 < k` (only passed on to `kroneckerExp_injOn`; superfluous, verified); `hP` (puts the support in the box `{e | ∀ j, e j < k}`); `hd` (puts `d` in the box); section variables `[Field K]` (not used, a CommSemiring is enough) and `[Fintype σ]` (only for `equivFin`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker`, `Roth.kroneckerExp`, `Roth.kronecker_monomial`, `Roth.kroneckerExp_injOn`
- **Used by**: `Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:111). Through that lemma it reaches `Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker` (l.121) and Lemma 1. No uses outside Roth/GenWronskian.lean.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 84-97, proof length 9 lines (89-97)
- **Notes**: `hk` can be dropped. Once `kroneckerExp_injOn` is proved from `Behrend.map_injOn`, nothing in this proof needs `1 < k`. For `k ≤ 1` the box is a subsingleton, so the claim holds anyway. Verified: `Evidence.coeff_kronecker'`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.coeff_kronecker.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.coeff_kronecker.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff`

### `theorem Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {k : ℕ} (hk : 1 < k) {P : MvPolynomial σ K} (hP : ∀ j, degreeOf j P < k) : kronecker k P = 0 ↔ P = 0`
- **What**: A multivariate polynomial whose partial degrees are all `< k` vanishes iff its Kronecker substitution `x_j ↦ t^{k^{e j}}` vanishes, i.e. the substitution is injective on the degree box (kernel form).
- **How**: Backward direction is `map_zero`. Forward: show `P.support = ∅` via `MvPolynomial.support_eq_empty` and `Finset.eq_empty_iff_forall_notMem`; for `d ∈ support`, `Roth.coeff_kronecker` (with `monomial_le_degreeOf` putting `d` in the box) gives `P.coeff d = (kronecker k P).coeff _ = 0`, contradicting `mem_support_iff`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hk : 1 < k` (only passed to `coeff_kronecker`; superfluous, verified); `hP` (support in the box; essential, e.g. `x_2 - x_1^k` maps to 0); section variables `[Field K]` (unused, CommSemiring suffices) and `[Fintype σ]` (only for `equivFin` in the weight).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker`, `Roth.coeff_kronecker`
- **Used by**: `Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:123). Through it, Lemma 1 (`exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` chain) in the same file. No uses outside Roth/GenWronskian.lean.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 102-111, proof length 5 lines (107-111)
- **Notes**: `hk` can be dropped (verified as `Evidence.kronecker_eq_zero_iff'`); then `linearIndependent_kronecker` still needs `0 < k` only via `Nat.zero_lt_of_lt hk`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker`

### `theorem Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {l : ℕ} {k : ℕ} (hk : 1 < k) {φ : Fin l → MvPolynomial σ K} (hφ : ∀ ν j, degreeOf j (φ ν) < k) (h : LinearIndependent K φ) : LinearIndependent K fun ν ↦ kronecker k (φ ν)`
- **What**: The Kronecker substitution `x_j ↦ t^{k^{e j}}` sends a linearly independent family of multivariate polynomials, all of whose partial degrees are `< k`, to a linearly independent family of univariate polynomials.
- **How**: Unfolds both sides with `Fintype.linearIndependent_iff`; given `∑ g ν • kronecker (φ ν) = 0`, it applies `Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff` to `∑ g ν • φ ν`, bounding its partial degrees by `MvPolynomial.degreeOf_sum_le`, `Finset.sup_lt_iff` and `MvPolynomial.degreeOf_C_mul_le`, and pushes `kronecker` through the sum with `map_sum`/`map_smul`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hk : 1 < k` (passed to `kronecker_eq_zero_iff`, whose `hk` is superfluous, and used as `0 < k` for `Finset.sup_lt_iff`; superfluous overall, verified); `hφ` (essential: supports in the digit box); `h` (essential); `[Field K]` (only semiring structure needed); `[Fintype σ]` (only for the weight `k ^ equivFin j`); `Fin l` index (any index type works).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker`, `Roth.kronecker_eq_zero_iff`
- **Used by**: `Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (Lemma 1) at Roth/GenWronskian.lean:263. No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 113-127, proof length 8 lines (120-127)
- **Notes**: The proof re-derives by hand (via `Fintype.linearIndependent_iff`) what mathlib's `LinearIndependent.map_injOn` gives in one call from an injectivity-on-span statement.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.derivative_aeval`

### `theorem Roth.derivative_aeval`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (γ : σ → Polynomial K) (P : MvPolynomial σ K), Polynomial.derivative (aeval γ P) = ∑ j, Polynomial.derivative (γ j) * aeval γ (pderiv j P)`
- **What**: The algebraic chain rule: for `γ = (γ_j)` univariate polynomials and `P` multivariate, `d/dt P(γ(t)) = ∑_j γ_j'(t) (∂_j P)(γ(t))`.
- **How**: `MvPolynomial.induction_on`: constants by `simp`; additivity by `simp [Finset.sum_add_distrib, mul_add]`; the `mul_X` step by `simp` with `Derivation.leibniz`/`pderiv_X` (simp lemmas), `Pi.single_apply`, `apply_ite (aeval γ)`, `Finset.mul_sum` and ring-normalising rewrites.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond section variables `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean:159 (`derivative_kronHasse_aux`, private, in a `simp [kronHasse, kronecker, derivative_aeval, pderiv_hasseDeriv, ...]` set). No other use in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 129–142 (docstring 129–132, statement 133–135), proof length 7 lines
- **Notes**: Implements the FACT in §2 proof of Lemma 1 of the source paper (`.mathlib-quality/references/clean.tex` l.285–290). `[Field K]` is a section variable; the proof only needs `CommSemiring` (evidence item 3).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.derivative_aeval.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.derivative_aeval.md)_

#### `def Roth.kronHasse`

### `def Roth.kronHasse`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (k : ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : MvPolynomial σ K → Polynomial K`
- **What**: The operator P ↦ (Δ_d P)(t, t^k, t^(k^2), ...), i.e. the Kronecker substitution of the d-th multivariate Hasse derivative of P, viewed as an element of the K[t]-module of functions MvPolynomial σ K → K[t].
- **How**: Direct definition `fun P ↦ kronecker k (hasseDeriv d P)` (composition of the project AlgHom `Roth.kronecker k` with the project linear map `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv d`); no proof.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` from the section variables (`[Fintype σ]` needed by `kronecker`, which uses `Fintype.equivFin σ`; `Field` is more than the definition needs, `CommSemiring` would do for the composition itself, but the section and parent `kronecker` fix it).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:53), `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:57).
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean only (`grep -rn kronHasse Roth/`): `kronSpan` l.151 (generators of the span, as `kronHasse k '' {d | d.degree ≤ μ}`), `derivative_kronHasse_aux` l.154,156 (statement) and l.159 (simp unfold), `derivative_mem_kronSpan` indirectly via `derivative_kronHasse_aux` at l.169, `hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem` l.196 (simp unfold), `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` l.221 (simp unfold). Transitively feeds `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (Lemma 1).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 144–146 (docstring l.144, declaration l.145–146), body 1 line
- **Notes**: `private noncomputable def`. Exists only so the K[t]-span `kronSpan` has a named generating family; the public statement `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` unfolds it away, so it does not leak into the public API.

#### `def Roth.kronSpan`

### `def Roth.kronSpan`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (k μ : ℕ) : Submodule (Polynomial K) (MvPolynomial σ K → Polynomial K)`
- **What**: The K[t]-submodule of the (pointwise) K[t]-module of functions MvPolynomial σ K → K[t] spanned by the operators P ↦ (Δ_d P)(t, t^k, t^(k^2), ...) for multi-indices d with total degree |d| ≤ μ. Membership of an operator F says F(P) = Σ_d c_d(t) · kronecker k (Δ_d P) uniformly in P, with all |d| ≤ μ.
- **How**: Direct definition `Submodule.span (Polynomial K) (kronHasse k '' {d | d.degree ≤ μ})` (mathlib `Submodule.span`, `Set.image`, `Finsupp.degree`); no proof.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` from the section variables (`[Fintype σ]` inherited from `kronHasse`/`kronecker`, which use `Fintype.equivFin σ`; the span itself only needs `CommSemiring K`, but the section and parents fix `Field`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronHasse` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:145).
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean only (`grep -rn kronSpan Roth/`): `derivative_mem_kronSpan` l.162–164 (hypothesis and conclusion; `Submodule.span_induction` on membership, `zero_mem` at l.175, `add_mem` at l.183, `span_mono` for the μ ≤ μ+1 monotonicity), `hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem` l.191 (statement), l.198 (`restrictScalars K` + `smul_mem_iff` to cancel the factor μ+1), l.201 (`change`), l.202 (calls `derivative_mem_kronSpan`). Transitively feeds `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` (l.219, via `Finsupp.mem_span_image_iff_linearCombination`, which unfolds the span into a finite combination) and hence `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (Lemma 1).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 148–151 (docstring l.148, declaration l.149–151), body 1 line
- **Notes**: `private noncomputable def`. It does not appear in any public statement: the public `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` states the conclusion explicitly as a finite sum, extracting it from span membership.

#### `lemma Roth.derivative_kronHasse_aux`

### `theorem Roth.derivative_kronHasse_aux`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (k : ℕ) (d : σ →₀ ℕ) : (fun P ↦ Polynomial.derivative (kronHasse k d P)) = ∑ j, (Polynomial.derivative (Polynomial.X ^ k ^ (Fintype.equivFin σ j : ℕ)) * Polynomial.C ((d j + 1 : ℕ) : K)) • kronHasse k (d + Finsupp.single j 1)`
- **What**: As an identity in the K[t]-module of functions MvPolynomial σ K → K[t]: differentiating (in t) the operator P ↦ (Δ_d P)(t, t^k, t^(k^2), ...) gives the K[t]-linear combination over j of (t^(k^(e j)))' · (d_j + 1) times the operator for the Hasse multi-index d + e_j. I.e. the derivative of a generator of `kronSpan k μ` lies in `kronSpan k (μ + 1)`, with explicit coefficients.
- **How**: `funext P` then a single `simp` that unfolds `kronHasse` and `kronecker` (to `aeval`), applies the project chain rule `Roth.derivative_aeval` (d/dt aeval γ P = ∑ j, (γ j)' * aeval γ (pderiv j P)), rewrites `pderiv j (hasseDeriv d P)` by the project lemma `MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv` (= (d j + 1) • hasseDeriv (d + single j 1) P), and normalises with `map_smul`, `Polynomial.smul_eq_C_mul`, `mul_assoc`, `mul_comm`, `mul_left_comm`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` from section variables; `classical` for the Finsupp/sum decidability. No hypothesis on `k` or `d`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronHasse` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:145), `Roth.kronecker` (l.53), `Roth.derivative_aeval` (l.133), `MvPolynomial.pderiv_hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:200); implicitly `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (through `kronHasse`).
- **Used by**: `grep -rn derivative_kronHasse_aux Roth/`: only `Roth.derivative_mem_kronSpan` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:169, `rw` in the `mem` case of `Submodule.span_induction`). Transitively feeds `hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem`, `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` and Lemma 1 `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 153–159, proof length 4 lines (l.156–159: `classical`, `funext P`, two-line `simp`)
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`. Stated at the level of functions (not pointwise) so it can be used with `rw` on the generator `kronHasse k d` inside the K[t]-span. The coefficient `C ((d j + 1 : ℕ) : K)` is put inside the K[t]-scalar so the result is visibly a K[t]-combination of generators.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.derivative_kronHasse_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.derivative_kronHasse_aux.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.derivative_mem_kronSpan`

### `theorem Roth.derivative_mem_kronSpan`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (k μ : ℕ) {F : MvPolynomial σ K → Polynomial K} (hF : F ∈ kronSpan k μ) : (fun P ↦ Polynomial.derivative (F P)) ∈ kronSpan k (μ + 1)`
- **What**: In the K[t]-module of functions MvPolynomial σ K → K[t], if F lies in the K[t]-span of the operators P ↦ kronecker k (Δ_d P) with |d| ≤ μ, then its pointwise t-derivative P ↦ (F P)' lies in the span of those with |d| ≤ μ + 1. This is the inductive step behind "d^μ/dt^μ h_ν is a K[t]-combination of Δ^{(i)}_μ φ_ν (γ(t)) with order ≤ μ" in §2, proof of Lemma 1.
- **How**: `Submodule.span_induction` on `hF`. Generator case: write the generator as `kronHasse k d` with `d.degree ≤ μ`, rewrite with the project lemma `derivative_kronHasse_aux`, close with `Submodule.sum_mem`, `Submodule.smul_mem`, `Submodule.subset_span` at index `d + Finsupp.single j 1` (degree bound by `map_add`, `Finsupp.degree_single`, `Nat.add_le_add_right`). Zero case: `zero_mem` after `funext`/`simp`. Add case: `Submodule.add_mem` plus `Polynomial.derivative_add`. Smul case (a • F): Leibniz `Polynomial.derivative_mul` splits into `a' • F + a • F'`; the first term lies in the span via `Submodule.span_mono` with `Set.image_mono` and `Nat.le_succ` (kronSpan k μ ≤ kronSpan k (μ+1)), the second by the induction hypothesis and `Submodule.smul_mem`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` from section variables; `classical` for the Finsupp decidability. No condition on `k`, `μ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronSpan` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:149), `Roth.kronHasse` (l.145, via the generator image set), `Roth.derivative_kronHasse_aux` (l.153); implicitly `Roth.kronecker` and `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` through `kronHasse`.
- **Used by**: `grep -rn derivative_mem_kronSpan Roth/`: only `Roth.hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:202, `convert ... using 1` in the `succ` case). Transitively feeds `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` and Lemma 1 `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 162–187, proof length 23 lines (l.165–187)
- **Notes**: The smul case re-derives the monotonicity kronSpan k μ ≤ kronSpan k (μ+1) inline via `Submodule.span_mono`; no separate monotonicity lemma exists in the project (it is used only here).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.derivative_mem_kronSpan.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.derivative_mem_kronSpan.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem`

### `theorem Roth.hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [CharZero K] (k μ : ℕ) : (fun P ↦ Polynomial.hasseDeriv μ (kronecker k P)) ∈ kronSpan k μ`
- **What**: The operator MvPolynomial σ K → K[t], P ↦ D^{(μ)}_t (P(t, t^k, …, t^{k^{p-1}})) (μ-th univariate Hasse derivative of the Kronecker substitution) lies in the K[t]-submodule of functions spanned by the operators P ↦ kronecker k (Δ_d P) with |d| ≤ μ (Δ_d the multivariate Hasse derivative). This is the "for each μ there are f^μ_i(t) and Δ^{(i)}_μ of order ≤ μ" statement of §2, proof of Lemma 1, in submodule-membership form.
- **How**: Induction on `μ`. Base: `Submodule.subset_span` at generator `d = 0` (`kronHasse k 0 = kronecker k`, by `simp [kronHasse]`, using `hasseDeriv` at 0 being the identity). Step: cancel the nonzero scalar `((μ+1 : ℕ) : K)` (needs `CharZero K`, `Nat.cast_ne_zero`) via `Submodule.smul_mem_iff` on `(kronSpan k (μ+1)).restrictScalars K`; then `convert` to the project lemma `derivative_mem_kronSpan k μ ih` and identify `(μ+1) • D^{(μ+1)} = D ∘ D^{(μ)}` pointwise from mathlib's `Polynomial.hasseDeriv_comp` (with `k = 1`, `l = μ`), `Nat.cast_smul_eq_nsmul`, `add_comm`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` (section variables), `[CharZero K]` (used to divide by μ+1). No condition on `k`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:53), `Roth.kronSpan` (l.149), `Roth.kronHasse` (l.145, in the base case), `Roth.derivative_mem_kronSpan` (l.162); implicitly `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:57) through `kronHasse`.
- **Used by**: `grep -rn hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem Roth/`: only `Roth.exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:219, unpacked by `Finsupp.mem_span_image_iff_linearCombination`). Transitively feeds Lemma 1 `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (l.254).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 190–205, proof length 14 lines (l.192–205)
- **Notes**: `CharZero K` is an artefact of the proof (dividing D∘D^{(μ)} by μ+1); mathematically the statement holds in any characteristic (Taylor-expand P(γ(t+h)) = Σ_d Δ_d P(γ(t)) (γ(t+h) − γ(t))^d and read off the h^μ coefficient; only |d| ≤ μ contribute since γ(t+h) − γ(t) is divisible by h). Not compiled; irrelevant for Roth (K = ℚ) and recorded only as a pointer for an eventual upstreaming.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum`

### `theorem Roth.exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [CharZero K] (k μ : ℕ) : ∃ (s : Finset (σ →₀ ℕ)) (c : (σ →₀ ℕ) → Polynomial K), (∀ d ∈ s, d.degree ≤ μ) ∧ ∀ P : MvPolynomial σ K, Polynomial.hasseDeriv μ (kronecker k P) = ∑ d ∈ s, c d * kronecker k (hasseDeriv d P)`
- **What**: The `μ`-th Hasse derivative of the Kronecker substitution `h(t) = P(t^{k^{e j}})` is, uniformly in `P`, a `K[t]`-linear combination of Kronecker substitutions of the multivariate Hasse derivatives `Δ_d P` with `|d| ≤ μ`.
- **How**: Uses the private membership lemma `hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem` (the operator `P ↦ D^(μ)(kronecker k P)` lies in the `K[t]`-span `kronSpan k μ` of the operators `kronHasse k d`, `|d| ≤ μ`), then unpacks span membership with `Finsupp.mem_span_image_iff_linearCombination` and reads off `s := c.support`; closes with `simpa [Finsupp.linearCombination_apply, Finsupp.sum, kronHasse]` applied pointwise (`congrFun`).
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` (section variables), `[CharZero K]` (needed by `hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem`, which divides by `μ + 1` to pass from `derivative ∘ D^(μ)` to `D^(μ+1)`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.kronecker`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean), private `Roth.hasseDeriv_kronecker_mem`, private `Roth.kronHasse` (and transitively `kronSpan`, `derivative_mem_kronSpan`, `derivative_kronHasse_aux`, `derivative_aeval`, `pderiv_hasseDeriv`).
- **Used by**: `Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:260, via `choose s c hs hc using fun μ : Fin l ↦ exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum k μ`). No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 214–220, proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: The statement is the source's "there are `r(μ)`, `f^μ_i(t)`, `Δ^{(i)}_μ` of order `≤ μ` with `d^μ/dt^μ h_ν = ∑ f^μ_i (Δ^{(i)}_μ φ_ν)(γ(t))`", with Hasse derivatives on both sides.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.det_sum_rows`

### `theorem Roth.det_sum_rows`
- **Type**: `{l : ℕ} {A : Type*} [CommRing A] {ι : Type*} (s : Fin l → Finset ι) (c : Fin l → ι → A) (N : Fin l → ι → Fin l → A) : (Matrix.of fun μ ν ↦ ∑ i ∈ s μ, c μ i * N μ i ν).det = ∑ f ∈ Fintype.piFinset s, (∏ μ, c μ (f μ)) * (Matrix.of fun μ ν ↦ N μ (f μ) ν).det`
- **What**: If row `μ` of a square matrix is the linear combination `∑_{i ∈ s μ} c μ i • N μ i`, the determinant is the sum, over choice functions `f` with `f μ ∈ s μ`, of `∏_μ c μ (f μ)` times the determinant of the matrix with rows `N μ (f μ)`.
- **How**: `MultilinearMap.map_sum_finset` for `(Matrix.detRowAlternating).toMultilinearMap` applied to the rows `fun μ i ↦ c μ i • N μ i`, then `MultilinearMap.map_smul_univ` and `smul_eq_mul` to pull out `∏ c`, then `simp only [Matrix.det, Finset.sum_fn, Pi.smul_def, smul_eq_mul]` to match `Matrix.of`.
- **Hypotheses**: none (only `[CommRing A]`, needed for `Matrix.det`).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean:245 (`wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux`, private). No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 228–236, proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**: Row/column type is `Fin l` (section variable `l`); the proof works verbatim for any `[Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]` (evidence).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.det_sum_rows.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.det_sum_rows.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux`

### `theorem Roth.wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {l : ℕ} (k : ℕ) (φ : Fin l → MvPolynomial σ K) (s : Fin l → Finset (σ →₀ ℕ)) (c : Fin l → (σ →₀ ℕ) → Polynomial K), (∀ μ ν : Fin l, Polynomial.hasseDeriv ↑μ (kronecker k (φ ν)) = ∑ d ∈ s μ, c μ d * kronecker k (hasseDeriv d (φ ν))) → wronskian (fun ν ↦ kronecker k (φ ν)) = ∑ f ∈ Fintype.piFinset s, (∏ μ, c μ (f μ)) * kronecker k (genWronskian f φ)` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: If, for every row index `μ` and every `ν`, the `μ`-th Hasse derivative of the Kronecker substitution `φ_ν(t, t^k, t^{k^2}, …)` is the `K[t]`-combination `∑_{d ∈ s μ} c μ d · kronecker k (Δ_d φ_ν)`, then the (Hasse) Wronskian of the substituted family equals `∑_f (∏_μ c μ (f μ)) · kronecker k (W(φ; Δ_{f 0}, …, Δ_{f (l-1)}))`, the sum running over choice functions `f` with `f μ ∈ s μ`. This is the "multilinearity of the determinant in each row" step of the source's proof of Lemma 1 (§2).
- **How**: `classical` then a single `simpa [wronskian, wronskianMatrix, hc, genWronskian, AlgHom.map_det, Matrix.map] using det_sum_rows s c (fun μ d ν ↦ kronecker k (hasseDeriv d (φ ν)))`. Unfolding `wronskian`/`wronskianMatrix` and rewriting each entry with `hc` puts the left side in the shape `det (of fun μ ν ↦ ∑_{d ∈ s μ} c μ d * N μ d ν)`, which is the left side of the project lemma `Roth.det_sum_rows`; on the right, `genWronskian` is unfolded and mathlib's `AlgHom.map_det` (with `Matrix.map`) pushes `kronecker k` inside the determinant so it matches `det (of fun μ ν ↦ N μ (f μ) ν)`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[Fintype σ]` (both from the file `variable` line; `Fintype σ` is needed because `kronecker` sends `X_j ↦ t^{k^{equivFin j}}`). `hc` is the essential hypothesis (row expansion); it is exactly what `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` produces, specialised at `φ ν`. No `CharZero` needed here. No redundant hypotheses: all of `k, φ, s, c, hc` appear in the conclusion or are used.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.wronskian` (Roth/Wronskian.lean:60), `Roth.wronskianMatrix` (Roth/Wronskian.lean:56), `Roth.kronecker` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:53), `Roth.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:57), `Roth.genWronskian` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:32), `Roth.det_sum_rows` (Roth/GenWronskian.lean:228). Mathlib: `Polynomial.hasseDeriv`, `Fintype.piFinset`, `AlgHom.map_det`, `Matrix.map`.
- **Used by**: Roth/GenWronskian.lean:268 `Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero` (Lemma 1) only; `grep -rn wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux Roth/` returns just the definition (l.238) and this use. Transitively everything downstream of Lemma 1.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 238–245, proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux` (no docstring, as is usual for private helpers). Index-type subtlety: `hc` uses `Polynomial.hasseDeriv ↑μ` with the coercion `Fin l → ℕ`, matching `wronskianMatrix`'s row convention. The caller supplies `hc` as `fun μ ν ↦ hc μ (φ ν)` from `choose s c hs hc using … exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`

### `theorem Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`
- **Type**: `{K : Type*} [Field K] {σ : Type*} {l : ℕ} [Finite σ] [CharZero K] {φ : Fin l → MvPolynomial σ K} (h : LinearIndependent K φ) : ∃ D : Fin l → (σ →₀ ℕ), (∀ μ, (D μ).degree ≤ μ) ∧ genWronskian D φ ≠ 0`
- **What**: Lemma 1 of the source (Roth's lemma): a linearly independent family of `l` multivariate polynomials over a characteristic-zero field has a generalized Wronskian `det (Δ_{D μ} φ_ν)`, with Hasse operators of total order `|D μ| ≤ μ`, that is not identically zero.
- **How**: Kronecker substitution. Take `k = 2 + Σ_ν Σ_j degreeOf j (φ ν)`, which exceeds every partial degree. For each `μ`, `exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum` writes `Polynomial.hasseDeriv μ (kronecker k P)` as `Σ_{d ∈ s μ} c μ d * kronecker k (hasseDeriv d P)` with `|d| ≤ μ`. If every admissible generalized Wronskian vanished, the private `wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux` (multilinear expansion via `det_sum_rows`) would make the univariate Wronskian of `kronecker k ∘ φ` zero. But that family is linearly independent (`linearIndependent_kronecker`), contradicting `wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Field K]`, `[CharZero K]` (needed for the univariate criterion), `[Finite σ]` (needed for the Kronecker substitution `kronecker`, which uses `Fintype.equivFin`), and `h : LinearIndependent K φ`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.genWronskian`, `Roth.exists_hasseDeriv_kronecker_eq_sum`, `Roth.wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, `Roth.linearIndependent_kronecker`, `Roth.wronskian_kronecker_eq_sum_aux` (private), `Roth.kronecker`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (via `genWronskian`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Separation.lean:524 (`exists_rat_factors_det_hasseDeriv_aux`, private, the proof of Lemma 2)
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 254–270, proof length 14 lines
- **Notes**: This is a main result of §2 of the source (`roth:lem1`, clean.tex l.224–225). The source states it over `ℚ`. The project already generalises it to any characteristic-zero field, and to any finite variable type.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.exists_genWronskian_ne_zero.md)_

### `Roth/Lattice.lean`

#### `theorem Roth.sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt`

### `theorem Roth.sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt`
- **Type**: `∀ {m : ℕ}, 2 ≤ m → ∀ {lam : ℝ}, 2 * √(m : ℝ) < lam → √(1 - 1 / (m : ℝ)) < 1 - lam⁻¹ ^ 2`
- **What**: For an integer `m ≥ 2` and real `λ > 2√m`, `√(1 - 1/m) < 1 - λ⁻²`. This is the chain `1 - λ⁻² > 1 - 1/(4m) > √(1 - 1/m)` in clean.tex §6, proof of Lemma 8 (l.1115–1117).
- **How**: Derives `4m < λ²` by `nlinarith` with `Real.sq_sqrt` and `Real.sqrt_nonneg`, then `λ⁻² < 1/(4m)` via `inv_pow`, `div_div` and `one_div_lt_one_div_of_lt`; rewrites the goal with `Real.sqrt_lt'` (positivity of the RHS from `one_div_le_one_div_of_le`) and closes the squared inequality by `nlinarith [sq_nonneg (lam⁻¹ ^ 2)]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hm : 2 ≤ m` (only `1 ≤ m` is needed, verified); `hlam : 2 * √m < lam` (essential).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: `Roth.sum_inv_lt` (Roth/Lattice.lean:87, inside a `gcongr` side goal). `grep -rn sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt Roth/` finds only l.29 and l.87.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 26–37 (docstring 26–28, statement 29–30, proof 31–37), proof length 7 lines
- **Notes**: Stated over `m : ℕ` with a cast; the real version with `1 ≤ m` holds with the same proof.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.inv_add_inv_le`

### `theorem Roth.inv_add_inv_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {lam x : ℝ}, 2 < lam → 0 ≤ x → x ≤ 2 → (lam - 1 + x)⁻¹ + (lam - 1 + (2 - x))⁻¹ ≤ 2 * lam / (lam ^ 2 - 1)`
- **What**: For real λ > 2 and 0 ≤ x ≤ 2, the sum of reciprocals 1/(λ - 1 + x) + 1/(λ + 1 - x) is at most 2λ/(λ² - 1). Writing a = λ - 1 + x and b = λ + 1 - x, one has a + b = 2λ and ab = λ² - (1 - x)² ≥ λ² - 1, so 1/a + 1/b = 2λ/(ab) ≤ 2λ/(λ² - 1); the extreme case is x ∈ {0, 2}. It is the bound on one pair (j, r - j) of terms in the harmonic-type sum of Lemma 8 (clean.tex §6), applied with x = 2j/r.
- **How**: Establishes positivity of both denominators by `linarith`, rewrites the left side with `inv_add_inv` and clears denominators with `div_le_div_iff₀` (positivity of λ² - 1 by `nlinarith`), and closes the polynomial inequality by `nlinarith [mul_nonneg hx0 (sub_nonneg.2 hx2)]`, i.e. from x(2 - x) ≥ 0.
- **Hypotheses**: `hlam : 2 < lam` (only `1 < lam` is used, verified in evidence); `hx0 : 0 ≤ x` and `hx2 : x ≤ 2` (both necessary: the statement fails at x = -1 and at x = 3 with λ = 3, verified in evidence).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: `Roth.sum_inv_le` (private, Roth/Lattice.lean:47; use at l.57 inside the `Finset.sum_le_sum` step, with x = 2j/r and 2 - x rewritten as 2(r - j)/r). `grep -rn inv_add_inv_le Roth/ Roth.lean` finds only l.40 and l.57. Transitively feeds `Roth.sum_inv_lt` (l.68) and Lemma 8.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 40–45, proof length 4 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem); no docstring. The name collides in spirit with mathlib's `inv_add_inv` (an equality), but being private in namespace `Roth` it causes no clash.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.inv_add_inv_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.inv_add_inv_le.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.sum_inv_le`

### `theorem Roth.sum_inv_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {r : ℕ}, 1 ≤ r → ∀ {lam : ℝ}, 2 < lam → ∑ j ∈ range (r + 1), (lam - 1 + 2 * (j : ℝ) / r)⁻¹ ≤ (r + 1) * (lam / (lam ^ 2 - 1))`
- **What**: For an integer r ≥ 1 and real λ > 2, the harmonic-type sum ∑_{j=0}^r 1/(λ - 1 + 2j/r) is at most (r + 1)·λ/(λ² - 1). Proof idea (source §6, Lemma 8): pair the term j with the term r - j; each pair is bounded by 2λ/(λ² - 1), so twice the sum is at most 2(r + 1)λ/(λ² - 1).
- **How**: Applies `Finset.sum_le_sum` termwise to the bound `inv_add_inv_le` (with x = 2j/r, after rewriting 2 - 2j/r as 2(r - j)/r via `Nat.add_sub_cancel`, `Nat.cast_sub`, `field_simp`; x ≤ 2 by `div_le_iff₀` + `gcongr`), giving ∑ (f j + f (r - j)) ≤ ∑ 2λ/(λ² - 1). Then rewrites with `Finset.sum_add_distrib`, `Finset.sum_range_reflect` (so the reflected sum equals the original), `Finset.sum_const`, `card_range`, `nsmul_eq_mul`, `mul_div_assoc`, normalises casts by `push_cast`, and closes with `linarith`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hr : 1 ≤ r` (necessary: at r = 0, λ = 3 the left side is 1/2 and the right side 3/8, refuted by `norm_num` in evidence); `hlam : 2 < lam` (only `1 < lam` is used, verified in evidence by recompiling with the helper weakened the same way).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.inv_add_inv_le` (private, Roth/Lattice.lean:40)
- **Used by**: `Roth.sum_inv_lt` (Roth/Lattice.lean:68, first `calc` step at l.83). `grep -rn sum_inv_le Roth/` finds only l.47 and l.83. Transitively feeds Lemma 8 (lattice count estimate).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 47–61, proof length 13 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem); no docstring (the public `sum_inv_lt` carries the source citation). `Finset.sum_flip` (`∑ r ∈ range (n+1), f (n - r) = ∑ k ∈ range (n+1), f k`) matches the indexing better than `sum_range_reflect` and would let the `r + 1 - 1 - j` rewrite be simplified to `r - j`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_inv_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_inv_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.sum_inv_lt`

### `theorem Roth.sum_inv_lt`
- **Type**: `∀ {r m : ℕ}, 1 ≤ r → 2 ≤ m → ∀ {lam : ℝ}, 2 * √(m : ℝ) < lam → ∑ j ∈ range (r + 1), (lam - 1 + 2 * (j : ℝ) / r)⁻¹ < lam⁻¹ * (√((m : ℝ) - 1))⁻¹ * √(m : ℝ) * (r + 1)`
- **What**: For integers `r ≥ 1` and `m ≥ 2` and a real `λ > 2√m`, `∑_{j=0}^r (λ - 1 + 2j/r)⁻¹ < λ⁻¹ (m-1)^{-1/2} m^{1/2} (r+1)`. This is the "statement to finish the proof" in clean.tex §6, proof of Lemma 8 (l.1100–1103).
- **How**: A three-step `calc`. Step 1 is the private pairing bound `Roth.sum_inv_le` (sum ≤ `(r+1) λ/(λ²-1)`). Step 2 rewrites `λ/(λ²-1) = λ⁻¹/(1-λ⁻²)` and uses `gcongr` with side goal `Roth.sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt` (`√(1-1/m) < 1-λ⁻²`). Step 3 turns `√(1-1/m)` into `√(m-1)/√m` with `Real.sqrt_div'`, then closes with `field_simp`. The setup uses `Real.lt_sqrt_of_sq_lt` (to get `2 < λ`) and `Real.sqrt_pos`, `div_lt_one`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hr : 1 ≤ r` (necessary; for `r = 0` the statement fails, see the compiled counterexample); `hm : 2 ≤ m` (necessary; for `m = 1` the RHS is `0`); `hlam : 2 * √m < lam` (the source's hypothesis, essential).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.sum_inv_le` (private, Roth/Lattice.lean:47), `Roth.sqrt_one_sub_inv_lt` (Roth/Lattice.lean:29).
- **Used by**: `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux` (private, Roth/Lattice.lean:173). `grep -rn sum_inv_lt Roth/` gives only l.68 and l.173.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 63–90 (docstring 63–67, statement 68–70, proof 71–90), proof length 20 lines
- **Notes**: Its only caller is private and in the same file, so the declaration could be private too. The `(√(m-1))⁻¹ * √m` form copies the source's `(m-1)^{-1/2} m^{1/2}` word for word; the caller immediately normalises it with `push_cast`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_inv_lt.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.sum_inv_lt.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.extend_apply_castSucc`

### `theorem Roth.extend_apply_castSucc`
- **Type**: `∀ {m : ℕ} (d : Fin m →₀ ℕ) (i : ℕ) (j : Fin m), (d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i) j.castSucc = d j` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: Extending a finitely supported exponent vector `d : Fin m →₀ ℕ` to `Fin (m + 1)` by pushing it forward along `Fin.castSucc` and putting `i` in the last slot does not change the old coordinates: the extended vector evaluated at `j.castSucc` is `d j`. This is the Finsupp analogue of `Fin.snoc_castSucc`.
- **How**: One `simp` call using mathlib's `Finsupp.mapDomain_apply_of_injective` instantiated at `Fin.castSucc_injective m`; the remaining `Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i (j.castSucc) = 0` is closed by simp via `Finsupp.single_apply` / `Fin.castSucc_ne_last` (simp lemmas), and `add_zero`.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the data `m, d, i, j`; all are used. The codomain `ℕ` could be any `AddZeroClass`/`AddCommMonoid` (`mapDomain` needs `AddCommMonoid`), but the lemma is private and only used at `ℕ`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib: `Finsupp.mapDomain`, `Finsupp.single`, `Fin.castSucc`, `Fin.last`, `Finsupp.mapDomain_apply_of_injective`, `Fin.castSucc_injective`).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux` only (private), at l.112 (bounding coordinates in the box, `Fin.lastCases` castSucc branch), l.115 (injectivity of the extension map in `Finset.card_bij`), l.122 (surjectivity, `hext` ext step). `grep -rn extend_apply_castSucc Roth/` returns only the definition and these three uses. Transitively feeds the public `Roth.card_filter_box_last` (l.130).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 92–94, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Companion of `Roth.extend_apply_last` (l.96). The extension expression `d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i` recurs in `card_filter_box_fiber_aux`, `card_filter_box_last` and `card_filter_fiber_eq`; it is a hand-rolled "Finsupp.snoc".

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.extend_apply_castSucc.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.extend_apply_castSucc.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.extend_apply_last`

### `theorem Roth.extend_apply_last`
- **Type**: `∀ {m : ℕ} (d : Fin m →₀ ℕ) (i : ℕ), (d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i) (Fin.last m) = i` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: Extending a finitely supported exponent vector `d : Fin m →₀ ℕ` to `Fin (m + 1)` by pushing it forward along `Fin.castSucc` and putting `i` in the last slot gives a vector whose last coordinate is `i`. This is the Finsupp analogue of `Fin.snoc_last`.
- **How**: One `simp` call with mathlib's `Finsupp.mapDomain_of_notMem_range` (Mathlib/Data/Finsupp/Basic.lean:286): `mapDomain Fin.castSucc d (Fin.last m) = 0` because `Fin.last m ∉ Set.range Fin.castSucc` (discharged by simp, via the simp lemma `Fin.range_castSucc`, Mathlib/Data/Fin/SuccPred.lean:241, reducing to `¬ (m < m)`); then `Finsupp.single_eq_same` and `zero_add`.
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the data `m, d, i`; all are used. The codomain `ℕ` could be any `AddCommMonoid` (needed by `mapDomain`), but the lemma is private and only used at `ℕ`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib: `Finsupp.mapDomain`, `Finsupp.single`, `Fin.castSucc`, `Fin.last`, `Finsupp.mapDomain_of_notMem_range`).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux` only (private), at l.110 (the extended vector lies in the fibre `d (Fin.last m) = i`, used as a term), l.111 (bounding the last coordinate in the box, `Fin.lastCases` last branch, as simp lemma), l.122 (surjectivity, `hext` ext step, as simp lemma). `grep -rn extend_apply_last Roth/` returns only the definition and these three uses. Transitively feeds the public `Roth.card_filter_box_last` (l.130).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 96–98, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Companion of `Roth.extend_apply_castSucc` (l.92). The extension expression `d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i` recurs in `card_filter_box_fiber_aux`, `card_filter_box_last` and `card_filter_fiber_eq`; it is a hand-rolled "Finsupp.snoc".

#### `lemma Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux`

### `theorem Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {m : ℕ} (r : Fin (m + 1) → ℕ) (p : (Fin (m + 1) →₀ ℕ) → Prop) [DecidablePred p] {i : ℕ}, i ≤ r (Fin.last m) → ((box (r ∘ Fin.castSucc)).filter fun d ↦ p (d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i)).card = (((box r).filter p).filter fun d ↦ d (Fin.last m) = i).card` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: For a box `box r ⊆ Fin (m+1) →₀ ℕ` and any decidable predicate `p`, the fibre of `{d ∈ box r | p d}` over last coordinate `i` (with `i ≤ r_last`) has the same cardinality as the set of `d` in the lower-dimensional box `box (r ∘ castSucc)` whose extension by `i` in the last slot satisfies `p`. Mathematically: the map `d ↦ (d, i)` is a bijection between those two sets.
- **How**: `Finset.card_bij` with the map `d ↦ d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i`. Maps-to: `mem_box` plus `Fin.lastCases`, with the last coordinate via `extend_apply_last` and the hypothesis `hi`, the others via `extend_apply_castSucc`. Injectivity: evaluate at `j.castSucc` (`congrArg`) and `extend_apply_castSucc`. Surjectivity: preimage `Finsupp.equivFunOnFinite.symm fun j ↦ d j.castSucc`, with the reconstruction identity `hext` proved by `ext` and `Fin.lastCases` using `extend_apply_last`, `extend_apply_castSucc` and the fibre condition.
- **Hypotheses**: `hi : i ≤ r (Fin.last m)` is genuinely needed (otherwise the right side is empty while the left side need not be, e.g. `p := True`). `[DecidablePred p]` is needed for `Finset.filter`. All used.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box`, `Roth.mem_box` (Roth/Box.lean:47, 51), `Roth.extend_apply_castSucc` (Roth/Lattice.lean:92), `Roth.extend_apply_last` (Roth/Lattice.lean:96).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_box_last` only (l.141, in the `Finset.sum_congr` step after `Finset.card_eq_sum_card_fiberwise`). `grep -rn card_filter_box_fiber_aux Roth/` returns only the definition and this use. Transitively feeds `card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux` (l.175) and the lattice-point counting of Roth/Lattice.lean.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 100–124, proof length 20 lines (105–124)
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`; would also be caught by the Step 9a pre-filter. The extension `d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i` is a hand-rolled "Finsupp.snoc" (see the reports for `Roth.extend_apply_castSucc` / `Roth.extend_apply_last`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.card_filter_box_last`

### `theorem Roth.card_filter_box_last`
- **Type**: `∀ {m : ℕ} (r : Fin (m + 1) → ℕ) (p : (Fin (m + 1) →₀ ℕ) → Prop) [DecidablePred p], ((box r).filter p).card = ∑ i ∈ range (r (Fin.last m) + 1), ((box (r ∘ Fin.castSucc)).filter fun d ↦ p (d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i)).card`
- **What**: For a box `∏_{j ≤ m} [0, r_j]` of multi-indices and any decidable predicate `p`, the number of points of the box satisfying `p` equals the sum over `i ∈ [0, r_last]` of the number of points `d` of the lower-dimensional box `∏_{j < m} [0, r_j]` whose extension `(d, i)` satisfies `p`. This is the discrete Fubini / double-counting identity for the last-coordinate projection.
- **How**: `Finset.card_eq_sum_card_fiberwise` with `f := fun d ↦ d (Fin.last m)` and `t := range (r (Fin.last m) + 1)` (maps-to via `mem_box`, `Nat.lt_succ_iff`), then `Finset.sum_congr` with the private fibre bijection `Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[DecidablePred p]` (needed for `Finset.filter`); no other hypotheses.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box`, `Roth.mem_box` (Roth/Box.lean:47, 51), `Roth.card_filter_box_fiber_aux` (Roth/Lattice.lean:100).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean:175 (`rw [card_filter_box_last, ...]` in private `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux`, the induction step of Lemma 8). `grep -rn card_filter_box_last Roth/` gives only l.131 and l.175.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 131–142, proof length 8 lines (135–142)
- **Notes**: The extension `d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last m) i` is a hand-rolled `Finsupp.snoc`; mathlib's `Mathlib/Data/Finsupp/Fin.lean` has only `Finsupp.cons`/`Finsupp.tail`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_box_last.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_box_last.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty`

### `theorem Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty`
- **Type**: `∀ {m : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) {lam : ℝ}, (m : ℝ) < lam → (box r).filter (fun d ↦ weight r d ≤ ((m : ℝ) - lam) / 2) = ∅` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: If `λ > m`, then no multi-index in the box `∏ [0, r_j]` has weight at most `(m - λ)/2`, because that threshold is negative while every weight `∑ d_j / r_j` is nonnegative. This is the trivial (empty) case of the Lemma 8 lattice count.
- **How**: Term proof `Finset.filter_eq_empty_iff.2 fun d _ h ↦ by linarith [weight_nonneg r d]`: for each `d`, the filter condition `h : weight r d ≤ (m - lam)/2` together with `hlam : m < lam` and `Roth.weight_nonneg r d : 0 ≤ weight r d` is contradictory by `linarith`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hlam : (m : ℝ) < lam` is used (it makes the threshold negative); it is strict, and needed: at `lam = m` the zero multi-index has weight `0 ≤ 0` and lies in the filter. Membership in `box r` (the `_` binder) is not used, so the statement holds for any `Finset (Fin m →₀ ℕ)`; `Fin m` could be any `Fintype σ` with `Fintype.card σ` in place of `m`. Neither generalisation is needed by the call sites.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box` (Roth/Box.lean:47), `Roth.weight` (Roth/Box.lean:71), `Roth.weight_nonneg` (Roth/Box.lean:75).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_weight_le` only, twice: l.208 (`zero` case of the induction on `m`, `m = 0`, from `0 < lam`) and l.214 (`succ` case with `n = 0` and `lam > 2 √1 = 2 > 1`). `grep -rn filter_weight_le_eq_empty Roth/` returns only the definition (l.143) and these two uses.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 143–145, proof length 1 line (145)
- **Notes**: none

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq`

### `theorem Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq`
- **Type**: `∀ {n : ℕ} (r : Fin (n + 1) → ℕ) (lam : ℝ) (i : ℕ), (((box (r ∘ Fin.castSucc)).filter fun d ↦ weight r (d.mapDomain Fin.castSucc + Finsupp.single (Fin.last n) i) ≤ (((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ) - lam) / 2).card : ℝ) = (((box (r ∘ Fin.castSucc)).filter fun d ↦ weight (r ∘ Fin.castSucc) d ≤ ((n : ℝ) - (lam - 1 + 2 * (i : ℝ) / r (Fin.last n))) / 2).card : ℝ)` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: In the inductive step of Lemma 8, the fibre of the filtered box over last coordinate `i` (a multi-index `d` in the first `n` coordinates, extended by `i` in the last) satisfies `weight_r(d, i) ≤ (n + 1 - λ)/2` iff `weight_{r∘castSucc}(d) ≤ (n - λ')/2` with `λ' = λ - 1 + 2i/r_{n+1}`; hence the two filtered cards (cast to ℝ) agree. This is the "reduce λ to λ' in one dimension lower" step of the paper's induction.
- **How**: `congr 2` reduces to equality of the filtered finsets; `Finset.filter_congr` reduces to pointwise equivalence of the predicates; `rw [Roth.weight_add, Roth.weight_mapDomain (Fin.castSucc_injective n), Roth.weight_single, mul_div_assoc]` turns the left weight into `weight (r ∘ Fin.castSucc) d + i / r (Fin.last n)`, and `grind` closes the resulting linear-arithmetic equivalence (including the `((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ)` cast).
- **Hypotheses**: none beyond the data `r`, `lam`, `i`. No positivity of `r (Fin.last n)` is needed (if it is `0`, both `i / 0` and `2 * i / 0` are `0` in Lean, and the identity still holds). The box-membership binder in `filter_congr` (`fun d _`) is unused, so the statement holds for any `Finset (Fin n →₀ ℕ)` in place of `box (r ∘ Fin.castSucc)`; the call site does not need this.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box` (Roth/Box.lean:47), `Roth.weight` (Roth/Box.lean:71), `Roth.weight_add` (Roth/Box.lean:85), `Roth.weight_mapDomain` (Roth/Box.lean:127), `Roth.weight_single` (Roth/Box.lean:96).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux` only (l.176, inside `Finset.sum_congr rfl fun i _ ↦ card_filter_fiber_eq r lam i` to rewrite each summand after `card_filter_box_last`). `grep -rn card_filter_fiber_eq Roth/` returns only the definition (l.147) and this use.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 147–155, proof length 4 lines (152–155)
- **Notes**: none

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt_aux`

### `theorem Roth.card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ (m : ℕ) (r : Fin m → ℕ) {lam : ℝ}, 0 < lam → lam ≤ 2 * √(m : ℝ) → (((box r).filter fun d ↦ weight r d ≤ ((m : ℝ) - lam) / 2).card : ℝ) ≤ 2 * √(m : ℝ) / lam * ∏ j, ((r j : ℝ) + 1)` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: The easy case of Lemma 8 (lattice count estimate): when `0 < λ ≤ 2√m`, the claimed bound `2√m λ⁻¹ ∏ (r_j + 1)` is at least the total number `∏ (r_j + 1)` of points of the box, so the filtered count is trivially below it.
- **How**: A three-step `calc`: filtered card ≤ `(box r).card` by `Finset.card_filter_le` (cast with `exact_mod_cast`); `(box r).card = ∏ (r j + 1)` by `simp [card_box]`; then `le_mul_of_one_le_left` with nonnegativity from `positivity` and `1 ≤ 2√m / λ` from `(one_le_div hlam).2 h`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hlam : 0 < lam` (used in `one_le_div`) and `h : lam ≤ 2 * √m` (the case condition) are both needed for `1 ≤ 2√m/λ`. The filter predicate (weight bound) is never used: the statement holds for any decidable predicate, the weight only appears to match the shape of `card_filter_weight_le`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box` (Roth/Box.lean:47), `Roth.weight` (Roth/Box.lean:71, statement only), `Roth.card_box` (Roth/Box.lean:57).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_weight_le` only (l.212, the `lam ≤ 2 * √(n+1)` branch of the `succ` case). `grep -rn card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt Roth/` returns only the definition (l.158) and this use.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 158–164, proof length 4 lines (161–164)
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`; would also be caught by the Step 9a pre-filter. `m` is explicit although determined by `r`; harmless for a private helper (the caller passes `(n + 1)`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt_aux.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux`

### `theorem Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {n : ℕ} (r : Fin (n + 1) → ℕ), 0 < r (Fin.last n) → 0 < n → ∀ {lam : ℝ}, 2 * √((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ) < lam → (∀ {t : ℝ}, 0 < t → (((box (r ∘ Fin.castSucc)).filter fun d ↦ weight (r ∘ Fin.castSucc) d ≤ ((n : ℝ) - t) / 2).card : ℝ) ≤ 2 * √(n : ℝ) / t * ∏ j, (((r ∘ Fin.castSucc) j : ℝ) + 1)) → (((box r).filter fun d ↦ weight r d ≤ (((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ) - lam) / 2).card : ℝ) ≤ 2 * √((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ) / lam * ∏ j, ((r j : ℝ) + 1)` (declared `private lemma`).
- **What**: The inductive step of Lemma 8 (lattice count estimate) in the nontrivial regime: assuming the Lemma 8 bound in dimension `n` for the truncated box `r ∘ castSucc` and all `t > 0`, it holds in dimension `n + 1` for every `lam > 2 √(n+1)`, provided `n ≥ 1` and the last side `r_last ≥ 1`. Mathematically: split the count by the value `i ∈ [0, r_last]` of the last coordinate; the fibre over `i` is the `n`-dimensional count with `lam` replaced by `lam - 1 + 2i/r_last`; apply the hypothesis to each fibre and bound the resulting harmonic-type sum `∑_i (lam - 1 + 2i/r_last)⁻¹` by `lam⁻¹ √((n+1)/n) (r_last + 1)`.
- **How**: `sum_inv_lt` (with `m := n + 1`, using `hr` and `Nat.succ_le_succ hn`), simplified by `push_cast [add_sub_cancel_right]`; rewrite the count with `card_filter_box_last`, `Nat.cast_sum`, and `Finset.sum_congr` with `card_filter_fiber_eq`; then a 4-step calc: (1) `Finset.sum_le_sum` with `ih`, positivity of `lam - 1 + 2i/R` via `Real.one_le_sqrt` and `linarith`; (2) factor out with `Finset.mul_sum` and `ring`; (3) `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hs.le` with `positivity`; (4) `Fin.prod_univ_castSucc`, `push_cast`, `field_simp`, `simp [R]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hr` (feeds `sum_inv_lt`'s `1 ≤ r` and the division by `R`), `hn` (feeds `sum_inv_lt`'s `2 ≤ m`; also needed since `√n` is inverted), `hlam` (feeds `sum_inv_lt` and positivity of the shifted parameters), `ih` (the per-fibre bound). All used; none removable.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box` (Roth/Box.lean), `Roth.weight` (Roth/Box.lean:71), `Roth.sum_inv_lt` (Roth/Lattice.lean:68), `Roth.card_filter_box_last` (Roth/Lattice.lean:131), `Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq` (Roth/Lattice.lean:148).
- **Used by**: Roth/Lattice.lean `Roth.card_filter_weight_le` only (l.218, the `succ n` case with `lam > 2 √(n+1)` and `n > 0`). `grep -rn card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux Roth/` returns only the definition (l.166) and this use.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 166–199, proof length 27 lines (173–199)
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`; would also be caught by the Step 9a pre-filter. The `ih` hypothesis is stated with an implicit-binder `∀ {t : ℝ}`, matching how `card_filter_weight_le`'s `induction ... generalizing lam` hypothesis is supplied.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.card_filter_weight_le`

### `theorem Roth.card_filter_weight_le`
- **Type**: `∀ (m : ℕ) (r : Fin m → ℕ), (∀ j, 0 < r j) → ∀ {lam : ℝ}, 0 < lam → (((box r).filter fun d ↦ weight r d ≤ ((m : ℝ) - lam) / 2).card : ℝ) ≤ 2 * √(m : ℝ) / lam * ∏ j, ((r j : ℝ) + 1)`
- **What**: Lemma 8 of the source (clean.tex §6, `roth:lem8`): for positive integers `r_1, …, r_m` and `λ > 0`, the number of multi-indices `0 ≤ j_i ≤ r_i` with `∑ j_i / r_i ≤ (m - λ)/2` is at most `2 √m λ⁻¹ ∏ (r_i + 1)`.
- **How**: Induction on `m` generalising `λ`. Case `m = 0`: the filter is empty by `Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty` (since `0 < λ`). Case `n + 1`: if `λ ≤ 2√(n+1)` use `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt_aux` (trivial bound by `card_box`); if `n = 0` and `λ > 2` the filter is again empty (`Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty`); otherwise the inductive step `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux` (fibre count `Roth.card_filter_box_last`, re-thresholding `Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq`, and the numeric estimate `Roth.sum_inv_lt`). Mathlib lemmas named directly: `Nat.eq_zero_or_pos`, `lt_of_not_ge`, `Finset.card_empty`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hr : ∀ j, 0 < r j` (necessary - compiled counterexample with `r = 0`); `hlam : 0 < lam` (necessary - at `λ = 0` the Lean RHS is `0` while the count of the zero index is positive). `m = 0` is allowed (the source requires `m ≥ 1`), so the Lean form is marginally more general than the source.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.box`, `Roth.weight`, `Roth.filter_weight_le_eq_empty` (private), `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_of_le_sqrt_aux` (private), `Roth.card_filter_weight_le_succ_aux` (private; which in turn uses `Roth.card_filter_box_last`, `Roth.card_filter_fiber_eq`, `Roth.sum_inv_lt`).
- **Used by**: `Roth.mul_card_filter_weight_le_aux` (private, Roth/MainLemma.lean:574). `grep -rn card_filter_weight_le Roth/` shows no other caller of this exact name (other hits are the `_aux` helpers in Roth/Lattice.lean).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 197–220 (docstring 197–201, statement 202–204, proof 205–220), proof length 16 lines
- **Notes**: This is the source's Lemma 8, a named lemma of the paper and of Roth's original 1955 proof. Its statement inherits `Roth.box` and `Roth.weight`, which earlier reports classify `NO-mathlib-has-it`/`REPLACE` (`Finset.Iic` and `Finsupp.weight`).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_weight_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.card_filter_weight_le.md)_

### `Roth/Index.lean`

#### `def Roth.index`

### `def Roth.index`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (P : MvPolynomial σ ℝ) (α : σ → ℝ) (r : σ → ℕ) : ℝ`
- **What**: The index of `P` at `α` relative to the parameters `r`: the infimum (a minimum for `P ≠ 0`) of the weights `∑ j, d j / r j` over the multi-indices `d` in the support of the Taylor translate `P(X + α)`, i.e. over `d` with `(Δ_d P)(α) ≠ 0`; junk value `0` for `P = 0` (`sInf ∅ = 0`).
- **How**: `sInf (weight r '' ((taylor α P).support : Set (σ →₀ ℕ)))`, using `Real.sInf` over the finite image of the support under `Roth.weight r`.
- **Hypotheses**: section variable `[Fintype σ]` (needed by `Roth.weight`). No positivity on `r`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight` (Roth/Box.lean:71), `MvPolynomial.taylor` (Roth/Taylor.lean:42).
- **Used by**: Roth/Index.lean (every lemma: `mem_support_taylor_iff` 62, `index_le_weight` 69, `exists_weight_eq_index` 76, `le_index_iff` 85, `index_nonneg` 93, `index_eq_zero_iff` 98, `index_sub_weight_le_index_hasseDeriv` 109, `le_index_sum` 118, `index_eq_weightedOrder_div` 143, `index_mul` 162, `index_prod` 175, `index_neg` 190, `index_C_mul` 195, `index_mul_left_weights` 200, `index_rename` 212, `index_le_card` 224, `index_unique` 234); Roth/MainLemma.lean:422, 455, 481, 482, 483, 556, 586, 623, 648, 700, 743, 749; Roth/Theta.lean:64, 101, 126, 226, 381, 382, 388, 402, 403, 408, 418, 465, 561, 586; Roth/Main.lean:381; Roth/Separation.lean:135 (docstring reference to the index of the embedded polynomial).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 55–59, body 1 line
- **Notes**: The module docstring lists it as the main definition. The file already proves the bridge `index_eq_weightedOrder_div` (Index.lean:143) to mathlib's `MvPowerSeries.weightedOrder` with natural weights `(∏ r) / r j`, and uses it for `index_mul`. The `P ≠ 0` hypothesis of that bridge is superfluous (evidence (1)).
