#### `theorem Roth.exists_det_eq_mul`

### `theorem Roth.exists_det_eq_mul`
- **Type**: `∀ {τ : Type u_1} [Finite τ] {P : MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℤ}, P ≠ 0 → ∀ {r : Option τ → ℕ}, (∀ j, degreeOf j P ≤ r j) → ∃ l, 1 ≤ l ∧ l ≤ r none + 1 ∧ ∃ D : Fin l → (τ →₀ ℕ), (∀ μ, (D μ).degree ≤ μ) ∧ ∃ (U : MvPolynomial τ ℤ) (V : Polynomial ℤ), U ≠ 0 ∧ V ≠ 0 ∧ (∀ j, degreeOf j U ≤ l * r (some j)) ∧ V.natDegree ≤ l * r none ∧ (Matrix.of fun μ ν ↦ hasseDeriv ((D μ).mapDomain some + Finsupp.single none ν) P).det = rename some U * Polynomial.aeval (X none) V` (from `#check`, evidence (0)).
- **What**: Roth's Lemma 2: for a nonzero integer polynomial `P(x_τ, y)` with partial degrees `≤ r`, there are `1 ≤ l ≤ r_y + 1` and Hasse operators `Δ_{D μ}` in the `x_τ` variables of orders `≤ μ` such that `F = det(Δ_{D μ} Δ_y^{(ν)} P)_{μ,ν<l}` equals `U(x_τ) V(y)` with `U, V` nonzero integral, `deg_{x_j} U ≤ l r_j`, `deg V ≤ l r_y`.
- **How**: `exists_rat_factors_det_hasseDeriv_aux` (private; uses `exists_separated_representation`, `exists_genWronskian_ne_zero`, `det_hasseDeriv_separated`, `wronskian_ne_zero_of_linearIndependent`, `hasseDeriv_map`) gives the factorisation over ℚ; `exists_int_factors` (Gauss-lemma step) descends it to ℤ; the degree bounds come from `degreeOf_rename_mul_aeval`, `degreeOf_none_rename_mul_aeval` and `degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le`.
- **Hypotheses**: `[Finite τ]` (needed by the private helper; evidence (1)); `hP : P ≠ 0` (needed: for `P = 0` the determinant is 0 but `U, V ≠ 0`); `hr` (all components used: `hr none` for `l ≤ r none + 1`, all for the degree bounds).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.exists_rat_factors_det_hasseDeriv_aux` (private), `Roth.exists_int_factors`, `Roth.degreeOf_rename_mul_aeval`, `Roth.degreeOf_none_rename_mul_aeval`, `Roth.degreeOf_det_hasseDeriv_le`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (project def)
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:571, `obtain ⟨l, hl, hl', D, hD, U, V, hU, hV, hUd, hVd, hF⟩ := exists_det_eq_mul hR.1 hR.2.1`); mentioned in module docstring Separation.lean:37. No other uses (`grep -rn exists_det_eq_mul Roth/`).

#### `def Roth.RSet`

### `def Roth.RSet`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} (B : ℝ) (r : σ → ℕ) : Set (MvPolynomial σ ℤ)`
- **What**: The set `ℛ_m(B; r)` of nonzero integer polynomials whose degree in each variable `X j` is at most `r j` and whose coefficients all have absolute value at most `B`.
- **How**: Set-builder `{R | R ≠ 0 ∧ (∀ j, degreeOf j R ≤ r j) ∧ ∀ d, |((R.coeff d : ℤ) : ℝ)| ≤ B}`, using mathlib's `MvPolynomial.degreeOf` and `MvPolynomial.coeff`.
- **Hypotheses**: none. The section variables `[Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ]` are not included (the evidence file elaborates `RSet` for a bare `σ`, and `sub_mem_RSet` uses it with no `Fintype`).
- **Uses from project**: `[]`

#### `def Roth.indexSet`

### `def Roth.indexSet`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (B : ℝ) (q r : σ → ℕ) : Set ℝ`
- **What**: The set of real numbers `ind(R; (h_j / q_j)_j; r)` where `R` ranges over `RSet B r` (nonzero integer polynomials with degree in `X j` at most `r j` and coefficients bounded by `B`) and `h : σ → ℤ` over tuples with each `h j` coprime to `q j`. Its supremum is `Θ_m(B; q; r)`.
- **How**: Set-builder `{x | ∃ R ∈ RSet B r, ∃ h, (∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j : ℤ)) ∧ index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) R) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r = x}`, using `MvPolynomial.map`, `Int.castRingHom` and `IsCoprime` from mathlib.
- **Hypotheses**: none. Only `[Fintype σ]` is needed (inherited from `Roth.index`); the section variable `[DecidableEq σ]` is not included (evidence (1)).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean only: `Θ` (68, `sSup (indexSet B q r)`), `indexSet_nonempty` (80), `indexSet_bddAbove` (87), `index_le_Θ` (102, via `indexSet_bddAbove` and membership), `Θ_le_of_forall_index_le` (127), `Θ_mono` (136), private `indexSet_comp_subset` (141–142), `Θ_equiv` (162–163). `Θ_nonneg` and `Θ_le_card` destructure membership through `Θ`'s `sSup`. Module docstring at lines 26 and 28. No uses outside Theta.lean (`grep -rn indexSet Roth/`).

#### `def Roth.Θ`

### `def Roth.Θ`
- **Type**: `{σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (B : ℝ) (q r : σ → ℕ) : ℝ` (noncomputable)
- **What**: `Θ_m(B; q_1, …, q_m; r_1, …, r_m)`, the supremum of the indices `ind(R; (h_j/q_j)_j; r)` over nonzero integer polynomials `R` with `deg_{X_j} R ≤ r_j` and coefficients bounded by `B`, and over `h : σ → ℤ` with each `h_j` coprime to `q_j`.
- **How**: `sSup (indexSet B q r)` in the conditionally complete lattice `ℝ`. The `csSup` side conditions come from the companion lemmas `indexSet_nonempty` (needs `1 ≤ B`) and `indexSet_bddAbove` (bound `Fintype.card σ` via `index_le_card`).
- **Hypotheses**: none. Only `[Fintype σ]` is used; the section `[DecidableEq σ]` is not included (evidence (1)).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.indexSet` (and through it `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean: `index_le_Θ` (101), `Θ_nonneg` (107), `Θ_le_card` (114), `Θ_le_of_forall_index_le` (126), `Θ_mono` (135), `Θ_equiv` (160), private `index_unique_le_log_div` (253–258), `Θ_unique_le` (256–257, Lemma 5), `lem6M` (273), `lem6Φ` (276), private `index_separated_mul_le_aux` (443–450), `index_le_lem6` (583–591), `Θ_option_le` (593–599, Lemma 6), `lem7_Φ_bound` (707), private `theta_scaled_unique_le_aux` (744–759), private `theta_prefix_lt_aux` (787–790), the `Θ_lt` base case at 820–822, private `theta_phi_lt_aux` (851–855), private `theta_lt_succ_aux` (901–920), private `theta_lt_of_large_delta_aux` (934–959), `Θ_lt` (962–966, Lemma 7). Roth/MainLemma.lean: private `theta_lt_eta_of_sq_log_bound_aux` (676, 687), private `exists_auxiliary_polynomial_with_index_gap_aux` (699), `main_lemma` (750, 760, including `index_le_Θ hW hh`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 68, proof length 0 lines (one-line def body)
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5 l.655–670. The paper assumes `q_j ≥ 1`, `r_j ≥ 1`, `B ≥ 1`; the Lean def takes none of these. For `B < 1` the defining family is empty, so `Θ B q r = sSup ∅ = 0` (junk value, compiled as evidence (3)); all consumers that need `Θ` as a true supremum assume `1 ≤ B`.

#### `theorem Roth.one_mem_RSet`

### `theorem Roth.one_mem_RSet`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} {B : ℝ}, 1 ≤ B → ∀ (r : σ → ℕ), (1 : MvPolynomial σ ℤ) ∈ Roth.RSet B r`
- **What**: If `1 ≤ B`, the constant polynomial `1` lies in `ℛ_m(B; r)`: it is nonzero, has degree 0 in every variable, and every coefficient is 0 or 1, hence at most `B` in absolute value.
- **How**: `classical; simp [RSet, coeff_one, apply_ite, ite_apply, hB, zero_le_one.trans hB]`. It unfolds the three-way conjunction. The degree clause closes by simp (`degreeOf` of `1`). The coefficient clause becomes `|if 0 = d then 1 else 0| ≤ B` and is split by `apply_ite`, closed by `hB` and `0 ≤ B`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hB : 1 ≤ B`. The section instances `[Fintype σ] [DecidableEq σ]` are omitted (`omit ... in`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.RSet`
- **Used by**: `Roth.indexSet_nonempty` (Roth/Theta.lean:81). No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 73–75, proof length 2 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5, where the paper says the constant polynomial 1 with every `h_i = 1` is allowed. The paper assumes `B ≥ 1` in the definition of `ℛ_m`. Lean drops that from `RSet` and adds it here as a hypothesis.

#### `theorem Roth.indexSet_nonempty`

### `theorem Roth.indexSet_nonempty`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {B : ℝ}, 1 ≤ B → ∀ (q r : σ → ℕ), (Roth.indexSet B q r).Nonempty`
- **What**: If `1 ≤ B`, the set of indices `ind(R; h/q; r)` (over `R ∈ ℛ_m(B; r)` and `h` coprime to `q`) whose supremum is `Θ_m(B; q; r)` is nonempty. So the supremum is well-defined in the `csSup` sense.
- **How**: A term proof `⟨_, 1, one_mem_RSet hB r, fun _ ↦ 1, fun _ ↦ isCoprime_one_left, rfl⟩`. The witness polynomial is the constant `1` (`Roth.one_mem_RSet`), and the point is `h = 1`, coprime to every `q j` by mathlib's `isCoprime_one_left`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hB : 1 ≤ B` (needed for `1 ∈ RSet B r`). The section instance `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted (`omit ... in`). `[Fintype σ]` is inherited from `indexSet`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.one_mem_RSet` (and through them `Roth.RSet` and `Roth.index`)
- **Used by**: `Roth.Θ_le_of_forall_index_le` (Roth/Theta.lean:127, `csSup_le (indexSet_nonempty hB q r) ?_`), `Roth.Θ_mono` (Roth/Theta.lean:136, `csSup_le_csSup (indexSet_bddAbove B' q) (indexSet_nonempty hB q r) ?_`). No other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 80–81, proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5 ("This supremum is well-defined"). The paper assumes `B ≥ 1` throughout. Lean makes it a hypothesis here.

#### `theorem Roth.indexSet_bddAbove`

### `theorem Roth.indexSet_bddAbove`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] (B : ℝ) (q : σ → ℕ) {r : σ → ℕ}, BddAbove (Roth.indexSet B q r)`
- **What**: The set of indices `ind(R; h/q; r)`, taken over `R ∈ ℛ_m(B; r)` and `h` coprime to `q`, is bounded above by the number of variables `m = card σ`. This is the boundedness half of "the supremum `Θ_m` is well-defined".
- **How**: It gives the explicit bound `Fintype.card σ`. For each member `R` it applies `Roth.index_le_card`, which needs two facts. Nonvanishing over ℝ comes from mathlib's `map_ne_zero_iff` and `MvPolynomial.map_injective` with `Int.cast_injective`. The per-variable degree bound comes from `simpa [degreeOf, MvPolynomial.degrees_map_of_injective]`.
- **Hypotheses**: none. `B` and `q` are arbitrary and `r` is implicit with no positivity. The section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.RSet` (destructured membership), `Roth.index_le_card`, and `Roth.index` through `indexSet`.
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_Θ` (Roth/Theta.lean:102, `le_csSup (indexSet_bddAbove B q) ...`) and `Roth.Θ_mono` (Roth/Theta.lean:136, `csSup_le_csSup (indexSet_bddAbove B' q) ...`). Nothing else uses it (`grep -rn indexSet_bddAbove Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 83–92 (docstring 83–86, statement 87, proof 88–92), proof length 5 lines
- **Notes**:
  - The docstring opens "If every `r j` is positive", but the statement has no such hypothesis. The lemma holds for every `r` (evidence (4)), so the docstring is stale and should drop that clause.
  - The argument order is also unusual: `B` and `q` are explicit while `r` is implicit.
  - The proof body (lines 89–92) is a near-verbatim copy of the body of `Θ_le_card` (Theta.lean:113–118).

#### `theorem Roth.index_le_Θ`

### `theorem Roth.index_le_Θ`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {B : ℝ} {q r : σ → ℕ} {R : MvPolynomial σ ℤ}, R ∈ RSet B r → ∀ {h : σ → ℤ}, (∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j : ℤ)) → index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) R) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r ≤ Θ B q r`
- **What**: Take any `R ∈ ℛ_m(B; r)` and any point `(h_j/q_j)_j` with each `h_j` coprime to `q_j`. The index of `R` at that point, relative to `r`, is at most `Θ_m(B; q; r)`. This is the "a member is at most the supremum" property of `Θ`.
- **How**: `le_csSup (indexSet_bddAbove B q) ⟨R, hR, h, hh, rfl⟩`. This is mathlib's `le_csSup`, given the project's `BddAbove` lemma and the anonymous-constructor witness of membership in `indexSet`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hR : R ∈ RSet B r` and `hh : ∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j)`. Both are needed to build the membership witness. There is no `1 ≤ B` hypothesis, because `le_csSup` needs only `BddAbove`, not `Nonempty`. The section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`, `Roth.indexSet_bddAbove`
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:443 and 445 (private `index_separated_mul_le_aux`, which feeds Lemma 6, as `hUΘ` and `hVΘ`); Roth/MainLemma.lean:760 (`main_lemma`, `linarith only [hgap, index_le_Θ hW hh]`). Found with `grep -rn index_le_Θ Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 95–102 (docstring 96–98, statement 99–101, proof 102), proof length 1 line
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5 l.655–670 (definition of `Θ_m` as a supremum). The Lean note at l.700–702 asks for "lemmas for bounding an individual index", and this is that lemma.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.index_le_Θ.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.index_le_Θ.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.Θ_nonneg`

### `theorem Roth.Θ_nonneg`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {B : ℝ} (q : σ → ℕ) {r : σ → ℕ}, 0 ≤ Θ B q r`
- **What**: For every coefficient bound `B`, denominators `q` and weights `r`, the quantity `Θ_m(B; q; r)` (the supremum of the indices `ind(R; h/q; r)` over admissible `R` and coprime numerators `h`) is nonnegative. This is the lower half of the paper's `0 ≤ Θ_m ≤ m`.
- **How**: `apply Real.sSup_nonneg`, then destructure membership in `indexSet` (`rintro _ ⟨R, _, h, _, rfl⟩`) and close with the project's `index_nonneg`. Mathlib's `Real.sSup_nonneg` needs only that every member is nonnegative, because `sSup ∅ = 0` and `sSup` of an unbounded set is `0` in `ℝ`; hence no `1 ≤ B` hypothesis and no nonemptiness or boundedness lemma is required.
- **Hypotheses**: none (beyond `[Fintype σ]`). No `1 ≤ B`, no positivity of `r`; the section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.index`, `Roth.index_nonneg` (and `Roth.RSet` implicitly through the `indexSet` membership pattern)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:583 (`add_nonneg (Θ_nonneg _) (Θ_nonneg _)`, the nonnegativity argument fed to `index_numeric_bound_aux` inside the Lemma 6 proof); also cited in the module docstring at Roth/Theta.lean:33. Found with `grep -rn Θ_nonneg Roth/`; no other file uses it.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 104–110 (docstring 105–106, statement 107, proof 108–110), proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5 l.665 ("satisfies `0 ≤ Θ_m ≤ m`"). The paper argues via the member `R = 1` (nonemptiness); the Lean proof avoids that by using the `sSup ∅ = 0` convention, so it holds for every `B`, including `B < 1` where `indexSet` is empty. Minor shape nit: `q` is explicit while `B`, `r` are implicit (all three are determined by the conclusion); harmless, and matches `Θ_le_card`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_nonneg.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_nonneg.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.Θ_le_card`

### `theorem Roth.Θ_le_card`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {B : ℝ} (q : σ → ℕ) {r : σ → ℕ}, Θ B q r ≤ ↑(Fintype.card σ)`
- **What**: For every coefficient bound `B`, denominators `q` and weights `r`, the quantity `Θ_m(B; q; r)` is at most the number `m = card σ` of variables. This is the upper half of the paper's `0 ≤ Θ_m ≤ m`.
- **How**: `refine Real.sSup_le ?_ (Nat.cast_nonneg _)`, then destructure membership in `indexSet` (`rintro _ ⟨R, ⟨hR0, hRd, -⟩, h, -, rfl⟩`) and close with the project's `index_le_card`, transporting `R ≠ 0` along the injective cast `ℤ → ℝ` (`map_ne_zero_iff`, `map_injective`, `Int.cast_injective`) and the degree bound with `degrees_map_of_injective`. Mathlib's `Real.sSup_le` needs only a member-wise bound and `0 ≤ card σ` (because `sSup ∅ = 0` in `ℝ`), so no `1 ≤ B` and no nonemptiness lemma is required.
- **Hypotheses**: none (beyond `[Fintype σ]`). No `1 ≤ B`, no positivity of `r`; the section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.RSet` (membership pattern), `Roth.index`, `Roth.index_le_card`
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:939 (`simpa using Θ_le_card (r := r) q`, inside private `theta_lt_of_large_delta_aux`, the large-δ case of Lemma 7); also named in the module docstring at Roth/Theta.lean:33. Found with `grep -rn Θ_le_card Roth/`; no other file uses it.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 112–118 (docstring 113, statement 114, proof 115–118), proof length 4 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5 l.665–667 ("`0 ≤ Θ_m ≤ m` ... Taylor's theorem gives a nonzero derivative ... of weight at most `m`"). The proof body (lines 116–118) is a verbatim duplicate of `indexSet_bddAbove`'s body (Theta.lean:89–93), as already recorded in Roth.indexSet_bddAbove.md; factoring the shared fact `card σ ∈ upperBounds (indexSet B q r)` would make both one-liners (compiled in evidence/Roth.indexSet_bddAbove/refactor.lean). `q` explicit while `B`, `r` implicit, matching `Θ_nonneg`; cosmetic.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_le_card.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_le_card.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.Θ_le_of_forall_index_le`

### `theorem Roth.Θ_le_of_forall_index_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {B : ℝ}, 1 ≤ B → ∀ {q r : σ → ℕ} {c : ℝ}, (∀ R ∈ RSet B r, ∀ h : σ → ℤ, (∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j : ℤ)) → index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) R) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r ≤ c) → Θ B q r ≤ c`
- **What**: Suppose `B ≥ 1` and `c` bounds the index `ind(R; (h_j/q_j)_j; r)` for every `R ∈ ℛ_m(B; r)` and every `h` with each `h_j` coprime to `q_j`. Then `Θ_m(B; q; r) ≤ c`. This is the "the supremum is the least upper bound" property of `Θ`, the counterpart of `index_le_Θ`.
- **How**: `csSup_le (indexSet_nonempty hB q r)`, then `rintro _ ⟨R, hR, h, hh, rfl⟩; exact hc R hR h hh`. This is mathlib's `csSup_le`, with nonemptiness from the project's `indexSet_nonempty` and the bound obtained by destructuring membership in `indexSet`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hB : 1 ≤ B`, which makes `indexSet B q r` nonempty (it contains the index of the constant polynomial `1`). `hc` gives the pointwise bound. The section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`, `Roth.indexSet_nonempty`
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:258 (`Θ_unique_le`, Lemma 5) and Roth/Theta.lean:599 (`Θ_option_le`, Lemma 6). Found with `grep -rn Θ_le_of_forall_index_le Roth/`. There are no uses outside Theta.lean.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 121–129 (docstring 122–123, statement 124–126, proof 127–129), proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.692 (proof of Lemma 5: "Taking the supremum proves the claim"). Lemma 6 makes the same move.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_le_of_forall_index_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_le_of_forall_index_le.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.Θ_mono`

### `theorem Roth.Θ_mono`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {B B' : ℝ}, 1 ≤ B → B ≤ B' → ∀ (q : σ → ℕ) {r : σ → ℕ}, Θ B q r ≤ Θ B' q r`
- **What**: If `1 ≤ B ≤ B'`, then `Θ_m(B; q; r) ≤ Θ_m(B'; q; r)`. Raising the coefficient bound enlarges `ℛ_m(B; r)`, which enlarges the set of indices, so it cannot decrease their supremum.
- **How**: The proof is `csSup_le_csSup (indexSet_bddAbove B' q) (indexSet_nonempty hB q r) ?_`. The inclusion `indexSet B q r ⊆ indexSet B' q r` is proved by destructuring a membership witness and weakening the coefficient bound with `(hRc d).trans hBB'`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hB : 1 ≤ B` is used only to make `indexSet B q r` nonempty. It is superfluous for the conclusion; see the evidence. `hBB' : B ≤ B'` carries the content. The section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`, `Roth.indexSet_bddAbove`, `Roth.indexSet_nonempty`
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:758 (in the private lemma that bounds Θ in one variable during the proof of Lemma 7) and Roth/Theta.lean:790 (`theta_prefix_lt_aux`, Lemma 7). Found with `grep -rn Θ_mono Roth/`. Theta.lean:34 is a docstring mention. There are no uses outside Theta.lean.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 131–138 (docstring 132–133, statement 134–135, proof 136–138), proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.668 ("Θ_m is nondecreasing in B, since increasing B enlarges the set of allowed polynomials"). The source states it with no hypothesis `B ≥ 1`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_mono.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_mono.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.indexSet_comp_subset`

### `lemma Roth.indexSet_comp_subset`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type u_1} [Fintype σ] {τ : Type u_2} [Fintype τ] (e : σ ≃ τ) (B : ℝ) (q r : τ → ℕ), indexSet B (q ∘ ⇑e) (r ∘ ⇑e) ⊆ indexSet B q r`
- **What**: Renaming variables along an equivalence `e : σ ≃ τ` maps the set of indices `{ind(R; h/(q∘e); r∘e)}` over `R ∈ ℛ_σ(B; r∘e)` into the corresponding set for `(q, r)` over `τ`. A witness `(R, h)` is sent to `(rename e R, h ∘ e.symm)`.
- **How**: Destructure a membership witness `⟨R, ⟨hR0, hRd, hRc⟩, h, hh, rfl⟩` and provide `rename e R` and `h ∘ e.symm`. The five goals are closed as follows. Nonzero: `rename_eq_zero_iff_of_injective`. Degree bound: surject with `e.surjective`, then `degreeOf_rename_of_injective`. Coefficient bound: surject on exponents with `Finsupp.mapDomain_surjective e.surjective`, then `coeff_rename_mapDomain`. Coprimality: `hh (e.symm j)`. Index equality: `simp [map_rename, index_rename e.injective, Function.comp_def]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `e : σ ≃ τ`. Only injectivity is needed for the index, nonzero, degree and coefficient goals. Surjectivity is needed to transport the degree bound to every `j : τ` and the coefficient bound to every exponent `d : τ →₀ ℕ`. Coprimality uses `e.symm`, so a full equivalence is the natural hypothesis. `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted. `B`, `q` and `r` are unconstrained.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.RSet` (destructured through `indexSet`), `Roth.index` (through `indexSet`), `Roth.index_rename` (Roth/Index.lean:212)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:162 and Roth/Theta.lean:163, both in `Roth.Θ_equiv`, once with `e` and once with `e.symm`. Found with `grep -rn indexSet_comp_subset Roth/`. There are no other uses.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 140–154 (the `omit` line is 140, the statement is 141–142, the proof is 143–154), proof length 12 lines
- **Notes**: The lemma has no docstring, which is acceptable for a private lemma. `Θ_equiv` is used at Roth/Theta.lean:919 in Lemma 7.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.indexSet_comp_subset.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.indexSet_comp_subset.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.Θ_equiv`

### `theorem Roth.Θ_equiv`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] {τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] (e : σ ≃ τ) (B : ℝ) (q r : τ → ℕ), Θ B (q ∘ e) (r ∘ e) = Θ B q r`
- **What**: `Θ_m(B; q; r)` does not change when the variables are relabelled by a bijection `e : σ ≃ τ`, with the denominators `q` and weights `r` relabelled along with them.
- **How**: `congrArg sSup` reduces the goal to the set equality `indexSet B (q ∘ e) (r ∘ e) = indexSet B q r`. That equality is `subset_antisymm` of two instances of the private helper `indexSet_comp_subset`, applied to `e` and to `e.symm`. The second instance is massaged by `simpa [Function.comp_def]`, using `e.symm ∘ e = id`. The helper sends a witness `R` to `rename e R`, using `rename_eq_zero_iff_of_injective`, `degreeOf_rename_of_injective`, `coeff_rename_mapDomain`, `map_rename` and the project's `index_rename`.
- **Hypotheses**: `e : σ ≃ τ` is a genuine bijection, needed for an equality and not just one inequality. `B` is arbitrary, with no `1 ≤ B`. Only `[Fintype σ]` and `[Fintype τ]` are needed; the section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.indexSet`, `Roth.indexSet_comp_subset` (private, Theta.lean:141). The helper in turn uses `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index` and `Roth.index_rename`.
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:919. That is the Lemma 7 proof, which transports from `Fin (n + 2)` to `Option (Fin (n + 1))` via `finSuccEquivLast`, with the rewrite `rw [← Θ_equiv e.symm ...]`. Theta.lean:34 is a module-docstring mention. Nothing outside Theta.lean uses it (`grep -rn Θ_equiv Roth/`).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 156–163 (docstring 157–158, statement 159–160, proof 161–163), proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.630–634 ("Prove invariance under nonzero scalars and variable renaming ... as separate lemmas, so later proofs do not repeat the conversions"). The name `Θ_equiv` is slightly unusual. Mathlib would say `Θ_comp_equiv` or `Θ_rename`. This is a cosmetic point only.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_equiv.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_equiv.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.isPrimitive_linear`

### `theorem Roth.isPrimitive_linear`
- **Type**: `∀ {h : ℤ} {q : ℕ}, IsCoprime h (q : ℤ) → (Polynomial.C (q : ℤ) * Polynomial.X - Polynomial.C h).IsPrimitive`
- **What**: If the integers h and q are coprime, the linear integer polynomial q·X − h is primitive, i.e. its only constant divisors in ℤ[X] are units.
- **How**: Unfolds `IsPrimitive` (`intro c hc`), turns `C c ∣ p` into divisibility of every coefficient by `Polynomial.C_dvd_iff_dvd_coeff`, then applies `IsCoprime.isUnit_of_dvd'` to c ∣ coeff 0 = −h (`simpa`) and c ∣ coeff 1 = q (`simpa only [Polynomial.coeff_sub, Polynomial.coeff_C_mul, Polynomial.coeff_X_one, Polynomial.coeff_C, ...]`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hcop : IsCoprime h (q : ℤ)`. No positivity of q is needed (q = 0 forces h to be a unit, still primitive).
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib lemmas)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:196 `exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity` (as `isPrimitive_pow (isPrimitive_linear hcop) t`, feeding `Polynomial.IsPrimitive.Int.dvd_iff_map_cast_dvd_map_cast`) only; `grep -rn isPrimitive_linear Roth/` gives the definition (l.167) and that use. Transitively `pow_rootMultiplicity_le` (Roth/Theta.lean:206, uses it at l.209) and the §5 Lemma 5 chain downstream.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 167–173, proof length 6 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem) inside `section Lemma5`. Implements the "(q_1 x_1 − h_1) is primitive" step of the source's Gauss-lemma argument (§5, proof of Lemma 5).

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.isPrimitive_linear.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.isPrimitive_linear.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.isPrimitive_pow`

### `theorem Roth.isPrimitive_pow`
- **Type**: `∀ {p : Polynomial ℤ}, p.IsPrimitive → ∀ (t : ℕ), (p ^ t).IsPrimitive`
- **What**: Every natural power of a primitive integer polynomial is primitive (the t-fold form of Gauss's lemma on products).
- **How**: Induction on `t` via `Nat.rec`: base case `Polynomial.isPrimitive_one` (since `p ^ 0 = 1`), step `simpa [pow_succ] using ih.mul hp` with mathlib's `Polynomial.IsPrimitive.mul` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Polynomial/Content.lean:373).
- **Hypotheses**: `hp : p.IsPrimitive`; `t : ℕ` explicit. No hypothesis is superfluous; the base ring ℤ is more specific than needed (any `CommRing` with `NormalizedGCDMonoid` works, see Evidence).
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib: `Polynomial.isPrimitive_one`, `Polynomial.IsPrimitive.mul`, `pow_succ`)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:196 `exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity` (as `isPrimitive_pow (isPrimitive_linear hcop) t`, the primitivity input to `Polynomial.IsPrimitive.Int.dvd_iff_map_cast_dvd_map_cast`) only; `grep -rn isPrimitive_pow Roth/` gives the definition (l.175) and that use. Transitively `pow_rootMultiplicity_le` (Roth/Theta.lean:206) and the §5 Lemma 5 chain.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 175–177, proof length 1 line (term-mode `Nat.rec`)
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem) inside `section Lemma5`. Implements "(q_1 x_1 − h_1)^t is primitive" from the source's Gauss-lemma argument (§5, proof of Lemma 5), together with `isPrimitive_linear`.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.isPrimitive_pow.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.isPrimitive_pow.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity`

### `theorem Roth.exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity`
- **Type**: `∀ {R : Polynomial ℤ} {h : ℤ} {q : ℕ}, 0 < q → IsCoprime h (q : ℤ) → ∃ Q : Polynomial ℤ, R = (C (q : ℤ) * X - C h) ^ rootMultiplicity ((h : ℚ) / q) (R.map (Int.castRingHom ℚ)) * Q`
- **What**: If `h` and `q > 0` are coprime and `t` is the multiplicity of `h/q` as a root of `R` over ℚ, then `(qX − h)^t` divides `R` in ℤ[X].
- **How**: `t` is set to the multiplicity. `hmap` computes `(qX − h)` mapped to ℚ[X] as `C q * (X − C (h/q))` via `Polynomial.map_sub/map_mul/map_C/map_X`, `mul_sub`, `mul_div_cancel₀`. Then Gauss's lemma `Polynomial.IsPrimitive.Int.dvd_iff_map_cast_dvd_map_cast` with primitivity from the private `isPrimitive_pow (isPrimitive_linear hcop) t` reduces to divisibility over ℚ. Over ℚ it rewrites with `Polynomial.map_pow`, `mul_pow`, `C_pow`, `Polynomial.C_mul_dvd` (unit constant) and closes with `Polynomial.pow_rootMultiplicity_dvd`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hq : 0 < q` (only `q ≠ 0` is used); `hcop : IsCoprime h q` (only relative primality is used). `R` need not be nonzero (for `R = 0` the multiplicity is 0).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.isPrimitive_linear` (private, Theta.lean:167), `Roth.isPrimitive_pow` (private, Theta.lean:175).
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:209 (`pow_rootMultiplicity_le`, the leading-coefficient bound of Lemma 5). `grep -rn exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity Roth/` gives only the definition and this use. Transitively used by `index_unique_le_log_div` (Theta.lean:245) and the Lemma 5 chain.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 179–200 (docstring 179–183, statement 184–187, proof 188–200), proof length 13 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.677–699 (§5, proof of Lemma 5, "The polynomial (q_1x_1-h_1)^t is primitive and divides R over ℚ. Gauss's lemma therefore gives ..."). The existential `∃ Q, R = _ * Q` is definitionally `_ ∣ R`; mathlib would state the `∣` form.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.pow_rootMultiplicity_le`

### `theorem Roth.pow_rootMultiplicity_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {R : Polynomial ℤ}, R ≠ 0 → ∀ {h : ℤ} {q : ℕ}, 0 < q → IsCoprime h (q : ℤ) → ∀ {B : ℝ}, |(R.leadingCoeff : ℝ)| ≤ B → (q : ℝ) ^ rootMultiplicity ((h : ℚ) / q) (R.map (Int.castRingHom ℚ)) ≤ B`
- **What**: If `R ∈ ℤ[X]` is nonzero with `|lc(R)| ≤ B`, and `h/q` (coprime, `q > 0`) is a root of `R` of multiplicity `t` over ℚ, then `q^t ≤ B`.
- **How**: Obtains `R = (qX − h)^t Q` from `Roth.exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity`; computes `lc R = q^t · lc Q` with `Polynomial.leadingCoeff_mul`, `Polynomial.leadingCoeff_pow`, `Polynomial.leadingCoeff_linear`; `1 ≤ |lc Q|` from `Int.one_le_abs` and `Polynomial.leadingCoeff_ne_zero` (Q ≠ 0 via `right_ne_zero_of_mul`); then casts to ℝ (`push_cast`, `abs_mul`, `abs_pow`, `Nat.abs_cast`) and closes with `le_mul_of_one_le_right`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hR : R ≠ 0` (needed: for `R = 0`, `t = 0` and `1 ≤ B` fails at `B = 0`); `hq : 0 < q` (only `q ≠ 0` used); `hcop : IsCoprime h q` (only relative primality used); `hB` the leading-coefficient bound.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.exists_eq_pow_mul_of_rootMultiplicity` (Theta.lean:184).
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:245 (`index_unique_le_log_div`, private; the one-variable case of Lemma 5). `grep -rn pow_rootMultiplicity_le Roth/` gives only the definition and this use.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 201–220 (docstring 201–205, statement 206–208, proof 209–220), proof length 12 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex l.686–690 (§5, proof of Lemma 5, "Comparing nonzero leading coefficients yields B ≥ |lc(R)| = q_1^t |lc(Q)| ≥ q_1^t"). The integer content is `q^t ∣ lc R`; the real-valued `B` bound is project packaging.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.pow_rootMultiplicity_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.pow_rootMultiplicity_le.md)_

#### `lemma Roth.index_unique_le_log_div`

### `theorem Roth.index_unique_le_log_div`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type u_1} [Fintype σ] [Unique σ] {B : ℝ} {q r : σ → ℕ}, 1 < q default → ∀ {R : MvPolynomial σ ℤ}, R ≠ 0 → |((uniqueAlgEquiv ℤ σ R).leadingCoeff : ℝ)| ≤ B → ∀ {h : σ → ℤ}, IsCoprime (h default) (q default : ℤ) → index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) R) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r ≤ Real.log B / (r default * Real.log (q default))`
- **What**: In one variable, if `R ∈ ℤ[X]` is nonzero with `|lc(R)| ≤ B`, and `h/q` is in lowest terms with `q > 1`, then `ind(R; h/q; r) ≤ log B / (r log q)`. This is the per-polynomial bound behind Lemma 5.
- **How**: Let `R1 := uniqueAlgEquiv ℤ σ R`. `hmapeq` shows that mapping to ℝ commutes with `uniqueAlgEquiv`, by `ext` and `coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv`, `Polynomial.coeff_map`, `coeff_map`. `hmult` moves the root multiplicity at `h/q` from ℝ to ℚ via `Polynomial.eq_rootMultiplicity_map` (with `Rat.castHom ℝ`, `Rat.cast_injective`) and `Polynomial.map_map`. The project's `index_unique` (Roth/Index.lean:234) then rewrites the index as `rootMultiplicity / r default`. Applying `Real.log_le_log` to the project's `pow_rootMultiplicity_le` (`q^t ≤ B`) and then `Real.log_pow` gives `t log q ≤ log B`. The bound follows from `le_div_iff₀` with `Real.log_pos` and `div_le_div_of_nonneg_right`, closed by `simpa [div_div, mul_comm]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hq : 1 < q default` is needed for `log q > 0` and, through `Nat.zero_lt_of_lt`, for `0 < q`. `hR : R ≠ 0` is needed by `index_unique` and `pow_rootMultiplicity_le`. `hRB` bounds only the leading coefficient of the one-variable polynomial, which is weaker than membership in `RSet`. `hh` gives coprimality at the single index only. `h` and `q` are functions on `σ` because `index` takes points `σ → ℝ`; only their values at `default` matter. The section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted. None of the hypotheses is superfluous. If `r default = 0`, both sides are `0` because of division by zero in ℝ, so the statement still holds.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.index`, `Roth.index_unique`, `Roth.pow_rootMultiplicity_le`, `MvPolynomial.coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv` (mathlib), `MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv` (mathlib). The project identifiers are `index`, `index_unique` and `pow_rootMultiplicity_le`.
- **Used by**: only Roth/Theta.lean:259, in `Roth.Θ_unique_le` (Lemma 5), through `Θ_le_of_forall_index_le`. `grep -rn index_unique_le_log_div Roth/` returns the definition (l.223) and that one use.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 222–250 (`omit` on l.222, statement 223–227, proof 228–250), proof length 22 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (recorded as theorem) in `section Lemma5`. It has no docstring, which is acceptable for a private lemma. It implements the step from `B ≥ q_1^t` to `t/r_1 ≤ log B/(r_1 log q_1)` in §5, proof of Lemma 5.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.index_unique_le_log_div.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.index_unique_le_log_div.md)_

#### `theorem Roth.Θ_unique_le`

### `theorem Roth.Θ_unique_le`
- **Type**: `∀ {σ : Type*} [Fintype σ] [Unique σ] {B : ℝ}, 1 ≤ B → ∀ {q : σ → ℕ}, 1 < q default → ∀ {r : σ → ℕ}, Θ B q r ≤ Real.log B / (↑(r default) * Real.log ↑(q default))`
- **What**: Lemma 5 of the source. In one variable, if `q > 1` and `B ≥ 1`, the supremum `Θ_1(B; q; r)` of indices of nonzero integer polynomials with degree at most `r` and coefficients bounded by `B`, taken at rational points `h/q` with `gcd(h, q) = 1`, is at most `log B / (r log q)`.
- **How**: A term-mode proof. `Θ_le_of_forall_index_le hB` reduces to a bound on each index. Each index is bounded by the private `index_unique_le_log_div`, whose leading-coefficient hypothesis comes from the `RSet` coefficient bound `hR.2.2` (rewritten with `Polynomial.leadingCoeff` and `coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv`). `index_unique_le_log_div` in turn uses `index_unique`, a `ℚ`-to-`ℝ` transfer of root multiplicity (`Polynomial.eq_rootMultiplicity_map`), `pow_rootMultiplicity_le` (Gauss's lemma and a leading-coefficient comparison), and `Real.log_le_log`.
- **Hypotheses**: `1 ≤ B` (needed for nonemptiness in `csSup_le`, and genuinely necessary, see Evidence (1)); `1 < q default` (needed so that `log q > 0`); `[Unique σ]` (the one-variable case); `[Fintype σ]` (needed by `Θ`). The section's `[DecidableEq σ]` is omitted. No positivity of `r` is assumed: when `r = 0` the RHS is `0` by Lean's `x / 0 = 0`, and the statement still holds.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.Θ`, `Roth.Θ_le_of_forall_index_le`, `Roth.index_unique_le_log_div` (private), `Roth.RSet` (membership projection `hR.1`, `hR.2.2`), `Roth.coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv` (in `simpa only`), `Roth.uniqueAlgEquiv` (via the helper's statement), `Roth.index`.
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean:759 (inside private `theta_scaled_unique_le_aux`, the one-variable term of Lemma 6/7's Φ bound); Roth/Theta.lean:822 (inside private `theta_lt_base_aux`, the `m = 1` base case of Lemma 7); module docstring Roth/Theta.lean:35. Found with `grep -rn Θ_unique_le Roth/`; no other file uses it.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 253–260 (docstring 253–255, statement 256–257, proof 258–260), proof length 3 lines
- **Notes**: Source: clean.tex §5, Lemma `roth:lem5` (l.671–691). The paper also assumes `r_1 > 0` in the proof ("Since `log q_1 > 0` and `r_1 > 0`"); the Lean statement drops it thanks to the division-by-zero convention.

_Detail report: [.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_unique_le.md](.mathlib-quality/overview/mathlibable/Roth.Θ_unique_le.md)_

#### `def Roth.lem6Φ`

### `def Roth.lem6Φ`
- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] (q r : Option τ → ℕ) (M : ℝ) (l : ℕ) : ℝ`
- **What**: The quantity `Φ` of Lemma 6. It is `Θ_1(M; q_p; l r_p) + Θ_{p-1}(M; q_1, …, q_{p-1}; l r_1, …, l r_{p-1})`. The variables are indexed by `Option τ`, and the distinguished last variable `p` is `none`.

#### `theorem Roth.one_le_lem6M`

### `theorem Roth.one_le_lem6M`
- **Type**: `(p r₁ : ℕ) {B : ℝ} (hB : 1 ≤ B) (l : ℕ) : 1 ≤ lem6M p r₁ B l`
- **What**: If `B ≥ 1`, then the Lemma 6 constant `M = (r₁+1)^{pl} · l! · B^l · 2^{plr₁}` is at least `1`.
- **How**: It unfolds `lem6M` with `rw` and splits the product with three nested `one_le_mul_of_one_le_of_one_le`. Each factor is then closed directly: `one_le_pow₀` handles the three powers (using `le_add_of_nonneg_left` with `Nat.cast_nonneg r₁` for `1 ≤ r₁ + 1`, `hB`, and `norm_num` for `1 ≤ 2`), and `Nat.factorial_pos` with `exact_mod_cast` handles `1 ≤ l!`.

#### `theorem Roth.floor_sq_div_two_le_sum`

### `theorem Roth.floor_sq_div_two_le_sum`
- **Type**: `∀ {a : ℝ}, 0 ≤ a → (⌊a⌋₊ : ℝ) ^ 2 / 2 ≤ ∑ ν ∈ Finset.range (⌊a⌋₊ + 1), (a - ν)`
- **What**: For real `a ≥ 0`, `∑_{0 ≤ ν ≤ ⌊a⌋} (a − ν) ≥ ⌊a⌋²/2`. This is the first FACT in the proof of Lemma 6 (§5, clean.tex l.857–875).
- **How**: It casts the Gauss sum identity `Finset.sum_range_id_mul_two (⌊a⌋₊ + 1)` to ℝ. It then splits the sum with `Finset.sum_sub_distrib`, `Finset.sum_const`, `Finset.card_range` and `nsmul_eq_mul`, and closes with `nlinarith [Nat.floor_le ha]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `ha : 0 ≤ a`, used for `Nat.floor_le`. It is needed: for negative `a`, `⌊a⌋₊ = 0` and the right side is `a < 0`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: unused in project. `grep -rn floor_sq_div_two_le_sum Roth Roth.lean` finds only the declaration at Roth/Theta.lean:289. `git show 9bf5ca8` shows that the old proof of the Θ positive-part bound called it (together with `sq_div_three_lt_floor_sq_div_two`). That commit ("Strengthen Theta positive-part sum bound") replaced the call with the inline `hbound` in the private `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux` (l.320–334), which proves the sharper `(θ r)²/3 ≤ ∑ (θ r − ν)` directly.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 287–296 (docstring 287–288, statement 289–290, proof 291–296), proof length 6 lines

#### `lemma Roth.mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux`

### `theorem Roth.mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {θ r : ℝ} {l : ℕ}, (l : ℝ) ≤ θ * r → (l : ℝ) * θ / 2 ≤ ∑ ν ∈ Finset.range l, max 0 (θ - ν / r)`
- **What**: If l ≤ θ r, then the truncated arithmetic-progression sum ∑_{ν<l} max(0, θ - ν/r) is at least lθ/2. This is the case "θ r_p ≥ l" of the combined estimate in §5, proof of Lemma 6 of the source (the sum has all l terms, average term ≥ θ/2).
- **How**: Case l = 0 by `simp`. Case θ ≤ 0: the left side is ≤ 0 (`div_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg`, `mul_nonpos_of_nonneg_of_nonpos`) and the right side is ≥ 0 (`Finset.sum_nonneg`, `le_max_left`). Otherwise r > 0 by `pos_of_mul_pos_right`; drop the `max` with `Finset.sum_le_sum` + `le_max_right`, expand ∑(θ - ν/r) = lθ - (∑ν)/r (`Finset.sum_sub_distrib`, `Finset.sum_const`, `Finset.card_range`, `nsmul_eq_mul`, `Finset.sum_div`), clear denominators (`div_add_div`, `div_le_iff₀`), and close with `linarith only` using the cast of the Gauss identity `Finset.sum_range_id_mul_two l` (∑_{ν<l} ν · 2 = l(l-1)) and l(θr - l) ≥ 0.
- **Hypotheses**: `hl : (l : ℝ) ≤ θ * r`. No sign hypotheses on θ or r (handled by case split: θ ≤ 0 is trivial, θ > 0 forces r > 0 when l > 0).
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib lemmas)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean only (`grep -rn mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux Roth/`): `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux` (private, l.343; use at l.354, boundary case θr = l) and `min_le_sum_max` (public, l.362; use at l.369, case l ≤ θr). Transitively via `min_le_sum_max`: `index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:556, use at l.586) and its downstream (Lemma 6).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 304–320, proof length 15 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The hypothesis is stronger than needed: in the θ, r > 0 case the inequality lθ - l(l-1)/(2r) ≥ lθ/2 holds iff l - 1 ≤ θr, so `l ≤ θ * r + 1` would suffice; the stronger form matches the source case split and both call sites, so no change is warranted.

#### `lemma Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux`

### `theorem Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {θ r : ℝ}, 0 < r → 0 ≤ θ → ∀ {l : ℕ}, θ * r < (l : ℝ) → r * θ ^ 2 / 3 ≤ ∑ ν ∈ Finset.range l, max 0 (θ - ν / r)`
- **What**: If r > 0, θ ≥ 0 and θ r < l, then the truncated arithmetic-progression sum ∑_{ν<l} max(0, θ - ν/r) is at least r θ²/3. This is the case "θ r_p < l" of the combined estimate in §5, proof of Lemma 6 of the source, but without the source's assumption θ r_p > 10: the inner bound (θr)²/3 ≤ ∑_{ν ≤ ⌊θr⌋} (θr - ν) is proved for every θr ≥ 0, replacing the source's two FACTs (⌊a⌋²/2 ≤ ∑ and a²/3 < ⌊a⌋²/2 for a > 10).
- **How**: Put n = ⌊θ r⌋₊, with `Nat.floor_le` (n ≤ θr) and `Nat.lt_floor_add_one` (θr < n + 1). Cast the Gauss identity `Finset.sum_range_id_mul_two (n + 1)` to ℝ. Inner claim `hbound`: (θr)²/3 ≤ ∑_{ν<n+1} (θr - ν), by expanding the sum (`Finset.sum_sub_distrib`, `Finset.sum_const`, `Finset.card_range`, `nsmul_eq_mul`), generalising θr to a, and a `linear_combination` certificate using the Gauss identity, `sq_nonneg a`, n ≥ 0 and (a - n)(n + 1 - a) ≥ 0. Then a `calc`: rewrite rθ²/3 = (θr)²/3 / r (`eq_div_iff`), divide `hbound` by r (`div_le_div_iff_of_pos_right`), distribute the division (`Finset.sum_div`, `sub_div`, `mul_div_cancel_right₀`) and bound each term by its positive part (`Finset.sum_le_sum`, `le_max_right`), and finally enlarge the index range from n + 1 to l (`Finset.sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg`, `Finset.range_subset_range`, `Nat.floor_lt`, `le_max_left`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hr : 0 < r` (needed for the division by r; the r ≤ 0 case is handled trivially by the caller), `hθ : 0 ≤ θ` (necessary: for θ < 0 < r the right side is 0 and the left side is positive), `hl : θ * r < l` (strict is needed for ⌊θr⌋ + 1 ≤ l; the equality case θr = l is handled by the caller via `mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux`).
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib lemmas)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean only (`grep -rn mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux Roth/`): `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux` (private, l.347; use at l.353, case θr < l). Transitively: `min_le_sum_max` (public, l.362; use at l.370), then `index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:586) and Lemma 6 downstream.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 322–345, proof length 22 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). Its inline `hbound` is strictly stronger than the composition of the public `floor_sq_div_two_le_sum` and `sq_div_three_lt_floor_sq_div_two` (no a > 10 needed); those two public theorems are now unused in the project (see their reports, both junk REMOVE). `hbound` could be extracted as a public lemma `sq_div_three_le_sum_range_floor` replacing them, but it has only this one use.

#### `lemma Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux`

### `theorem Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {θ r : ℝ}, 0 ≤ θ → ∀ {l : ℕ}, θ * r ≤ (l : ℝ) → r * θ ^ 2 / 3 ≤ ∑ ν ∈ Finset.range l, max 0 (θ - ν / r)`
- **What**: If θ ≥ 0 and θ r ≤ l, then the truncated arithmetic-progression sum ∑_{ν<l} max(0, θ - ν/r) is at least r θ²/3. This is the case "θ r_p < l" of the combined estimate in §5, proof of Lemma 6 of the source ("∑_{ν<l} max(0, θ - ν/r_p) ≥ min(lθ/2, r_p θ²/3)"), extended to the closed case θ r ≤ l and to arbitrary real r, with no θ r_p > 10 assumption.
- **How**: Three-way case dispatch. (i) r ≤ 0: left side ≤ 0 (`div_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg`, `mul_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg`, `sq_nonneg`) and right side ≥ 0 (`Finset.sum_nonneg`, `le_max_left`). (ii) θ r < l (via `lt_or_eq_of_le`): the private `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux`. (iii) θ r = l: from the private `mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux` (lθ/2 ≤ ∑) and lθ = rθ² (`congrArg (· * θ) hl`), rθ²/3 ≤ rθ²/2 = lθ/2, closed by `linarith only` with `mul_nonneg hr.le (sq_nonneg θ)`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hθ : 0 ≤ θ` (necessary: for θ < 0 < r with l = 0 the right side is 0 and the left side is positive), `hl : θ * r ≤ l` (closed form; the only caller actually has the strict form). No sign hypothesis on r (case r ≤ 0 is trivial).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux` (private, Roth/Theta.lean:322), `Roth.mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux` (private, Roth/Theta.lean:304)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean only (`grep -rn mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux Roth/`): `min_le_sum_max` (public, l.362; single use at l.370, case θ ≤ 0 excluded and l > θ rp strict, passed as `hl.le`). Transitively: `index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:556, use at l.586) and Lemma 6 downstream.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 347–355, proof length 7 lines
- **Notes**: Declared `private lemma` (kind recorded as theorem). The only call site supplies `hl.le` from a strict `θ * rp < l`, so branch (iii) (and with it the use of `mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux` at l.354) is never exercised from the project; branch (i) is exercised only when rp = 0 (rp : ℕ at the call site).

#### `theorem Roth.min_le_sum_max`

### `theorem Roth.min_le_sum_max`
- **Type**: `∀ {θ : ℝ} {rp : ℕ} (l : ℕ), min (↑l * θ / 2) (↑rp * θ ^ 2 / 3) ≤ ∑ ν ∈ Finset.range l, max 0 (θ - ↑ν / ↑rp)`
- **What**: For all real θ and natural l, r_p: the sum of positive parts ∑_{ν<l} max(0, θ - ν/r_p) is at least min(lθ/2, r_p θ²/3). This is the "Combining everything" bound of clean.tex §5, proof of Lemma 6 (l.880–910), stated without the source's hypotheses θ > 0 and θ r_p > 10.
- **How**: Case split. (i) θ ≤ 0: `min_le_left`, then lθ/2 ≤ 0 (`div_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg`, `mul_nonpos_of_nonneg_of_nonpos`) ≤ sum (`Finset.sum_nonneg`, `le_max_left`). (ii) l ≤ θ r_p: `min_le_left` with the private `mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux` (Gauss sum `Finset.sum_range_id_mul_two`). (iii) θ r_p < l: `min_le_right` with the private `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux` (which uses `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux`, a floor/Gauss-sum argument).
- **Hypotheses**: none (θ, r_p, l arbitrary). The source's assumptions θ > 0 and θ r_p > 10 are not needed.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.mul_div_two_le_sum_max_aux` (private, Roth/Theta.lean:304), `Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux` (private, Roth/Theta.lean:347); transitively `Roth.mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux` (private, l.322).
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:586). `grep -rn min_le_sum_max Roth/` gives only l.362 and l.586 (other hits are the `_aux` helpers' names).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 357–370 (docstring 357–361, statement 362–363, proof 364–370), proof length 7 lines

The sibling report for `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_aux` recommends inlining that helper here (rp = 0 split + direct call to `mul_sq_div_three_le_sum_max_of_pos_aux`).

#### `theorem Roth.sum_max_sub_le_index_det`

### `theorem Roth.sum_max_sub_le_index_det`
- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {R : MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℝ} {l : ℕ} (D : Fin l → (τ →₀ ℕ)) (hD : ∀ μ, (D μ).degree ≤ μ) (hdet : (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ hasseDeriv ((D μ).mapDomain some + Finsupp.single none ν) R).det ≠ 0) (α : Option τ → ℝ) {r : Option τ → ℕ} (hr : ∀ j, 0 < r j) {δ : ℝ} (hδ : ∀ d : τ →₀ ℕ, d.degree ≤ l - 1 → weight (r ∘ some) d ≤ δ) : ∑ ν ∈ Finset.range l, max 0 (index R α r - ν / r none) - l * δ ≤ index (Matrix.of fun μ ν ↦ hasseDeriv (...) R).det α r`
- **What**: For a real polynomial `R` in variables `Option τ` (`none` playing the role of `x_p`), the determinant `F = det(Δ_{D_μ} ∂_{x_p}^{(ν)} R)_{μ,ν<l}` (Hasse derivatives) has Roth index at least `∑_{ν<l} max(0, ind R - ν/r_p) - lδ`, provided `F ≠ 0`, `D_μ` has total degree `≤ μ`, and `δ` bounds the weights of all multi-indices of degree `≤ l - 1`. This is the displayed conclusion "We have proved ind(F) ≥ …" of clean.tex §5, proof of Lemma 6 (l.814–840).
- **How**: (i) Entrywise bound `hterm`: for each nonzero entry, `index_sub_weight_le_index_hasseDeriv` gives `ind R - weight ≤ ind(entry)`; `weight_add`, `weight_mapDomain`, `weight_single` split the weight into `weight(D μ) + ν/r_p`, bounded via `hδ`/`hD`; `index_nonneg` and `weight_nonneg` give the `max 0` form. (ii) Expand `det` with `Matrix.det_apply'`; `le_index_sum` reduces to each nonzero permutation term; `index_C_mul` removes the sign; `index_prod` splits the product; `Fin.sum_univ_eq_sum_range`, `Fin.sum_const`, `Finset.sum_sub_distrib`, `Finset.sum_le_sum` finish.
- **Hypotheses**: `hD` and `hδ` (only used through `weight (r ∘ some) (D μ) ≤ δ`, see evidence); `hdet` (needed: Lean has `index 0 = 0`); `hr` (needed by `index_prod`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.index`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:57), `Roth.weight`, `Roth.index_sub_weight_le_index_hasseDeriv`, `Roth.weight_add`, `Roth.weight_mapDomain`, `Roth.weight_single`, `Roth.index_nonneg`, `Roth.weight_nonneg`, `Roth.le_index_sum`, `Roth.index_C_mul`, `Roth.index_prod`.
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:576, `have hlower := sum_max_sub_le_index_det D hD (mapped_det_ne_zero_aux hU hV hF) α hr0 ...`). No other occurrence in `Roth/` or `Roth.lean`.

#### `lemma Roth.index_separated_mul_le_aux`

### `theorem Roth.index_separated_mul_le_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {r : Option τ → ℕ}, (∀ j, 0 < r j) → ∀ {q : Option τ → ℕ} {h : Option τ → ℤ}, (∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j : ℤ)) → ∀ {M : ℝ} {l : ℕ}, 0 < l → ∀ {U : MvPolynomial τ ℤ}, U ∈ RSet M (fun j ↦ l * r (some j)) → ∀ {V : Polynomial ℤ}, V ≠ 0 → V.natDegree ≤ l * r none → (∀ b, |(V.coeff b : ℝ)| ≤ M) → index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) (rename some U * Polynomial.aeval (X none : MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℤ) V)) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r ≤ l * lem6Φ q r M l`
- **What**: For a product F = U(x_1, …, x_{p-1}) · V(x_p) of an integer polynomial U ∈ ℛ_{p-1}(M; l r_1, …, l r_{p-1}) and a nonzero one-variable integer polynomial V of degree ≤ l r_p with coefficients bounded by M, the index of F at the point (h_j/q_j) with weights r is at most l Φ, where Φ = Θ_1(M; q_p; l r_p) + Θ_{p-1}(M; q_1..q_{p-1}; l r_1..l r_{p-1}). This is the source's Lemma 4-2 (additivity of the index for separated variables) combined with the definition of Θ as a supremum, Step 3 of eq. (17) in the proof of Lemma 6.
- **How**: Transport V to `V' : MvPolynomial Unit ℤ` via `(uniqueAlgEquiv ℤ Unit).symm` and show `V' ∈ RSet M (fun _ ↦ l * r none)` using `degreeOf_uniqueAlgEquiv_symm` and `MvPolynomial.coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv_symm`; rewrite `aeval (X none) V` as a `rename` with `aeval_X_eq_rename`, push `map` through with `_root_.map_mul`, `map_rename`; split the index with `index_mul` (nonzeroness via `map_injective`, `rename_injective`), drop the renamings with `index_rename` (injective `some` and the constant map `Unit → none`); bound each factor by `index_le_Θ` after rescaling weights with `index_mul_left_weights`, and finish with `div_le_iff₀'`, unfolding `lem6Φ` and `add_le_add`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hr0` (positive weights, needed by `index_mul`); `hh` (coprimality, needed by `index_le_Θ`); `hl : 0 < l` (to clear the `/ l` from `index_mul_left_weights`); `hU` membership in RSet; `hV`, `hVd`, `hVc` are the unpacked RSet conditions for V (stated for a `Polynomial ℤ` rather than an `MvPolynomial Unit ℤ`). All hypotheses are used. Section variable `[DecidableEq τ]` is omitted (`omit ... in`); `classical` is used inside.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`, `Roth.lem6Φ`, `Roth.Θ` (via lem6Φ), `Roth.index_le_Θ`, `Roth.index_mul`, `Roth.index_rename`, `Roth.index_mul_left_weights`, `aeval_X_eq_rename` (Roth/Separation.lean:137), `degreeOf_uniqueAlgEquiv_symm` (Roth/Separation.lean:174), `MvPolynomial.coeff_uniqueAlgEquiv_symm` (Roth/Separation.lean:169)
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean: `index_det_le` (l.468; `exact index_separated_mul_le_aux hr0 hh hl ⟨hU, hUd, hUc⟩ hV hVd hVc` at l.491). `grep -rn index_separated_mul_le_aux Roth/` returns only the definition (l.414) and this use. Transitively: `index_le_lem6` (l.556, via l.578) and `Θ_option_le` (Lemma 6, l.593), used at l.920 for Lemma 7.

#### `theorem Roth.index_det_le`

### `theorem Roth.index_det_le`
- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {r : Option τ → ℕ} (hr0 : ∀ j, 0 < r j) {r₁ : ℕ} (hr₁ : ∀ j, r j ≤ r₁) {q : Option τ → ℕ} {B : ℝ} {R : MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℤ} (hR : R ∈ RSet B r) {h : Option τ → ℤ} (hh : ∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j : ℤ)) {l : ℕ} (hl : 1 ≤ l) {D : Fin l → (τ →₀ ℕ)} {U : MvPolynomial τ ℤ} {V : Polynomial ℤ} (hU : U ≠ 0) (hV : V ≠ 0) (hUd : ∀ j, degreeOf j U ≤ l * r (some j)) (hVd : V.natDegree ≤ l * r none) (hF : (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ hasseDeriv ((D μ).mapDomain some + Finsupp.single none ν) R).det = rename some U * Polynomial.aeval (X none) V) : index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) (Matrix.of …).det) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r ≤ l * lem6Φ q r (lem6M (Fintype.card τ + 1) r₁ B l) l`
- **What**: Let `R ∈ ℛ_p(B; r)` with all `r_j ≤ r₁`, and suppose the generalised Wronskian `F = det(Δ_{D_μ} (1/ν!) ∂^ν_{x_p} R)_{μ,ν<l}` factors as `U(x_1, …, x_{p-1}) V(x_p)` with `U, V ≠ 0`, `deg_{x_j} U ≤ l r_j`, `deg V ≤ l r_p`. Then `ind(F; h/q; r) ≤ l Φ`, where `Φ = Θ_1(M; q_p; l r_p) + Θ_{p-1}(M; q'; l r')` and `M = (r₁+1)^{pl} l! B^l 2^{p r₁ l}`. Source: clean.tex §5, proof of Lemma 6, eq. (17) (l.734–777).
- **How**: Step 1: coefficients of `F` are bounded by `M` via `Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le` (Lemma 3) after `simpa` with `lem6M`, `Finset.prod_const`, `Fintype.card_option`, `pow_mul`. Step 2: coefficients of `U` and `V` are bounded by `M`, using `Roth.coeff_rename_mul_aeval` (coefficient of `U·V` in separated variables is a product), `Int.one_le_abs` on the leading coefficient of `V` / a nonzero coefficient of `U` (`MvPolynomial.ne_zero_iff`), `le_mul_of_one_le_right/left`. Step 3: `rw [hF]` and apply the private `Roth.index_separated_mul_le_aux` (Lemma 4-2 + `index_le_Θ` + weight scaling).
- **Hypotheses**: `hr0` (for `index_mul` inside the aux lemma); `hr₁` (uniform degree bound for Lemma 3); `hR` (only the degree and coefficient clauses are used; `R ≠ 0` is superfluous, see evidence); `hh` (coprimality for `index_le_Θ`); `hl` (to clear `/ l`); `hU`, `hV`, `hUd`, `hVd`, `hF` (Lemma 2 output). `[DecidableEq τ]` omitted.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index`, `Roth.lem6Φ`, `Roth.lem6M`, `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean), `Roth.abs_coeff_det_hasseDeriv_le` (Roth/CoeffNorm.lean:258), `Roth.coeff_rename_mul_aeval` (Roth/Separation.lean:123), `Roth.index_separated_mul_le_aux` (Roth/Theta.lean:414, private)
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:578, `have hupper := index_det_le hr0 hr₁ hR hh hl hU hV hUd hVd hF`). No other occurrence in `Roth/` or `Roth.lean`. Transitively feeds `Roth.Θ_option_le` (Lemma 6, l.593).
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 452–491 (docstring 452–455, statement 457–467, proof 468–491), proof length 24 lines

#### `lemma Roth.weight_le_delta_aux`

### `lemma Roth.weight_le_delta_aux`
- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {δ : ℝ} (hδ : 0 ≤ δ) {r : Option τ → ℕ} (hratio : ∀ j, (r none : ℝ) ≤ δ * r (some j)) {d : τ →₀ ℕ} (hd : d.degree ≤ r none) : Roth.weight (r ∘ some) d ≤ δ`
- **What**: If every parameter `r (some j)` of the first `p - 1` variables satisfies `r_p ≤ δ r_j` (the source's ratio condition, with `r_p = r none`), then every multi-index `d` in those variables of total degree at most `r_p` has weight `∑ j, d j / r_j ≤ δ`. This is the source's estimate `∑_{j<p} i_j / r_j ≤ w / r_{p-1}`-style step in the proof of Lemma 6, recast with a real ratio `δ`.
- **How**: Case split on `r none = 0`: then `hd` forces `d.degree = 0`, so `d = 0` by `Finsupp.degree_eq_zero_iff` and the weight is `0 ≤ δ` (`simpa [weight] using hδ`). Otherwise `r none > 0`, and a four-step `calc`: unfold `weight` (`rfl`); termwise `d j / r_j ≤ d j * δ / r_p` via `Finset.sum_le_sum`, `div_le_div_iff₀` (positivity of `r_j` from `pos_of_mul_pos_right` applied to `hratio j`) and `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (hratio j)`; regroup to `δ * d.degree / r_p` with `Finset.sum_div`, `Finset.sum_mul`, `Finsupp.degree_eq_sum`, `Nat.cast_sum`; conclude with `div_le_iff₀` and `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hd hδ`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hδ : 0 ≤ δ` (needed: with `δ < 0` and `d = 0` the conclusion `0 ≤ δ` fails); `hratio` (the ratio condition, needed for the termwise comparison and also yields `r (some j) > 0` when `r none > 0`); `hd : d.degree ≤ r none`. Section variable `[Fintype τ]` (needed for the finite sum in `weight`); `[DecidableEq τ]` is omitted via `omit ... in`.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.weight` (Roth/Box.lean:71). All other identifiers are mathlib (`Finsupp.degree_eq_zero_iff`, `Finsupp.degree_eq_sum`, `Finset.sum_le_sum`, `Finset.sum_div`, `Finset.sum_mul`, `Nat.cast_sum`, `div_le_div_iff₀`, `div_le_iff₀`, `pos_of_mul_pos_right`, `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left`, `Nat.pos_of_ne_zero`, `Nat.eq_zero_of_le_zero`, `Nat.cast_nonneg`).
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:577), where `fun d hd ↦ weight_le_delta_aux hδ0 hratio (hd.trans (Nat.sub_le_iff_le_add.2 hl'))` discharges the hypothesis `hδ : ∀ d, d.degree ≤ l - 1 → weight (r ∘ some) d ≤ δ` of `Roth.sum_max_sub_le_index_det`. No other uses project-wide (`grep -rn weight_le_delta_aux Roth/`: declaration line 494 and call line 577 only).

Relationship to `Roth.weight_le_degree_div` (Roth/Box.lean:118, public, unused): that lemma states the same kind of bound with a natural-number common lower bound `m ≤ r j` and conclusion `weight r d ≤ d.degree / m`; it cannot express the real ratio `r_p ≤ δ r_j`, which is why Lemma 6 uses this helper instead (its report, Roth.weight_le_degree_div.md, flags this helper as the one actually used and recommends REMOVE for the Box lemma).

#### `lemma Roth.mapped_det_ne_zero_aux`

### `lemma Roth.mapped_det_ne_zero_aux`
- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} {R : MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℤ} {l : ℕ} {D : Fin l → (τ →₀ ℕ)} {U : MvPolynomial τ ℤ} {V : Polynomial ℤ} (hU : U ≠ 0) (hV : V ≠ 0) (hF : (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ hasseDeriv ((D μ).mapDomain some + Finsupp.single none (ν : ℕ)) R).det = rename some U * Polynomial.aeval (X none) V) : (Matrix.of fun μ ν : Fin l ↦ hasseDeriv ((D μ).mapDomain some + Finsupp.single none (ν : ℕ)) (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) R)).det ≠ 0`
- **What**: In the proof of Lemma 6 (source §5), the `l × l` determinant `F = det(Δ_{(D μ, ν)} R)` of Hasse derivatives of the integer polynomial `R` factors as `U(x_1,…,x_{p-1}) · V(x_p)` with `U, V ≠ 0` (from `exists_det_eq_mul`). This lemma concludes that the same determinant built from `R` viewed over `ℝ` is nonzero, which is the hypothesis `hdet` that `sum_max_sub_le_index_det` (the lower bound for `ind F`) requires.
- **How**: First `hmapdet`: mapping the integer determinant along `Int.castRingHom ℝ` gives the real determinant, via `RingHom.map_det` and entrywise `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map` (project, Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:134). Then rewrite the goal with `← hmapdet`, use injectivity of `MvPolynomial.map` (`map_injective _ Int.cast_injective`, `map_eq_zero_iff`) to reduce to the integer determinant, substitute `hF`, split `mul_eq_zero`/`not_or`, kill the `U` factor with `rename_injective _ (Option.some_injective _)`, and the `V` factor with `Roth.aeval_X_eq_rename` (Roth/Separation.lean:137) plus injectivity of `rename (fun _ : Unit ↦ none)` (`Subsingleton.elim`) and of `(MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv ℤ Unit).symm`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hU : U ≠ 0`, `hV : V ≠ 0` (both needed: either factor zero makes the determinant zero); `hF` the factorisation. Section variables `[Fintype τ] [DecidableEq τ]` are omitted (`omit ... in`), so the lemma holds for arbitrary `τ`. Implicitly uses that `ℤ` and `ℝ` are domains (via `mul_eq_zero` in `MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℤ`) and that `Int.cast : ℤ → ℝ` is injective.
- **Uses from project**: `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:57), `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:134), `Roth.aeval_X_eq_rename` (Roth/Separation.lean:137). All other identifiers are mathlib (`RingHom.map_det`, `Matrix.ext`, `map_eq_zero_iff`, `MvPolynomial.map_injective`, `Int.cast_injective`, `MvPolynomial.rename_injective`, `Option.some_injective`, `mul_eq_zero`, `not_or`, `MvPolynomial.uniqueAlgEquiv`, `AlgEquiv.injective`, `Subsingleton.elim`).
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:576), as the `hdet` argument of `Roth.sum_max_sub_le_index_det`. No other uses project-wide (`grep -rn mapped_det_ne_zero_aux Roth/`: declaration line 511 and call line 576 only).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 510–529 (`omit` line 510, statement 511–517, proof 518–529), proof length 12 lines
- **Notes**: No docstring (private). The last two proof lines re-derive, via the project lemma `aeval_X_eq_rename`, the injectivity of `Polynomial.aeval (X i)`, which mathlib already provides as `Polynomial.toMvPolynomial_injective` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Equiv.lean:897; `Polynomial.toMvPolynomial i` is by definition `aeval (X i)`). Verified in place with `lean_multi_attempt`: replacing line 528 by `exact (map_ne_zero_iff _ (Polynomial.toMvPolynomial_injective (R := ℤ) none)).2 hV` closes the goal (the only remaining error is "No goals" on the then-redundant line 529).

#### `lemma Roth.index_numeric_bound_aux`

### `lemma Roth.index_numeric_bound_aux`
- **Type**: `{θ Φ δ rp l : ℝ} (hΦ : 0 ≤ Φ) (hδ1 : δ ≤ 1) (hrp : 3 ≤ rp) (hl : 0 < l) (hl' : l ≤ rp + 1) (hmin : min (l * θ / 2) (rp * θ ^ 2 / 3) ≤ l * (Φ + δ)) : θ ≤ 2 * (Φ + √Φ + √δ)`
- **What**: The numerical conclusion of the source's Lemma 6 (§5). If `Φ ≥ 0`, `δ ≤ 1`, `r_p ≥ 3`, `0 < l ≤ r_p + 1` and `min(lθ/2, r_p θ²/3) ≤ l(Φ + δ)`, then `θ ≤ 2(Φ + Φ^{1/2} + δ^{1/2})`. In the paper this is the closing step "comparing the lower and upper bounds for the index of the Wronskian-type determinant `F`".
- **How**: `rcases min_le_iff.1 hmin`. Case 1 (`lθ/2 ≤ l(Φ+δ)`): cancel `l` with `mul_div_assoc`, `mul_le_mul_iff_right₀ hl`, `div_le_iff₀` to get `θ ≤ 2(Φ+δ)`, then `linarith only` with `Real.le_sqrt_self_iff.2 hδ1` (`δ ≤ √δ`) and `Real.sqrt_nonneg`. Case 2 (`r_p θ²/3 ≤ l(Φ+δ)`): from `hl'`, `hrp` get `3l ≤ 4 r_p`; a `linear_combination` (using `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right` and `nonneg_of_mul_nonneg_right` to get `Φ + δ ≥ 0`) plus `mul_le_mul_iff_right₀ hrp0` gives `θ² ≤ 4(Φ+δ)`; then `le_of_sq_le_sq` with a `linear_combination` of `Real.sq_sqrt'`, `Real.sq_sqrt hΦ`, `le_max_left` and `mul_nonneg (√Φ) (√δ)` yields `θ ≤ 2(√Φ + √δ)`, finished by `linarith only [hθ, hΦ]`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hΦ` (needed for `(√Φ)² = Φ` in case 2); `hδ1` (needed for `δ ≤ √δ` in case 1); `hrp` (gives `r_p > 0` and, with `hl'`, `3l ≤ 4r_p`); `hl` (cancelling `l`, and nonnegativity of `Φ+δ`); `hl'` (size comparison). No nonnegativity of `δ` is required because `Real.sqrt` truncates (`Real.sq_sqrt'` with `max δ 0`). All are used per inspection of the proof; no Lean minimality experiment was run.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (all identifiers are mathlib: `min_le_iff`, `mul_div_assoc`, `mul_le_mul_iff_right₀`, `div_le_iff₀`, `Real.le_sqrt_self_iff`, `Real.sqrt_nonneg`, `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right`, `nonneg_of_mul_nonneg_right`, `div_nonneg`, `mul_nonneg`, `sq_nonneg`, `le_of_sq_le_sq`, `le_max_left`, `Real.sq_sqrt'`, `Real.sq_sqrt`; tactics `linarith`, `linear_combination`, `positivity`, `norm_num`).
- **Used by**: `Roth.index_le_lem6` (Roth/Theta.lean:582), via `apply index_numeric_bound_aux (Φ := Φ) (l := (l : ℝ)) ...`, with `hmin` supplied by `Roth.min_le_sum_max` chained with the lower/upper index bounds `sum_max_sub_le_index_det` and `index_det_le`. No other uses project-wide (`grep -rn index_numeric_bound_aux Roth/`: lines 531 and 582 only).
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 531–551 (statement 531–533, proof 534–551), proof length 18 lines
- **Notes**: No docstring (private); a comment line explains the `δ ≤ √δ` trick. The lemma deliberately works over arbitrary reals `rp l`, so the caller casts from `ℕ`. Its role mirrors `Roth.min_le_sum_max` (Roth/Theta.lean:362), which supplies the `min` lower bound.

#### `theorem Roth.index_le_lem6`

### `theorem Roth.index_le_lem6`
- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {δ : ℝ} (hδ0 : 0 ≤ δ) (hδ1 : δ ≤ 1) {r : Option τ → ℕ} (hrp : 3 ≤ (r none : ℝ)) (hratio : ∀ j, (r none : ℝ) ≤ δ * r (some j)) {r₁ : ℕ} (hr₁ : ∀ j, r j ≤ r₁) {q : Option τ → ℕ} (_hq : ∀ j, 0 < q j) {B : ℝ} {R : MvPolynomial (Option τ) ℤ} (hR : R ∈ RSet B r) {h : Option τ → ℤ} (hh : ∀ j, IsCoprime (h j) (q j : ℤ)) : ∃ l, 1 ≤ l ∧ l ≤ r none + 1 ∧ index (map (Int.castRingHom ℝ) R) (fun j ↦ (h j : ℝ) / q j) r ≤ 2 * (lem6Φ q r (lem6M (Fintype.card τ + 1) r₁ B l) l + √(lem6Φ q r (lem6M (Fintype.card τ + 1) r₁ B l) l) + √δ)`
- **What**: Lemma 6 of the source (clean.tex §5, Lemma `roth:lem6`, l.704–) for a single polynomial and point: if `R ∈ ℛ_p(B; r)`, `r_p ≥ 3`, `r_p ≤ δ r_j` for `j < p`, `r_j ≤ r₁`, and `gcd(h_j, q_j) = 1`, then for some `1 ≤ l ≤ r_p + 1`, `ind(R; h/q; r) ≤ 2(Φ + Φ^{1/2} + δ^{1/2})` with `Φ = Θ_1(M; q_p; l r_p) + Θ_{p-1}(M; q'; l r')`, `M = (r₁+1)^{pl} l! B^l 2^{p r₁ l}`.
- **How**: Obtain `l`, `D`, `U`, `V` with the generalised Wronskian `F = U(x') V(x_p)` from `Roth.exists_det_eq_mul` (Lemma 2); lower bound `∑_{ν<l} max(0, ind R − ν/r_p) − lδ ≤ ind F` from `Roth.sum_max_sub_le_index_det` (with private `mapped_det_ne_zero_aux`, `weight_le_delta_aux`); upper bound `ind F ≤ lΦ` from `Roth.index_det_le` (eq. (17)); combine with `Roth.min_le_sum_max` and conclude with the private numeric lemma `index_numeric_bound_aux` (case split on the `min`).
- **Hypotheses**: `hδ0`, `hδ1`, `hrp`, `hratio` (weight comparisons, used for `r j > 0` and the weight-≤-δ bound); `hr₁` (uniform degree bound for Lemma 3 via `index_det_le`); `hR` (Lemma 2 needs `R ≠ 0` and the degree bounds); `hh` (coprimality for `index_le_Θ`); `_hq` (positivity of the `q j`) is **unused** — see evidence.
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.RSet`, `Roth.index` (Roth/Index.lean:58), `Roth.Θ_nonneg`, `Roth.lem6Φ`, `Roth.lem6M`, `Roth.exists_det_eq_mul` (Roth/Separation.lean:542), `Roth.sum_max_sub_le_index_det` (Theta.lean:376), `Roth.mapped_det_ne_zero_aux` (Theta.lean:511, private), `Roth.weight_le_delta_aux` (Theta.lean:494, private), `Roth.index_det_le` (Theta.lean:457), `MvPolynomial.hasseDeriv_map` (Roth/HasseDeriv.lean:134), `Roth.index_numeric_bound_aux` (Theta.lean:531, private), `Roth.min_le_sum_max` (Theta.lean:362)
- **Used by**: `Roth.Θ_option_le` (Roth/Theta.lean:600, `obtain ⟨l, hl, hl', hle⟩ := index_le_lem6 hδ0 hδ1 hrp hratio hr₁ hq hR hh`). No other occurrence in `Roth/` or `Roth.lean`. Transitively feeds Lemma 7 via `Θ_option_le` (Theta.lean:920).

- **Type**: `{τ : Type*} [Fintype τ] {δ : ℝ} (hδ0 : 0 ≤ δ) (hδ1 : δ ≤ 1) {r : Option τ → ℕ} (hrp : 3 ≤ (r none : ℝ)) (hratio : ∀ j, (r none : ℝ) ≤ δ * r (some j)) {r₁ : ℕ} (hr₁ : ∀ j, r j ≤ r₁) {q : Option τ → ℕ} (hq : ∀ j, 0 < q j) {B : ℝ} (hB : 1 ≤ B) : Θ B q r ≤ 2 * (Finset.Icc 1 (r none + 1)).sup' _ (fun l ↦ lem6Φ q r (lem6M (Fintype.card τ + 1) r₁ B l) l + √(lem6Φ q r (lem6M (Fintype.card τ + 1) r₁ B l) l) + √δ)`

#### `lemma Roth.factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux`

### `theorem Roth.factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ (p r₁ l : ℕ), l ≤ 2 ^ r₁ → (r₁ + 1) ^ (p * l) * l.factorial * 2 ^ (p * l * r₁) ≤ 2 ^ ((2 * p + 1) * r₁ * l)` (declared `private lemma`, all in `ℕ`).
- **What**: If `l ≤ 2^{r₁}` then `(r₁+1)^{pl} · l! · 2^{p l r₁} ≤ 2^{(2p+1) r₁ l}`. These are exactly the factors of `lem6M p r₁ B l = (r₁+1)^{pl} l! B^l 2^{p l r₁}` other than `B^l`; the lemma is the "the remaining factors are at most `2^{(2p+1) r₁ l}`" step of the source's proof of Lemma 7 (§5).
- **How**: Two-step `calc`. Step 1: `gcongr` reduces to `r₁ + 1 ≤ 2^{r₁}` (closed by `Nat.lt_two_pow_self`) and `l! ≤ (2^{r₁})^l` (closed by `Nat.factorial_le_pow l` composed with `Nat.pow_le_pow_left hl`). Step 2: the identity `(2^{r₁})^{pl} (2^{r₁})^l 2^{p l r₁} = 2^{(2p+1) r₁ l}` by `rw [← pow_mul, ← pow_mul, ← pow_add, ← pow_add]` and `ring_nf`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hl : l ≤ 2 ^ r₁` (used only for the factorial bound `l! ≤ l^l ≤ (2^{r₁})^l`). Necessary in some form: without a bound on `l` relative to `r₁` the claim fails (e.g. `p = 0, r₁ = 0, l = 2`: `2! = 2 > 1 = 2^0`). Already the natural weak form (the call site has the stronger `l ≤ r₁` and weakens it).
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib/core: `Nat.lt_two_pow_self`, `Nat.factorial_le_pow`, `Nat.pow_le_pow_left`, `pow_mul`, `pow_add`, `gcongr`, `ring_nf`).
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean `Roth.lem6M_lt` only (l.658, `exact_mod_cast factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux p r₁ l (hl'.trans Nat.lt_two_pow_self.le)`, to get the real-valued `hnatR`). `grep -rn factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux Roth/` returns only the definition (l.609) and this use.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 609–618, proof length 8 lines (611–618)
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`; would also be caught by the Step 9a pre-filter. Stated in `ℕ` and cast to `ℝ` at the call site, which keeps the arithmetic in the easier semiring.

#### `lemma Roth.two_pow_lt_rpow_aux`

### `theorem Roth.two_pow_lt_rpow_aux`
- **Type**: `∀ {p r₁ l : ℕ}, 0 < p → ∀ {δ q : ℝ}, 0 < δ → 0 ≤ q → (p : ℝ) * (2 * p + 1) / δ ≤ Real.log q → 0 < l * r₁ → (2 : ℝ) ^ ((2 * p + 1) * r₁ * l) < q ^ (δ / p * (l * r₁))` (declared `private lemma`; left side is a natural-number power `Monoid.npow`, right side `Real.rpow`).
- **What**: If `p > 0`, `δ > 0`, `q ≥ 0`, `log q ≥ p(2p+1)/δ` and `l r₁ > 0`, then `2^{(2p+1) r₁ l} < q^{(δ/p) l r₁}`. This is the step of the source's proof of Lemma 7 (§5) that absorbs the factor `2^{(2p+1) r₁ l}` bounding the non-`B` part of `lem6M` into the extra `q₁^{δ l r₁ / p}`, giving `M < q₁^{δ(1+1/p) l r₁}`.
- **How**: First upgrades `0 ≤ q` to `0 < q` via `Real.log_pos_iff` (since `log q ≥ p(2p+1)/δ > 0`, so `q > 1`). Proves `log 2 < 1` from `Real.log_lt_sub_one_of_pos`, and `2p+1 ≤ log q · (δ/p)` from `hlog` by `mul_div_assoc'`, `le_div_iff₀`, `div_le_iff₀`, `linarith`. Then rewrites both sides as exponentials (`Real.rpow_natCast`, `Real.rpow_def_of_pos` twice, `Real.exp_lt_exp`) and closes the resulting `log 2 · (2p+1) r₁ l < log q · (δ/p) · l r₁` by a 3-step `calc` using `push_cast`/`ring`, `mul_lt_mul_of_pos_right` and `mul_lt_of_lt_one_left` chained with the bound `h3`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hp : 0 < p` (needed: the division `δ / p` and the `le_div_iff₀` step; for `p = 0` the right side is `q^0 = 1` while the left is `2^{r₁ l} > 1`). `hδ : 0 < δ` (used in `div_le_iff₀`). `hq : 0 ≤ q` (needed for `Real.log_pos_iff`; for negative `q` Mathlib's `log q = log |q|` and `Real.rpow` of a negative base involves a cosine factor, so the claim is not available in that form). `hlog` (the quantitative core). `hlr : 0 < l * r₁` (needed for strictness: if `l r₁ = 0` both sides equal 1). All hypotheses are used and natural for the call site.
- **Uses from project**: `[]` (only mathlib: `Real.log_pos_iff`, `Real.log_lt_sub_one_of_pos`, `le_div_iff₀`, `div_le_iff₀`, `mul_div_assoc'`, `Real.rpow_natCast`, `Real.rpow_def_of_pos`, `Real.exp_lt_exp`, `mul_lt_mul_of_pos_right`, `mul_lt_of_lt_one_left`, tactics `positivity`, `linarith`, `push_cast`, `ring`).
- **Used by**: Roth/Theta.lean `Roth.lem6M_lt` only (l.668, `two_pow_lt_rpow_aux hp hδ0 hq1pos.le hq₁ (Nat.mul_pos (by lia) (by lia))`, the strict step of its `calc`). `grep -rn two_pow_lt_rpow_aux Roth/` returns only the definition (l.621) and this use.
- **Visibility**: private
- **Lines**: 621–644, proof length 21 lines (624–644)
- **Notes**: Name ends in `_aux`, so the Step 9a pre-filter would also fire. Sibling of `factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux` (l.609); together they split `lem6M_lt` into an ℕ-arithmetic part and a real-analytic part. Minor golf opportunity (not required): `hlog2` could come from mathlib's `Real.log_two_lt_d9` plus `norm_num` instead of `Real.log_lt_sub_one_of_pos`.

#### `theorem Roth.lem6M_lt`

### `theorem Roth.lem6M_lt`
- **Type**: `{p : ℕ} (hp : 0 < p) {δ : ℝ} (hδ0 : 0 < δ) {r₁ q₁ : ℕ} (hq₁ : (p : ℝ) * (2 * p + 1) / δ ≤ Real.log q₁) {l : ℕ} (hl : 1 ≤ l) (hl' : l ≤ r₁) : lem6M p r₁ ((q₁ : ℝ) ^ (δ * r₁)) l < (q₁ : ℝ) ^ (δ * (1 + 1 / p) * (l * r₁))`
- **What**: If `log q₁ ≥ p(2p+1)/δ` and `1 ≤ l ≤ r₁`, then the Lemma 6 constant `M = (r₁+1)^{pl} l! 2^{plr₁} B^l` with `B = q₁^{δ r₁}` satisfies `M < q₁^{δ₁ l r₁}` where `δ₁ = δ(1 + 1/p)`.
- **How**: Shows `q₁ > 0` from the log hypothesis (`Real.log_pos_iff`). Bounds the non-`B` factors by `2^{(2p+1) r₁ l}` using private `factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux` (cast with `exact_mod_cast`, with `l ≤ 2^{r₁}` from `Nat.lt_two_pow_self`). Then uses private `two_pow_lt_rpow_aux` for `2^{(2p+1) r₁ l} < q₁^{(δ/p) l r₁}`, and merges exponents with `Real.rpow_mul_natCast` and `Real.rpow_add`. The chain is a 4-step `calc`.
- **Hypotheses**: `hp` (p > 0, divides by p); `hδ0`; `hq₁` (forces `q₁ > 1` and gives `2p+1 ≤ (δ/p) log q₁`); `hl` (needed for strictness, since at `l = 0` both sides equal 1); `hl'` (only used to get `l ≤ 2^{r₁}` and `r₁ > 0`).
- **Uses from project**: `Roth.lem6M`, `Roth.factorial_coefficient_le_two_pow_aux` (private), `Roth.two_pow_lt_rpow_aux` (private)
- **Used by**: `Roth.theta_phi_lt_aux` (private) at Roth/Theta.lean:871; no other uses in `Roth/`
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 646–667 (statement 649–651, proof 652–667, proof length 16 lines)
- **Notes**: Source is clean.tex §5, proof of Lemma 7, l.973–981. The docstring cites it correctly.

#### `theorem Roth.lem7_Φ_bound`

### `theorem Roth.lem7_Φ_bound`
- **Type**: `{p : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) {δ : ℝ} (hδ0 : 0 < δ) (hδ1 : δ ≤ 1) : δ * (1 + 1 / p) + 10 ^ (p - 1) * (δ * (1 + 1 / p)) ^ ((1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ (p - 1)) < 3 * 10 ^ (p - 1) * δ ^ ((1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ (p - 1))`
- **What**: For `p ≥ 2` and `0 < δ ≤ 1`, with `δ₁ = δ(1 + 1/p)` and `e = (1/2)^{p-1}`, the inequality `δ₁ + 10^{p-1} δ₁^e < 3 · 10^{p-1} δ^e` holds.
- **How**: Sets `e`. Gets `e ≤ 1` (`pow_le_one₀`) and `1 ≤ 1 + 1/p ≤ 3/2` (`one_div_le_one_div_of_le`). Gets `δ ≤ δ^e` (`Real.rpow_le_rpow_of_exponent_ge`). Bounds `(δ(1+1/p))^e ≤ δ^e · 3/2` (`Real.mul_rpow`, `Real.rpow_le_rpow_of_exponent_le`). Then closes with one `linear_combination` of these facts, `le_self_pow₀` (`1 ≤ 10^{p-1}`) and `δ^e > 0` (`Real.rpow_pos_of_pos`).
- **Hypotheses**:
  - `hp`: needed for `1 + 1/p ≤ 3/2` and `10^{p-1} ≥ 10`. It fails at `p = 1`.
  - `hδ0`: needed for strictness. Both sides are 0 at `δ = 0`.
  - `hδ1`: needed for `δ ≤ δ^e`. It fails for large `δ`.
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: `Roth.theta_phi_lt_aux` (private) at Roth/Theta.lean:876. It has no other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 674–702 (statement 678–680, proof 681–702, proof length 22 lines)
- **Notes**: The source is clean.tex §5, proof of Lemma 7, l.1008–1019. The Lean proof goes through `3/2` where the paper uses `δ₁ < 2δ` and `(1+p⁻¹)^e < 2`. Both give the same final bound.

#### `theorem Roth.lem7_numeric`

### `theorem Roth.lem7_numeric`
- **Type**: `{p : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) {δ : ℝ} (hδ0 : 0 < δ) (hδ1 : δ ≤ 1) {Φ : ℝ} (hΦ : Φ ≤ 3 * 10 ^ (p - 1) * δ ^ ((1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ (p - 1))) : 2 * (Φ + √Φ + √δ) < 10 ^ p * δ ^ ((1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ p)`
- **What**: For `p ≥ 2`, `0 < δ ≤ 1` and any real `Φ ≤ 3·10^{p-1} δ^{(1/2)^{p-1}}`, the inequality `2(Φ + √Φ + √δ) < 10^p δ^{(1/2)^p}` holds.
- **How**: Writes `p = k + 1` and sets `a = δ^{(1/2)^{k+1}}`, `T = 10^k`. It proves:
  - `a² = δ^{(1/2)^k}` (`Real.rpow_mul_natCast`);
  - `√δ ≤ a` (`Real.sqrt_eq_rpow`, `Real.rpow_le_rpow_of_exponent_ge`);
  - `10 ≤ T` (`le_self_pow₀`);
  - `√Φ < T a` (`Real.sqrt_lt'`);
  - `a² ≤ a` (`Real.rpow_le_one`).
  It then closes with one `linear_combination`.
- **Hypotheses**:
  - `hp`: needed. It fails at `p = 1`.
  - `hδ0`: needed for strictness. It fails at `δ = 0`.
  - `hδ1`: needed for `a² ≤ a` and `√δ ≤ a`. It fails for large `δ`.
  - `hΦ`: the input bound. `Φ` may be negative, and equality is allowed.
- **Uses from project**: `[]`
- **Used by**: `Roth.theta_phi_lt_aux` (private, the Lemma 7 induction step) at Roth/Theta.lean:879. It has no other uses in `Roth/`.
- **Visibility**: public
- **Lines**: 704–740 (docstring 704–708, statement 709–711, proof 712–740, proof length 29 lines)
- **Notes**: The source is clean.tex §5, proof of Lemma 7, l.1023–1035 ("Set a = δ^{2^{-p}} …"). The Lean proof bounds term by term (`3T a² ≤ 3T a`, `√Φ < T a`, `√δ ≤ 10 T a`) instead of the paper's explicit coefficient `2(3/10 + √3/10^{3/2} + 1/100) < 1`. The two give the same conclusion. It is the companion of `Roth.lem7_Φ_bound` (assessed BORDERLINE-needs-human, recommendation keep local).
